Source: NBM
According to the end-2022 exchange rate, the national currency
depreciated against the US dollar and the European single currency
by 8.1% and 1.4%, respectively (Chart 3.46). On average, the
MDL depreciated against the US dollar by 6.9% and recorded the
opposite dynamics against the euro, appreciating by 4.9%.
Regarding the dynamics of the currencies of the countries with
signiﬁcant shares in Moldova’s foreign trade and, respectively,
included in the REER basket42, all of them, with the exception of
the currency of the Russian Federation (capital outﬂow control
measures were introduced), lost positions against the US dollar.
Depreciation higher than that of the MDL recorded by the Türkiye
lira, 90.5%, the currencies of EU member states and the pound
sterling (Chart 3.47).
In this respect,
in 2022,
the MDL
appreciated, on average, by 7.7% in nominal terms and 18.5% in
real terms against the currencies of the main trading partners, the
appreciation being determined by all currencies included in the
REER basket, except the Russian ruble (Charts 3.48 and 3.49).
According to year-end values, the MDL appreciated by 10.9% in
real terms.
At the beginning of 2022, the local currency market was exposed
to major imbalances caused by the timing of severe exogenous
shocks: the deepening energy crisis caused by high import prices
of natural gas and oil products and the war unleashed by Russia
against Ukraine on 24 February 2022. The war in Ukraine caused a
freeze in the local currency market that lasted until 18 March 2022.
Thereby, both economic agents and individuals, who traditionally
represent a source of net foreign exchange supply, have been
net demanders of foreign exchange. Foreign exchange outﬂows
from individuals’ accounts have also increased, in addition to their
tendency to accumulate foreign currency cash. During this period,
balances on individuals’ foreign and domestic currency accounts
declined by around 9.0% and 8.8%, respectively. The increased
demand for foreign currency by economic agents was conditioned
by their preference to migrate their funds from MDL accounts to
foreign currency accounts. Thus, as of 18 March 2022 compared
to 23 February 2022, the balance in the foreign currency accounts
of legal entities increased by 8.1%, while the balance in their MDL
accounts decreased by 8.5%. Under these circumstances, during
the period of the turmoil, the foreign exchange deﬁcit deepened by
6.5 times compared to that recorded at the beginning of February,
up to the invasion. Respectively, the need for intervention by
the NBM to calm the market by not allowing a strong volatility
of the MDL exchange rate has increased, as the resilience of
foreign exchange reserves allows this. In this regard, the NBM’s
foreign exchange sales interventions more than tripled compared
to those carried out from the beginning of the year until the conﬂict,
amounting to USD 346.0 million (Chart 3.50). In the ﬁrst quarter of
2022 as a whole, the NBM intervened through net sales of foreign
exchange in the amount of USD 439.2 million.
42According to the updated shares of the main trading partners in Moldova’s
foreign trade in 2020-2022.


64
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
Chart 3.51: Foreign exchange structure of net supply
of foreign currency from individuals (recalculated at a
constant exchange rate)
Source: NBM
Chart 3.52: Ofﬁcial reserve assets, by ﬂow type (USD
million)
Source: Bloomberg
After a depreciation of almost 8.0% since the beginning of the year,
the MDL exchange rate stabilized as the situation on the foreign
exchange market normalized towards the end of May. The NBM’s
tightening measures of monetary policy on the foreign exchange
market resulted in a surplus of foreign exchange, which allowed
the NBM to recover some of the currency sold during the turmoil.
The only exception was in November, when developments on the
energy market (conditioned by the full but temporary import of
electricity from European markets at high prices and the need
for the state to build up strategic reserves of natural gas) led to
a shortage of foreign exchange. The supply-demand coverage
ratio was 56.3%, with the gap made up by the NBM through
interventions to sell USD 175.2 million.
Under these circumstances, in May-December 2022, the net supply
of foreign currency from individuals increased by 32.9% compared
to May-December 2021, due to the preference of individuals for
savings in national currency, given the high interest rates on
deposits in MDL and the return of transfers from abroad in favour
of individuals to a positive level (+15.8%), after a decrease of 6.5%
in the ﬁrst four months of the year.
During the same period, exports of goods increased by 28.3% and
imports – by 26.6%, causing an increase in the trade balance
deﬁcit by 25.3%. Despite this deterioration, the net demand for
foreign exchange from economic agents increased by only 1.1%,
with the supply of foreign exchange from the representatives of
international organisations contributing to some extent to this
dynamic, as a result of external ﬁnancing disbursed for the
management of the ﬂow of refugees from Ukraine, mitigation of
the effects of the energy crisis and for the implementation of
various development projects in the country.
For 2022 as a whole, the net supply of foreign exchange from
individuals at USD 2,470.3 million increased by 3.7% compared to
the previous year, and the net demand for foreign exchange from
economic agents – by 2.1% to USD 3,010.2 million. Under these
conditions, the supply coverage of demand amounted to 82.1%
compared to 80.8% in 2021, creating premises for net foreign
exchange sales interventions in the amount of USD 249.8 million.
The net supply of foreign currency from individuals continued to
be denominated, predominantly, in euro (80.8%43), the share of
other currencies, mostly the pound sterling, being on an obvious
increase (Chart 3.51).
Ofﬁcial reserve assets
According to the situation at the end of the year, ofﬁcial reserve
assets amounted to USD 4,474.2 million, strengthening by 14.7%
compared to the end of 2021, due to ﬁnancial assistance from
Moldova’s main development partners, which reached a new
historical high. In this regard, the following external credit and
grant inﬂows were noted:
43Calculated by applying a constant rate.


Chapter 3. Monetary policy
65
• The IMF, following missions to assess the authorities’
compliance with the conditionalities set out in the economic
and ﬁnancial policy programme agreed with the Republic of
Moldova in December 2021, disbursed the second and third
loan tranche (ECF/EFF) for budget support in an amount
equivalent to USD 170.7 million;
• The World Bank, through its institutions IDA and IBRD has
allocated for budget support the equivalent of USD 207.1
million, of which USD 196.4 million under the Development
Policy Operations Programme on emergency response,
resilience and competitiveness of the Republic of Moldova
(including USD 53.0 million in the form of a grant), with the
remaining amount being disbursed to support reforms in
health and tax administration. Therewith, the World Bank
provided USD 22.7 million, mainly loans, to support several
projects, in particular, local road rehabilitation and education
reform;
• The European Commission granted, on behalf of the EU,
budget support amounting to EUR 124.6 million, of which
EUR 75.0 million as the second grant tranche to support the
vulnerable population affected by increased energy prices
and to alleviate economic pressure associated with the inﬂux
of refugees from Ukraine and EUR 49.6 million under the new
macro-ﬁnancial assistance programme (including EUR 15.0
million in the form of a grant). The European Commission has
also provided grants to support several investment projects
amounting approximately to EUR 6.0 million;
• The French Development Agency has disbursed two budget
support loans totalling EUR 75.0 million, of which EUR 60.0
million are targeted to the sustainable energy and railway
sectors and EUR 15.0 million to ﬁnance state budget needs,
including health services, education, social transfers, etc.;
• The German government has awarded a grant of EUR 40.0
million to promote energy security for vulnerable groups;
• The Government of the Republic of Poland has allocated
EUR 20.0 million as an interest-free loan to ﬁnance the state
budget, including for the management of the refugee crisis
from Ukraine;
• The Romanian Government has allocated a grant of EUR 10.5
million in two tranches;
• The European Investment Bank granted loans equivalent to
EUR 47.6 million, of which EUR 7.9 million in the form of
budget support for the development of railway infrastructure,
the remaining amount being allocated to the rehabilitation of
roads, streets and the modernization of public lighting and
support for the "Livada Moldovei" project.
Humanitarian aid for refugees from Ukraine, amounting to the
equivalent of USD 15.6 million from several donors, including
individuals.
At the end of 2022, the ofﬁcial reserve assets sufﬁciently covered
the actual import of goods and services, about 5.3 months of
imports.


66
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
Management of foreign exchange reserves
According to Articles 5, 16, 53 and 71 of Law No. 548/1995 on the
National Bank of Moldova, the NBM shall keep and manage the
foreign exchange reserves of the state, carry out foreign exchange
operations using the reserves and maintain them at an adequate
level for the implementation of the monetary and foreign exchange
policy of the state. The role of foreign exchange reserves is to
ensure the credibility and macro-ﬁnancial stability of the country,
to honour payments on the state’s external obligations and to cope
with unpredictable exogenous shocks.
In the process of managing foreign exchange reserves, the NBM
ensures a high degree of security and a necessary level of
investment liquidity. The NBM’s investment policy is prudent, with
proﬁtability optimisation as a secondary objective, safety and
liquidity of reserves being paramount, which indicates a low risk
appetite. An essential element of the foreign exchange reserves
management process is investment risk management, which is
achieved by imposing investment constraints and limits.
Table A.14 shows the main risks related to the management of
foreign exchange reserves and the techniques applied to mitigate
exposure.
Since July 2013, in collaboration with the World Bank, Strategic
Asset Allocation (SAA) is being implemented, which involves a
long-term asset management vision aimed at achieving the
optimal level of return and risk.
In the context of the SAA,
international reserves are divided into three tranches (current,
liquidity and investment) according to its speciﬁc objectives and
regulations, this approach ensures that the objective is achieved
more efﬁciently.
Over the whole of 2022, the global economy continues to
experience a series of destabilising shocks. Pandemic-induced
inﬂationary pressures ampliﬁed by the energy crisis have proven
to be persistent. The armed conﬂict in Ukraine, soaring food and
energy prices and renewed supply shocks have not only fuelled a
surge in inﬂation, but have also added momentum to inﬂationary
expectations in the short to medium term. As a result, headline
inﬂation has risen sharply, leading to a tightening of monetary
policy in advanced economies and, consequently, rising borrowing
costs.
The process of managing foreign exchange reserves is conditioned
by the development of the world economy, the decisions of the
world’s major central banks, in particular the monetary authorities
of the US, the euro area and the UK, considering the importance
of these countries’ currencies in the structure of ofﬁcial reserve
assets.
Thereby, the US Federal Reserve’s benchmark rate moved from
0-0.25% at the beginning of the year to 4.25-4.50% at the end
of December 2022, while the Bank of England’s rate increased
from 0.25% to 3.50% at the end of the year as monetary policy
was tightened to combat inﬂationary pressures. Therewith, during
the third and fourth quarters of 2022, according to the monetary


Chapter 3. Monetary policy
67
policy decisions of the European Central Bank, the base rate was
successively increased from 0.0% to 2.50% (Chart 3.53), shifting
the interest rates into positive territory. The decisions were also
reﬂected in the volatility of exchange rates, in terms of speed of
reaction, policy adaptation, transmission, expected effect, etc.
Developments in monetary policy rates set by the monetary
authorities were implicitly reﬂected in the dynamics of yields on
ﬁnancial instruments, with a signiﬁcant impact on the ﬁnancial
performance of the National Bank, increasing the return on foreign
exchange reserves.
Chart 3.53: Monetary policy rates in the US, EU, UK
Source: NBM
Ofﬁcial reserve assets as of 31 December 2022 compared to 31
December 2021 increased by 14.7% or approximately USD 572
million (from USD 3,902 million to USD 4,474 million).
Within
the management of foreign exchange reserves, the investment
policy of the National Bank is prudent, with assets allocated in
safe instruments, also used by other central banks: placements on
correspondent accounts (usually with other central banks), term
investments and securities, which are classiﬁed into supranational
securities (issued by supranational institutions), governmental
securities (issued by the US government, the governments of
EU member countries and other government issuers) and non-
governmental securities (issued by agencies).
Some of the foreign exchange reserves are managed externally
by the World Bank. In accordance with the Investment Advisory
and Management Agreement between the International Bank for
Reconstruction and Development (IBRD) and the National Bank
of Moldova of 8 December 2010 (extended by DEB No. 168 of
30 December 2015), the IBRD is the advisor and trustee of the
NBM for the management of part of the external assets, limited to
20% of foreign exchange reserves. As of 31 December 2022, the
share of externally managed assets constituted 5.1% of foreign
exchange reserves (Chart 3.54).
Chart 3.54: Structure of foreign exchange reserves by
investment instruments at the end of 2019-2022 (USD
million)
Source: NBM
In 2022, taking into account expectations of yield developments
amid persistent inﬂationary pressures, the portfolio of securities
valued
at
amortised
cost
(previously
the
portfolio
of
held-to-maturity
securities)
was
not
replenished
with
new
securities. The value of this portfolio, as of 31 December 2022,
constituted approximately USD 82.0 million or 1.8% of foreign
exchange reserves.
According to the statement as of 31 December 2022, 60.3% of
reserves are invested in securities, 26.4% are term investments
and 13.3% are held in cash and liquid assets in correspondent
accounts (Chart 3.54).
At the end of 2022, there was a decrease in correspondent
account holdings in favour of securities investments, due to the
establishment of the EUR portfolio and the replacement of the
internally managed USD portfolio. The increase in the share of
term placements was mainly due to the placement of deposits
with the Bank for International Settlements.
During 2022, the US dollar appreciated against most reserve
currencies on international currency markets amid diverging
economic cycles, the consistency and magnitude of monetary
policy decisions and, by implication, the observed and anticipated
spread in monetary policy interest rates. In addition, the armed


68
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
conﬂict between the Russian Federation and Ukraine increased
risks, which further contributed to the attractiveness of the US
dollar as a safe haven asset.
At the same time, as a result of Russia’s expanding aggression
against Ukraine, countries have been prompted to adopt a series
of sanctions against Russia that have had a signiﬁcant impact on
supply chains, commodity markets, energy markets and economic
growth prospects, particularly in the euro area, with projected
GDP growth being revised downwards, weakening the previously
robust euro as a result. The European single currency has been
strongly affected by geopolitical risks and since the second decade
of August, for the ﬁrst time in 20 years, the euro has fallen below
parity against the US dollar, reversing its trend only in the second
half of November. This development was caused by the worsening
macroeconomic situation in the euro area, given the threats to
Europe’s energy security, the contraction of major economies and,
in this respect, a slower monetary policy response to inﬂationary
pressures.
Chart 3.55: Structure of foreign exchange reserves as
of 31.12.2022
Source: NBM
Developments in the global economic environment and the
persistence of heightened uncertainty led to the maintenance of
the normative foreign exchange structure, with the permissible
deviation of ±10 percentage points favouring tactical decisions to
adjust the currency composition of reserves during the year and
to adapt it according to market expectations. Therewith, a tactical
decision was taken to diversify the portfolio through limited
exposure in Chinese yuan renminbi. According to year-end ﬁgures,
the US dollar continues to hold the largest share of foreign
exchange reserves – 66.16% (Chart 3.55).
Starting from the development of the world economy in 2022, in
particular the US, EU and UK, the ﬁnancial market environment
and the development of exchange rates against the US dollar (the
reserve
reporting
currency),
foreign
exchange
reserve
management generated a proﬁt of USD 44.75 million at an
average proﬁtability rate of 1.18%.

Chapter 4
The NBM supervisory activity
4.1
Banking supervision
Developments in the banking sector
Core indicators of the banking system
As of 31 December 2022, 11 banks licensed by the National Bank
of Moldova were operating in the Republic of Moldova, including 5
branches of foreign banks and ﬁnancial groups.
The total number of subdivisions of licensed banks in the Republic
of Moldova amounted to 579, of which 285 branches and 294
agencies.
As of 31 December 2022, 8,504 people were employed in the
banking sector.
On average, each employee managed assets
worth MDL 15.5 million, an increase of MDL 1.2 million (8.4%)
compared to the end of 2021.
The ﬁnancial situation of the banking sector as of 31 December
2022 was satisfactory.
Banks are adequately capitalized, own
funds amounted to MDL 18,394.7 million, increasing by MDL
3,215.9 million (21.2%) compared to the previous year, and the
total own funds ratio remains at a high level, recording a value of
29.5%, increasing by 3.6 percentage points compared to 2021 due
to the increase in own funds. According to the reports submitted by
banks, all banks complied with the "Total own funds ratio" indicator
(limit for each bank ≥10%) and additional requirements imposed
by supervisory measures.
The dynamics of the main ﬁnancial indicators are shown in the
chart 4.1.
Chart 4.1: Dynamics of main ﬁnancial indicators of the
banking sector of the Republic of Moldova for the period
31.12.2021* – 31.12.2022
Source: NBM
*Data as of 31.12.2021 throughout the text are adjusted
according to the results of the external audit.
Proﬁt for the year ended on 31 December 2022 amounted to
MDL 3,627.2 million per sector, increasing by MDL 1,304.4 million
(58.61%) compared to the previous year, due to the increase in
interest income by MDL 4,519.0 million (88.5%), income from
exchange rate differences – by MDL 547.6 million (46.7%) and
income from fees and commissions – by MDL 480.1 million (19.1%).
At the same time, grew up non-interest expenses – by MDL 2,934.1
million (53.8%) and interest expenses – by MDL 1,285.4 million
(103.3%)

Total assets amounted to MDL 131,444.5 million, increasing by
MDL 12,942.6 million (10.9%) during 2022.
As of 31 December 2022, the balance of the gross loan portfolio
(prudential) accounted for 46.9% of total assets or MDL 61,627.2
million, increasing by MDL 5,268.0 million (9.3%) during the period
under review. At the same time, non-performing loans increased
by MDL 510.0 million (14.7%) to MDL 3,970.4 million, and the share
of non-performing loans in total loans increased by 0.3 percentage
points to 6.4%.
The balance of deposits increased during 2022 by MDL 4,886.7
million (5.4%) to MDL 94,968.9 million.
Own funds
As of 31 December 2022, own funds by sector, according to data
provided by banks, amounted to MDL 18,394.7 million, recording
an increase of MDL 3,215.9 million (21.2%) during the year.
The increase in own funds was determined by the banks’ reﬂection
of eligible proﬁts after the general meetings of shareholders and
after obtaining the NBM’s permission for the inclusion of proﬁts
in own funds. At the same time, the positive difference between
allowances for losses on assets and conditional commitments
(prudential allowances) and allowances for impairment losses on
assets and provisions (IFRS allowances) was reduced by MDL 760.9
million, other reserves by MDL 465.1 million (prudential and IFRS
difference accounted for at the end of the year). Also, subordinated
debts were increased by MDL 174.8 million and capital instruments
eligible as core tier 1 own funds (share capital) following the
completion of the transaction to repurchase treasury shares of
a bank for MDL 168.2 million and other intangible assets by MDL
57.3 million.
In 2022, the NBM, taking into account the current regional security
situation, as well as the related establishment of a state of
emergency in the Republic of Moldova by the Parliament Decision
No. 41/2022, based on the provisions of Article 99 paragraph 6) of
Law No. 202/2017 on the activity of banks, in order to ensure an
adequate level of own funds and liquidity of licensed banks to
ensure their viability and stability in the situation of uncertainty
regarding
the
developments
in
the
region,
banks
were
recommended to refrain during 2022, until the negative effects
caused by the current situation are mitigated, from dividend
distributions and repurchases of own funds instruments within the
meaning of the Regulation of the National Bank of Moldova No.
109/2018 on banks’ own funds and capital requirements.
Respectively, taking into account the above recommendations,
during the reporting year, banks did not distribute dividends.
The total own funds structure of 9 banks consists entirely of
common equity tier 1 capital (total by sector – MDL 17,801.6
million), while 2 banks (BC "MOLDOVA – AGROINDBANK" S.A. and
B.C.
"ProCredit
Bank"
S.A.)
also
have
tier
2
own
funds

(subordinated loans in the amount of MDL 499.6 million and MDL

Therewith, the share of total own funds of medium-sized and
small banks was 19.6% and 3.2% respectively, decreasing by 1.2
percentage points and 0.5 percentage points.
During the period under review, the signiﬁcant share of foreign
investments in banks’ capital was maintained, which as of 31
December 2022 amounted to 88.9% (Chart 4.4), increasing by 2.3
percentage points compared to the end of the previous year as a
result of the repurchase by AS "Iute Credit Europe" on 14 February
2022 of treasury shares of BC "ENERGBANK" S.A. (49.4%), as well
as other shares from the bank’s shareholders. Respectively, a new
shareholder with the country of residence Estonia has appeared
with the share of 95.9% in the share capital of the bank.
Foreign investors participating in the capital formation of Moldovan
banks include banks from Italy, Hungary, Romania, as well as the
European Bank for Reconstruction and Development and investors
from Germany, the Netherlands, Bulgaria, Austria, Estonia, the
United Kingdom, Greece, Iraq.
Out of the total number of banks, 4 banks have capital consisting

S.A. and B.C. "ProCredit Bank" S.A.), 6 banks – with capital
composed of foreign and domestic investments and one bank with
capital composed entirely of domestic investments.

Following the analysis of the concentration of the banking sector
of the Republic of Moldova, the Herﬁndahl-Hirschmann index was
calculated (the value of which does not exceed 10,000), based
on the share of the bank’s assets in total assets in the banking
sector. The given indicator is calculated as the sum of squares
of the market shares of all banks in the sector. In international
practice the following division is used:
- HHI below 100 indicates a perfectly competitive market;
- HHI between 100 and 1,500 indicates a non-concentrated
market;
- HHI
between
1,500
and
2,500
indicates
a
moderate
concentration;
- HHI above 2,500 indicates a high concentration.

Chapter 4. The NBM supervisory activity
75
The share of allowances calculated for loan losses in total loans as
of 31 December 2022 was 6.3%, decreasing insigniﬁcantly by 0.1
percentage points compared to the end of 2021.
It should be noted that the calculated allowances (prudential
allowances) on all assets and contingent liabilities as of 31
December 2022 amounted to MDL 4,696.7 million, decreasing
compared to 31 December 2021 by MDL 91.8 million (1.9%), and
the allowances for impairment losses formed (IFRS allowances)
amounted to MDL 4,330.3 million, signiﬁcantly increasing by MDL
1,092.5 million (33.7%), the difference recorded – MDL 366.4
million.
Thus, the share of allowances for impairment losses formed on
loans (IFRS allowances) in the calculated allowances on loans
(prudential allowances) in the banking sector increased by 21.8
percentage points to 95.3%, ranging from 15.9% to 183.1%.
Banks’ exposures to related parties account for insigniﬁcant shares
in total loans – 2.0% (banking sector average). The indicator "Ratio
of exposures to related parties to eligible capital" (limit for each
bank ≤20% of eligible capital) as of 31 December 2022 was met
by all banks (banking sector average – 6.6%).
As of 31 December 2022, the indicator "The ratio of the sum of the
ten largest net debts to the total portfolio of loans and contingent
liabilities" (maximum limit for each bank – 30% of total net loans)
was met by all banks (average for the banking sector – 19.7%).
At the same time, only one bank exceeded the above-mentioned
indicator, which, according to Chapter IV item 26 of the Regulation
on Large Exposures No. 109 of 5 April 2019, is not considered a
violation if the bank maintains an additional own funds requirement
on the respective surplus.
The indicator on the total amount of the bank’s currency-linked
net exposures in MDL to individuals, including those engaged in
entrepreneurial or other type of activity (limit for each bank ≤30%
of eligible capital) was met by all banks. The sector average of the
nominated indicator was 4.1%.
The ratio of total amount of net currency-linked non-mortgage
exposures to individuals to total regulatory capital (limit for each
bank ≤10% of eligible capital) was also met by all banks. The
sector average of the nominated indicator was 0.4%.
The ratio of total investment in real estate to total own funds by
sector was 18.4% (limit for each bank ≤50%) and was respected
by all banks.
Incomes and proﬁtability development
As of 31 December 2022, the proﬁt for the year for the banking
sector amounted to MDL 3,627.2 million. Compared to the end
of the previous year, the proﬁt increased by MDL 1,340.4 million
(58.6%), mainly due to the increase in interest income by MDL
4,519.0 million (88.5%), income from exchange rate differences –
by MDL 547.6 million (46.7%), income from fees and commissions
by MDL 480.1 million (19.1%).


76
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
Interest income increased by MDL 4,519.0 million (88.5%) to
MDL 9,626.0 million.
The highest income was earned on loan
investments, which amounted to MDL 5,570.7 million (57.9% of
total interest income), increasing by MDL 1,522.3 million (37.6%).
The income from funds placed with the NBM (required reserves)
also increased by MDL 2,025.4 million (16.6 times) to MDL 2,155.1
million (22.4% of total interest income), following the increase
in the required reserve ratio held by banks from 26.0% on 31
December 2021 to 37.0% on 31 December 2022. Income from
debt securities (SS, NBC) increased by MDL 971.9 million (104.7%)
to MDL 1,900.1 million (19.7% of total interest income).

At the same time, non-interest expenses increased by MDL 2,934.1
million (53.8%) to MDL 8,385.3 million, mainly due to the increase
in losses on impairment of ﬁnancial assets by MDL 1,323.0 million
(909.1%), administrative expenses by MDL 587.1 million (20.7%)
to MDL 3,418.1 million and fees and commissions by MDL 438.8
million (40.6%) to MDL 1,519.5 million.
Total revenues amounted to MDL 14,541.9 million, increasing by
MDL 5,559.4 million (61.9%) compared to the end of the previous
year, of which interest income accounted for 66.2% (MDL 9,626.0
million) and non-interest income – 33.8% (MDL 4,915.9 million).
At the same time, total expenditure amounted to MDL 10,914.7
million, increasing compared to the previous year by MDL 4,219.5
million (63.0%), of which interest expenditure accounted for
23.2% (MDL 2,529.4 million) of total expenditure and non-interest
expenditure – 76.8% (MDL 8,385.3 million) of total expenditure.
Signiﬁcant shares of non-interest expenses are accounted for by
administrative expenses – 40.8% (MDL 3,418.1 million) and fees
and commissions – 18.1% (MDL 1,519.5 million).
The structure of the banking sector’s income and expenditure for
2022 is shown in charts 4.14 and 4.15.
For 2022, the return on assets48 and return on equity49 of banks
recorded values of 2.9% and corresponding 16.9%, up by 0.9
percentage points and 4.6 percentage points,
respectively,
compared to the end of the previous year.
The net interest margin50 amounted to 6.7% as of 31 December
2022, an increase of 2.6 percentage points compared to the end
of the previous year.

Market risk sensitivity
As of 31 December 2022, the combined share of foreign currency
balance sheet assets and foreign currency pegged assets in total
assets stood at 34.4%. This was slightly higher, by 0.5 percentage
points, than the share represented by foreign currency balance
sheet bonds and foreign currency pegged bonds in total assets,
which was 33.9%. The insigniﬁcant difference between these two


Chapter 4. The NBM supervisory activity
79
percentages suggests that banks have effectively managed foreign
exchange risk, keeping it at a minimum.
As of 31 December 2022, according to the data provided by
banks the foreign placements of banks in the Republic of Moldova
amounted to MDL 4,254.1 million, decreasing by MDL 8,168.1
million (65.8%) compared to the end of the previous year and
representing 3.2% of total assets (as of 31 December 2021 –
10.5%) and 23.1% of own funds (as of 31 December 2021 – 81.8%).
The greatest decrease was recorded for placements in Germany –
by MDL 5,715.0 million (89.4%), in Austria – by MDL 1,618.4 million
(73.1%), in the USA – by MDL 481.6 million (29.6%), in Italy – by
MDL 259.9 million (38.0%). At the same time, placements in the
United Kingdom increased the most by MDL 55.0 million (34.8%).
Details are shown in table A.16.
As of 31 December 2022, the largest share of total placements
by sector went to the USA – 26.9%, followed by France – 19.7%,
Germany – 16.0%, Austria – 14.0%. Placements are predominantly
made in highly rated countries and banks, indicating cautious
activity. According to the Regulation on the classiﬁcation of assets
and contingent liabilities, banks reduce their own funds by the size
of the reductions formed, taking into account the rating assigned
by international agencies on countries and banks.
Prudential supervision of banks
In order to protect the interests of depositors and to ensure the
stability and viability of the banking system, the National Bank of
Moldova conducts prudential supervision of banks in the Republic
of Moldova, ensuring compliance with legal requirements for the
prevention and limitation of risks speciﬁc to banking activity.
The multiple shocks to which the Republic of Moldova has been
exposed during 2022 have led to increased uncertainties about
the evolution of the economy, as well as ampliﬁed risks in the
banking system.
Effectively managing the immediate effects of the War in Ukraine
and those caused by inﬂation and the COVID-19 pandemic was one
of the National Bank’s core concerns during 2022. Thus, despite
negative external factors, banking sector indicators were within
the regulated range, reﬂecting the resilience of the sector as a
result of reforms in recent years.
The main areas of focus for banking supervision in 2022, as
prioritised, were:
credit risk, information and communication
technology risk (ICT), internal governance, risk management and
internal risk capital adequacy assessment process (ICAAP),
internal
liquidity
adequacy
assessment
process
(ILAAP),
anti-money laundering and combating the ﬁnancing of terrorism,
payment systems.
In 2022, the NBM continued the supervision of banks under the
SREP supervision and evaluation process. Thus, the results of the
supervision and evaluation process (SREP) for 2020 were prepared
and sent to all 11 banks. The results of the Supervisory Review
and Evaluation Process (SREP) for 2021 were also prepared and
sent to 6 banks.


80
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
Ex ofﬁcio controls
The National Bank carries out ex ofﬁcio supervision based on the
annual prudential supervision programme.
For 2022,
the
prudential supervision programme included various ongoing
actions (monitoring of key indicators, analysis of ﬁnancial reports
submitted by banks,
external audit reports,
monitoring of
remediation plans following on-site inspections) as well as actions
for
the
preparation
of
the
SREP
report
(business
model
assessment, governance and internal control analysis, capital and
liquidity risk assessment, analysis of ICAAP reports etc.).
The National Bank will continue to act in accordance with the
legislation in force and international best practices, applying all
instruments assigned by law and statute, in order to increase the
level of transparency of the ownership structure and ensure a
prudent and sound management of licensed banks.
On-site controls
During 2022, 4 thematic inspections were carried out at licensed
banks, including 3 planned inspections and 1 unannounced
inspection.
Decisions of the Executive Board of the NBM on thematic controls
on the activity of 6 licensed banks were also approved.
As a result, the following actions were implemented:
• warnings were issued to 3 banks, 8 members of the Bank’s
Board, 3 members of the Bank’s Executive Board and 1 Head
of Internal Audit;
• ﬁnes were imposed on 2 banks, 1 member of the Bank’s
Board, 5 members of the Bank’s Executive Board, 2 heads of
internal control functions.
In general, the breaches and weaknesses identiﬁed in the
thematic controls relate to the framework for the management of
the Bank’s business, including issues of internal governance,
including the delegation of certain tasks of a member of the
executive board to another person, ensuring the independence
and organisation of the work of the internal control functions,
including the insufﬁcient and late response of the internal control
functions to certain risk events, ensuring the internal control
mechanism in the area of bank ownership, some weaknesses in
credit risk assessment, breach of minimum requirements for
banks’ Information and Communication Systems, including with
regard to ICT risk management, access control, vulnerability and
incident management, ensuring business continuity, failure to
comply with the measures foreseen for the previous control.


Chapter 4. The NBM supervisory activity
81
Early intervention and shareholder transparency actions
As of 31 December 2022, B.C. "ENERGBANK" S.A. was supervised
under the early intervention regime applied on 11 January 2019,
as a result of the ﬁnding of concerted activity of a group of
shareholders with a qualiﬁed holding in the amount of 52.55%
in the bank’s capital, without prior written permission of the NBM,
the effects of the law on the suspension of the exercise of certain
rights of shareholders became applicable.
Subsequently, in January 2023, the Executive Board of the
National Bank of Moldova unanimously voted to lift the early
intervention regime at B.C. "ENERGBANK" S.A. This decision was
taken with the restoration of the functionality of the Board and
Management Committee of the Bank, ensuring the quorum
necessary for conducting meetings and taking decisions in
accordance
with
the
regulations
and
internal
acts
of
B.C.
"ENERGBANK" S.A.
During 2022, AS "IuteCredit Europe" has acquired qualiﬁed
shareholding up to and exceeding 50.0% of the share capital of
B.C. "ENERGBANK" S.A., becoming the majority shareholder of the
bank.
Banks in liquidation
As of 31 December 2022, 4 banks were in the process of forced
liquidation.
In the liquidation process of Banca de Economii S.A., B.C. "BANCA
SOCIAL˘A" S.A. and B.C. "UNIBANK" S.A., initiated in 2015, it was
mentioned that, with the aim of recovering the funds of these
banks, the liquidators reported in 2022 that they had worked with
the prosecution authorities, undertaken organizational measures
regarding the sale of assets owned and possessed by the banks,
and continued working with the banks’ debtors.
In its activity, the bank’s liquidator has full and exclusive powers
to direct, manage and control the bank and to take any measures
in order to liquidate it efﬁciently and obtain the maximum amount
from the sale of assets.
As a result, at the end of 2022, 43 actions were pending in the
courts to recover funds previously granted by banks, for a total
amount of MDL 3,884.2 million. At the same time, there were
73 criminal cases under the management of criminal prosecution
bodies, ﬁled on the basis of liquidators’ referrals.
As a result of the actions taken by the liquidators during the
liquidation process of the three banks, funds in the total amount of
MDL 2,821.6 million were collected, including in 2022 – MDL 75.9
million (Banca de Economii S.A. – MDL 22.2 million, B.C. "BANCA
SOCIAL˘A" S.A. – MDL 48.6 million and B.C. "UNIBANK" S.A. – MDL
5.1 million).
The funds obtained in the process of liquidating the banks were
used to cover the expenses related to the liquidation process, as
well as to pay the claims related to the emergency loans granted


82
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
to Banca de Economii S.A., B.C. "BANCA SOCIAL˘A" S.A. and B.C.
"UNIBANK" S.A., until the appointment of the liquidators of the
respective banks. Thus, until 31 December 2022, funds in the
amount of MDL 2,729.5 million were repaid, including in 2022 –
MDL 63.5 million (Banca de Economii S.A. – MDL 12.0 million, B.C.
"BANCA SOCIAL˘A" S.A. – MDL 45.0 million and B.C. "UNIBANK"
S.A. – MDL 6.5 million).
Resolution funding mechanisms
In 2020 the Bank Resolution Fund became operational. It was
set up to cover the needs related to the effective application
of resolution tools in accordance with the provisions of Law No.
232/2016 on Bank Recovery and Resolution.
The Bank Resolution Fund consists of:
- annual contributions to reach the target level set by law;
- extraordinary contributions, in case the annual contributions
are insufﬁcient in the opinion of the resolution authority;
- loans and other forms of support.
The resources of the Bank Resolution Fund are managed by the
Deposit Guarantee Fund in the Banking System, which, as
administrator of the bank resolution fund, is obliged to invest the
available ﬁnancial resources of the Fund in low-risk assets. The
use of the resources of the Bank Resolution Fund is decided by the
NBM, as resolution authority, for the purposes described in Law
No. 232/2016, and the calculation and payment of contributions
by banks is carried out in accordance with the Regulation on the
calculation and payment of contributions to the Bank Resolution
Fund, approved on 30 April 2020.
According to the law, the ﬁnancial resources of the Bank Resolution
Fund shall reach 3.0% of the number of guaranteed deposits of all
licensed banks on the territory of the Republic of Moldova. When
the last annual contributions were calculated for 2023, this amount
was approximately MDL 507.8 million. As of 31 December 2022,
the banks have fully paid the annual contributions calculated by
the NBM for 2020, 2021 and 2022. Thus, at the end of 2022,
the resources of the resolution fund amounted to MDL 350.7
million. During 2023, banks are expected to pay contributions
to the resolution fund in the amount of MDL 117.8 million.
The
establishment
of
the
resolution
fund
contributes
to
strengthening the banking sector and its credibility in support of
depositors’ interests.


Chapter 4. The NBM supervisory activity
83
Banking sector risk assessment
Macroprudential supervision
During
2022,
the
National
Bank
of
Moldova
promoted
macroprudential supervision driven by developments in the
domestic ﬁnancial sector, in the macroeconomic context of the
Republic of Moldova and the region, as well as unfavourable
forecasts and uncertainties generated by the regional geopolitical
conﬂict.
In order to avoid excessive build-up of systemic risk and to ensure
the stability of the banking sector in adverse regional economic
conditions, during 2022, the National Bank of Moldova continued to
conduct regular stress tests and sensitivity studies of the banking
sector. These macro-prudential analysis tools helped to assess
the resilience of Moldovan banks to potential shocks generated
by uncertain macroeconomic developments during the reporting
period.
The stress tests used by the National Bank focus on credit risk,
liquidity risk, interest rate risk, foreign exchange rate risk and
contagion risk scenarios and combinations thereof. The stress
tests are based on information reported by banks and estimate
the impact of simulated shocks at the banking sector level and at
the level of individual banks.
The results of the credit risk stress tests carried out in the banking
sector do not point to the build-up of signiﬁcant systemic
vulnerabilities. At the same time, the simulation of shocks at the
level of individual banks indicates a sufﬁcient level of banks’ own
funds, and most of the simulated shocks did not lead to breaches
of the minimum prudential capital requirements for banks.
In 2022, the results of the stress tests conﬁrmed the high level
of liquidity of the banking sector, with banks able to withstand
scenarios simulating deposit withdrawals at historical highs and
no liquidity shortfalls or breaches of prudential requirements for
the stress scenarios applied.
Also, in order to assess potential risks that could affect the
stability of the sector and of each bank, the National Bank
conducts the study on the identiﬁcation of vulnerabilities in the
banking sector. During 2022, the results of the study showed a
favourable development of the banking sector vulnerability. At the
individual
level,
most
banks
continued
the
process
of
strengthening and improving their resilience.
Although some
banks
have
experienced
insigniﬁcant
accumulations
of
vulnerabilities for some balance sheet items, overall vulnerability
indices remain below the alert threshold.
To survey the opinion of risk managers in banks, the National Bank
of Moldova conducts the systemic risk survey every six months.
According to the respondents, both traditional (macroeconomic,
sovereign) and speciﬁc (geopolitical) key risks persist in the
ﬁnancial system. At the same time, at the end of 2022, banks
have estimated the probability of a high impact event on the
ﬁnancial system in the next 12 months to be medium to high. The


84
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
medium-term (1-3 years) expectation of an adverse event is
estimated at medium level.
To determine the ﬁnancing conditions and the risk proﬁle of the
sectors ﬁnanced by banks in the Republic of Moldova, the NBM
conducts the quarterly bank lending survey. At the end of 2022, the
results of the survey indicated a tightening of lending standards for
both legal entities and individuals. Banks’ short-term expectations
were for a tightening of lending standards for corporates and a
slight tightening of lending standards for individuals.
Macroprudential policy tools
To
increase
the
resilience
of
banks
to
possible
adverse
developments, within the strategic objective of strengthening the
ﬁnancial stability function and macro-prudential supervision, the
National Bank of Moldova, as supervisory authority, applies the
macro-prudential supervisory toolkit for banks in the Republic of
Moldova.
Thus, the Executive Board of the National Bank of Moldova set the
capital buffer rates applied to banks of the Republic of Moldova as
of the end of 2022, as follows:
• The capital conservation buffer rate applied to all banks was
maintained at 2.5% of banks’ total risk exposure.
• The countercyclical buffer rate for exposures located in the
Republic of Moldova was maintained at the level of 0% of the
amount of banks’ risk exposure.
• The systemic risk buffer rate for exposures located in the
Republic of Moldova was maintained at the level of 1.0% or
3.0% of the banks’ risk exposure amount, depending on the
ownership structure of the respective bank.
• The systemic risk buffer rate for banks’ credit exposures to
resident individuals, excluding entrepreneurial individuals,
was set at 2.0% and was maintained by all banks in addition
to the systemic risk buffer rate.
• In order to ensure an adequate treatment of structural risks
of the banking sector in the Republic of Moldova, the National
Bank determines which banks are systemically important
companies (O-SIIs). Based on the results of the study on
the identiﬁcation of banks that are O-SIIs and in accordance
with paragraph 53 of Regulation No. 110/2018 on capital
buffers of banks, the National Bank has established the capital
buffer rates for systemically important companies for banks
identiﬁed as O-SIIs in the Republic of Moldova as follows:
1. 1.5% for BC "MOLDOVA – AGROINDBANK" S.A.
2. 1.0% for BC "Moldindconbank" S.A.
3. 1.0% for B.C. "VICTORIABANK" S.A.
4. 0.5% for OTP Bank S.A.


Chapter 4. The NBM supervisory activity
85
Therefore, at the end of 2022, the total capital buffer requirement
applied to banks in the Republic of Moldova varied between 3.6%
and 7.4%, depending on the systemic importance of the bank.
In addition to capital buffers, Regulation No.
101/2022 on
Responsible
Lending
to
Consumers
by
Banks
introduced
requirements against certain individual indicators at the consumer
level to ensure responsible lending where banks assess the
creditworthiness of the consumer and do not allow the consumer
to take on ﬁnancial obligations that exceed its ability to pay,
thereby accumulating excessive debt.
Thus, the loan-to-collateral ratio applies to investment property
loans and may not exceed 80% of the value of the pledged
property, and the debt service-to-income ratio applies to consumer
loans and may not exceed 40% of the consumer’s conﬁrmed
income. At the same time, the maximum maturity of consumer
credit has been limited – 5 years for consumer credit and 30
years for real estate investment credit. It should be noted that
Regulation No. 101/2022 provides for certain exceptions or stricter
requirements for some speciﬁc cases, and also describes how to
calculate these indicators.


86
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
4.2
Supervision of non-bank payment
service providers and inspection of
foreign exchange entities
Supervision of non-bank payment service providers
According to Article 93 paragraph 2) letter b) of the Law No.
114/2012 on payment services and electronic money, the National
Bank supervises the activity of payment institutions, electronic
money institutions, postal service providers as payment service
providers, banks as payment service providers and electronic
money issuers.
In 2022, thematic inspections were carried out at 4 non-bank
payment service providers, during which their compliance with the
requirements of payment systems legislation and the provision of
payment services was veriﬁed, as a result of which the following
violations were found:
→conducting business through payment agents not registered
with the NBM;
→failure to inform/ delayed informing the NBM of changes in the
information on the administrators of some payment agents;
→double charging of fees from both payment service users and
the utility provider;
→framework contracts concluded with payment service users
contain provisions contrary to the legislation in force;
→lack of internal procedures for identifying fraudulent use of
cards issued and/ or accepted and for ﬁling and settling
complaints;
→lack of risk assessment for activity in money remittance
systems;
→lack of monthly reporting to the NBM of local money
remittances made to the order of individuals;
→lack of notiﬁcation to the NBM of changes in the data in the
documents attached to the licence statement, etc.
As
a
result
of
the
checks
carried
out,
proposals
and
recommendations were made on the actions to be taken to
remedy the infringements and shortcomings detected and, where
appropriate, remedial measures and sanctions were imposed.
Thus, in 2022 sanctions in the form of a warning were applied to 2
providers and a ﬁne to 1 non-bank payment service provider.

Chapter 4. The NBM supervisory activity
87
Control of foreign exchange entities
According to the provisions of Article 62 of the Law No. 62/2008 on
foreign exchange regulation, the NBM shall control the compliance
of foreign exchange entities with the provisions of the foreign
exchange legislation.
During 2022, 62 inspections were carried out at foreign exchange
entities (of which 49 – planned and 13 – unannounced), as a result
of which violations were found such as:
• failure to maintain/ maintain current funds, consisting of cash
contributions to share capital at the level established by law;
• late
reporting
to
the
NBM
of
the
monthly
report
on
transactions performed;
• reporting errors/ non-reporting/ changing information on daily
buying and selling rates of major foreign currencies;
• lack of internal procedures governing the way in which the
buying and selling rates of foreign currencies are changed;
• failure to display the necessary information in a visible place
for individuals;
• use of outdated/ non-compliant forms;
• violations related to failure to notify the NBM of changes in
the data submitted for licensing.
As a result of the infringements and deﬁciencies found during the
on-the-spot checks carried out at the foreign exchange entities,
the following Decisions of the Executive Board on the application
of sanctions were adopted, namely:
• 2 decisions on the withdrawal of the licence of the foreign
exchange entity;
• 7 decisions on the suspension of the activity of the foreign
exchange entity;
• 21 decisions on the application of the ﬁne to the foreign
exchange entity;
• 24 written warnings.


88
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
4.3
Combating money laundering and
terrorist ﬁnancing
One of the strategic objectives of the National Bank of Moldova is
to ensure a safe, stable and resilient ﬁnancial system. Achieving
this objective requires risk-based regulatory and supervisory
activities, including in the area of preventing and combating
money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing.
By virtue of the
provisions of the Law No. 308/2017 on prevention and combating
of money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing, the National Bank is
the body with supervisory functions for the following categories of
reporting entities: i) banks speciﬁed in the Law No. 202/2017 on
banks’ activity; ii) foreign exchange entities (other than banks); iii)
payment institutions, electronic money issuers and postal service
providers operating in accordance with the Law No. 114/2012 on
payment services and electronic money.
During 2022, the National Bank of Moldova undertook several
proactive regulatory and supervisory actions on reporting entities,
aimed both at improving the compliance of supervised entities in
the area of preventing and combating money laundering and
terrorist ﬁnancing and increasing the effectiveness of their
internal control mechanisms, and at detecting and deterring the
use of services and delivery channels of relevant entities for illicit
purposes.
One of the important aspects of the activity in the ﬁeld of
preventing
and
combating
money
laundering
and
terrorist
ﬁnancing is the legislative process, in which the National Bank
participates by issuing opinions on primary regulatory acts related
to the ﬁeld, initiating or endorsing amendments to improve the
relevant regulatory framework.
Thus,
the most important
legislative drafts relevant to this area that were the subject of the
expert analysis and for which the NBM issued opinions were: the
draft Law on amending and supplementing Law No. 308/2017 on
prevention
and
combating
money
laundering
and
terrorist
ﬁnancing, with subsequent amendments and additions; the draft
Law on amending and supplementing the Law No. 75/2020 on the
procedure for establishing violations in the ﬁeld of prevention of
money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing and the application of
sanctions; the draft Law on participatory ﬁnancing services; the
draft Law on the implementation of international restrictive
measures
against
persons
involved
in
acts
that
violated
fundamental human rights, democracy and the rule of law; the
draft Law on the transparency of beneﬁciaries of assets on the
territory of the Republic of Moldova. Therewith, the National Bank
examined and issued the relevant opinion on the draft decision on
amending and supplementing the Decision of the Parliament No.
239/2020 on approving the National Strategy for preventing and
combating
money
laundering
and
terrorist
ﬁnancing
for
2020-2025 and the Action Plan for implementing the National
Strategy for preventing and combating money laundering and
terrorist ﬁnancing for 2020-2025.


Chapter 4. The NBM supervisory activity
89
In the context of the NBM’s regulatory powers in the area of
preventing
and
combating
money
laundering
and
terrorist
ﬁnancing and in line with the recommendations of the Council of
Europe’s MONEYVAL Committee, several draft regulatory acts
have been prepared, aimed at strengthening the secondary
regulatory framework on preventing and combating money
laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing in the supervised sectors,
namely:
the draft Regulation on the way to individualize and
establish
sanctions
in
the
ﬁeld
of
prevention
of
money
laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing; the draft recommendations on
the risk-based approach to customers by banks and non-bank
payment service providers in order to prevent and combat
money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing; the draft Instruction on
the procedure for the electronic identiﬁcation of customers, the
draft
Recommendations
on
the
monitoring
by
banks
and
non-bank payment service providers of customers’ transactions
and activities to prevent and combat money laundering and
terrorist
ﬁnancing;
the
draft
Recommendations
on
the
establishment of cross-border relationships in the context of
legislation on preventing and combating money laundering and
terrorist ﬁnancing. The drafted projects are to be ﬁnalised and
submitted
to
approval
procedures
in
accordance
with
the
legislation in force.
Representative for the work in the ﬁrst part of 2022 was the
approval of the National Money Laundering and Terrorist Financing
Risk Assessment Report.
This complex process, in which the
National Bank of Moldova actively participated, together with
other authorities and institutions within the national system for
preventing
and
combating
money
laundering
and
terrorist
ﬁnancing, was carried out in accordance with international
standards in this ﬁeld, taking into account the recommendations
of the MONEYVAL Committee of the Council of Europe and with
the aim of achieving the objectives set out in the National
Strategy for Preventing and Combating Money Laundering and
Terrorist Financing for 2020-202551. In terms of the National Bank
of Moldova’s tasks in the area of reference, the National Report on
the assessment of money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing risks
is one of the key elements for the appropriate management of
these risks in each of the sectors supervised, including the
conduct of veriﬁcation and control activities on reporting entities.
Considering the results of the National Money Laundering and
Terrorist Financing Risk Assessment, according to which the
banking sector is exposed to a medium to high level of money
laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing risk, the non-bank payment
service providers sector – to a medium level of risk, and the
foreign exchange sector – to a medium to high level of risk, a
number of inspections and veriﬁcations were carried out, covering
both issues related to the compliance of reporting entities
supervised on the area of reference, including the assessment of
programmes and internal control systems dedicated to preventing
and combating money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing, as well
as the veriﬁcation of higher risk operations, such as external
transfers, operations carried out through card payment systems
and digital applications, etc. (more than 29 remote veriﬁcations,
as a result of which the competent authorities were informed (13
information releases). In addition, 39 inspections were carried out
during 2022, including 1 off-site complex inspection at a licensed
51Approved by Parliament Resolution No. 239 of 16.12.2020.


90
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
bank (2 other complex inspections at 2 banks have been initiated
and are ongoing), 3 inspections at non-bank payment service
providers and 35 inspections at foreign exchange entities (other
than banks). As a result of the controls carried out, deﬁciencies in
the own programmes to prevent and combat money laundering
and terrorist ﬁnancing and situations of non-compliance with the
provisions of the legislation in the ﬁeld of prevention of money
laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing were identiﬁed. Consequently,
on the basis of the ﬁndings recorded in the control documents,
sanctions and/or measures to remedy the deﬁciencies were
ordered in accordance with the provisions of Law No. 75/2020 on
the procedure for establishing violations in the ﬁeld of prevention
of money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing and how to apply
sanctions, namely 2 ﬁnes and 9 prescriptions. In order to remedy
the deﬁciencies and non-conformities found and to improve the
internal control systems, each reporting entity covered by the
inspections carried out has drawn up action plans.
Supervisory activities in the area of prevention of money
laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing of the relevant sectors also
included continuous information ﬂows between the National Bank
and the supervised entities. The amount, complexity and intensity
of these information ﬂows were determined both by the type of
supervised entity, business model, internal governance, level of
money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing risk and the global and
regional circumstances of the period in question.
In 2022, with the participation of the NBM, a series of projects
related to the prevention and ﬁght against money laundering were
started and continued.
Thus, in the context of the provisions
of Law No. 178/2020 on the amendment of certain regulatory
acts, but also considering the provisions of Article 4 of Law No.
308/2017 on prevention and combating money laundering and
terrorist ﬁnancing, the necessary measures for taking over the
supervision of reporting entities in the insurance sector and non-
banking credit institutions were established. To this end, the money
laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing risks associated with the non-
banking sector were analysed, a study of international practices in
the ﬁeld was carried out, a summary analysis and documentation
of the processes within the transfer perimeter was carried out,
and the instruments and mechanisms for the transfer of powers
related to the ﬁeld of preventing and combating money laundering
and terrorist ﬁnancing were established.
Also, with USAID support, the National Bank of Moldova continued
the process of implementing the IT solution on monitoring
shareholder transparency in banks and remote analysis in the
ﬁeld of preventing and combating money laundering and terrorist
ﬁnancing.
In parallel with the surveillance actions, an intensive exchange of
relevant information in this area has been maintained with national
authorities as well as with relevant international bodies.
In this respect, information on the issues identiﬁed by the proﬁle
analyses in relation to the activities carried out by the supervised
reporting entities and/or related to high-risk operations, such as
external transfers, operations carried out through card payment
systems and digital applications, etc., have been submitted to the
Money Laundering Prevention and Control Service.


Chapter 4. The NBM supervisory activity
91
Externally, the participation of the National Bank of Moldova in the
meetings of the MONEYVAL Committee of the Council of Europe
was ensured, in order to assess the progress made by the Republic
of Moldova in preventing and combating money laundering and
terrorist ﬁnancing.
Having regard to the objectives and actions set out in the Action
Plan for the implementation of the National Strategy to Prevent
and Combat Money Laundering and Terrorist Financing for
2020-2025,
approved by Parliament Decision No.
239 of
16.12.2020, with reference to the conduct of training/ information
actions for reporting entities, considering the risk proﬁle of each
of the sectors supervised, during 2022, the National Bank of
Moldova has sent information letters to reporting entities on the
risks identiﬁed as a result of the national assessment of money
laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing risks and participated in the
organisation of several trainings, workshops and conferences
dedicated to the identiﬁcation and assessment of risks associated
with the ﬁnancial sector, typologies of operations that may be
circumscribed to suspected money laundering/ terrorist ﬁnancing
operations, monitoring of client activities/ transactions, prevention
of proliferation of weapons of mass destruction, etc.


92
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
Chapter 5
Financial market infrastructures
5.1
Regulation,
monitoring
and
supervision
of
ﬁnancial
market
infrastructures
In accordance with Law No. 548/1995 on the National Bank of
Moldova, regulation, monitoring and supervision of ﬁnancial
market infrastructures in the Republic of Moldova is one of the
core tasks of the National Bank of Moldova.
This task is implemented according to the best international
practices, recommendations, principles, and standards in this
domain issued by the Bank for International Settlements, the
European Central Bank, the International Monetary Fund, and the
World Bank.
In order to modernize the automated interbank payment system
(AIPS) of the NBM and implement the instant payment scheme,
provided for in the Concept for the development of the payment
sector in the Republic of Moldova, two implementation projects
continued during 2022.
These projects aim at developing the payment system in the
Republic of Moldova and modernizing payment services, to provide
the ﬁnancial community in our country a payment system with
high-performance infrastructure in line with the expectations and
demands of payment service providers, but also modern payment
tools meeting the needs of individual and corporate consumers.
The ﬁrst implementation project is dedicated to the modernization
of AIPS and includes the logical separation of the AIPS modules
RTGS (real-time gross settlement system) and DNS (designated-
time net settlement system), the transition to a more advanced
payment message format (ISO 20022), the creation of a functional
model for the management of liquidity limits by AIPS participants.
The upgrade works were successfully completed during 2022
without any deviations from the deadlines set in the project plan,
with the new system being launched in the production environment
on 23.05.2022.
The second project is dedicated to the implementation of a new
payment
scheme
(instant
payments).
During
2022
the
Procurement Contract with the supplier responsible for the
implementation of the solution was signed and the Project
Management Plan was approved.
According to this plan the
launch of the system is expected to be in February 2024.


Chapter 5. Financial market infrastructures
93
The targeted measures will strengthen the payment system in the
Republic of Moldova, improve operational and liquidity risk
management and facilitate the implementation of new payment
instruments.
It will also create conditions for the efﬁcient
development of innovative payments, including mobile payments,
with immediate ﬁnal settlement and the possibility of immediate
re-use of incoming funds. As a result, the ﬂow of economic ﬂows
will become easier, the innovative payments sector will be better
able to attract investment and the speed of money circulation in
the economy will increase.
They will beneﬁt at many levels –
payment service providers and end consumers (individuals), also
economic operators and authorities.
According to Article 5 of Law No. 548/1995 on the National Bank
of Moldova, the NBM monitors and supervises ﬁnancial market
infrastructures, under the conditions established by law, and
promotes their stable and efﬁcient functioning. In the context
of the basic objective, the National Bank monitors the payment
and settlement systems in the Republic of Moldova, whose stable
and efﬁcient functioning is essential for ﬁnancial stability, the
implementation of monetary policy and the promotion of public
conﬁdence in making cashless payments.
In the domain of payments and settlement systems’ supervision,
the following are subject to supervision:
1. Payment and settlement systems (high value payment
systems and high volume and low value payment systems);
2. Clearing and settlement systems of securities;
3. Money remittances;
4. Payment tools;
5. Automated remote service systems.
When carrying out its oversight function, the NBM pays special
attention to systems that process, compensate, and settle high
value payments and pose a substantial risk in the event of an
operational or settlement failure.
At the same time, the NBM
ensures an appropriate level of attention to all components of the
payment system subject to supervision.
5.2
Licensing,
regulation
and
supervision
of
the
activity
of
providing payment and electronic
money issuing services
According to Law No.
114/2012 on payment services and
electronic money, the NBM licenses, regulates and supervises the
activity of payment service providers and electronic money
issuers.


94
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
The licensing process of non-bank payment service providers
(payment companies, electronic money issuers and postal service
providers) and the registration of payment agents is carried out
in compliance with a set of requirements, which ensure a uniform
and competitive approach to accessing and operating payment
services in Moldova.
As of 31.12.2022, nine non-bank payment service providers were
operating in the Republic of Moldova: two payment companies,
one postal service provider and six e-money issuers, of which, in
2022, ÎM "Moldcell" S.A. was licensed as an e-money issuer and
"Nova Pay" S.R.L. as a payment company for money remittance
services.
The regulatory framework developed by the NBM, in the
context of the payment services activity, aligns the domestic
regulations with the European rules, resulting in the adoption of
regulatory acts necessary to operate in the payment services
market. This framework is an essential support for increasing the
number of modern and secure payment services, increasing the
degree of competition in the payment services market due to
market access for non-bank providers, thus fostering efﬁciency
and better quality, reducing service costs, as well as achieving a
high level of non-cash payments and reducing cash in circulation.
In 2022, the National Bank of Moldova, together with the Ministry
of Finance, went through a number of stages in the process of
legislating the Draft Law related to the transposition into national
legislation of the provisions of Directive (EU) 2015/2366 on
payment
services
in
the
internal
market
(according
to
international usage – PSD2), such as: ﬁnalisation following opinion,
participation
in
discussions
organised
by
parliamentary
committees. In this context, the Law on the amendment of some
normative acts No.
209 of 15.07.2022 (hereinafter – Law No.
209/2022) transposing Directive (EU) 2015/2366 was adopted and
published in the Monitorul Oﬁcial of the Republic of Moldova.
The transposition of the European Directive (PSD2) into national
legislation is an exercise to further align the regulatory framework
for payment services with the new EU regulations in the ﬁeld and
is an important act for innovations in payment services, following
best practices and experiences in the region.
Among the main changes of the Law No. 209/2022, shall be listed
the following: new payment methods and solutions, more legal
clarity of the current legal framework on payment services, setting
new rules for both existing and new payment service providers and,
last but not least, more protection for payment service users. The
changes are major and require sufﬁcient time to be implemented,
and for this reason, several stages have been foreseen for the entry
into force of the amendments introduced by Law No. 209/2022.
Therewith, starting from 2022, the National Bank of Moldova has
become one of the beneﬁciaries of the Twinning technical
assistance project entitled: "Strengthening Supervision, Corporate
Governance and Risk Management in the Financial Sector",
implemented in the Republic of Moldova with the support of the
European Union, and one of the components of this project is to
ensure the necessary prerequisites for the accession of the
payment service providers community in the Republic of Moldova
to the Single Euro Payments Area (SEPA).


Chapter 5. Financial market infrastructures
95
Thereby, through this project, the National Bank of Moldova
beneﬁts from support in aligning its legislation and regulatory
framework with the SEPA rules, as well as assistance in preparing
the SEPA accession ﬁle (demonstrating the Republic of Moldova’s
compliance with the SEPA geographical scope extension criteria)
as a non-EEA (European Economic Area) member state.
It is
expected that the relevant application will be submitted to the
responsible EU authorities in the second half of 2023.
Therewith, on 17.11.2022, the National Bank of Moldova, within
the framework of the EU Twinning project, held an event entitled
"Accession of the Republic of Moldova to the Single Euro Payments
Area – achievements and prospects". The conference aimed at
informing the society, as well as the private business environment,
about the main beneﬁts of the Republic of Moldova’s accession to
SEPA, as well as the strategy for compliance with the European
Payments Council’s SEPA accession criteria, the efforts and
progress made in this respect by the country’s authorities, as well
as the steps to be taken in assuming this commitment.
The
conference was attended by senior representatives of the National
Bank of Moldova’s partners in the EU Twinning project, the
Director
General
of
the
European
Payments
Council,
representatives of the authorities of the Republic of Moldova
responsible for fulﬁlling some of the requirements of the SEPA
accession criteria, as well as the community of payment service
providers in the Republic of Moldova (banks and non-banks).
Similarly, during 2022 the following changes were made to the
secondary regulatory framework relevant to payment systems and
their participants:
1. On 20.01.2022 was approved the Decision of the Executive
Board of the NBM No. 4 "On amending the Regulation on the
activity
of
non-bank
payment
service
providers
No.
217/2019" which entered into force on 04.04.2022.
The
approval of the Decision of the Executive Board of the NBM
No. 4 of 20.01.2022 was dictated by the need to optimize
the regulatory framework applicable to the activity of
non-bank payment service providers.
The rules on the
licensing/registration process were adopted and improved,
through the National Bank of Moldova’s Information System
on licensing, authorisation and notiﬁcation, registration of
agents in the Register of payment companies/postal service
providers/Register of electronic money issuing companies
and efﬁcient capital management;
2. On 07.04.2022 was approved the Decision of the Executive
Board of the NBM No. 65 "On amending some normative acts
of the National Bank of Moldova", which entered into force on
20.05.2022.
Approval of Decision No.
65 of 07.04.2022
derives from the context of the implementation of the Project
for the Modernization of the Automated Interbank Payment
System (which is part of the Concept for the Development of
the Payments Sector in the Republic of Moldova) which
involves: logical separation of the RTGS (real-time gross
settlement
system)
and
DNS
(designated-time
net
settlement system) modules of the Automated Interbank
Payment System (AIPS), moving to a more advanced
payment message format (ISO 20022), creation of a more
advanced model of liquidity limit management by AIPS
participants.


96
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
5.3
Automated
interbank
payment
system
The Automated Interbank Payment System (AIPS) is the system
through which payments in MDL of/by participants on the territory
of the Republic of Moldova are carried out automatically. AIPS
is composed of the Real-Time Gross Settlement system (RTGS
system) and the Designated-time Net Settlement system (DNS
system). The RTGS system is intended for the processing of urgent
and large-value payments, while the DNS system is intended for
the processing of small-value payments.
At the end of 2022, 20 participants were registered in AIPS, namely:
• The National Bank of Moldova;
• 11 banks licensed by the National Bank of Moldova;
• Cash and settlement centre in Tiraspol city;
• 3 non-bank payment service providers;
• The State Treasury of the Ministry of Finance, including as a
mandated participant;
• The Single Central Securities Depository S.A.;
• Deposit Guarantee Fund in Banking System.
In 2022, a total volume of 14.3 million transactions worth MDL
1.2 trillion was processed through AIPS, increasing both in number
(3.8%) and value (13.0%) compared to 2021.
Average daily
volume52 of operations amounted to 56.5 thousand transactions
worth MDL 4.8 billion. The maximum volume of payments settled
daily in AIPS was reached on July 25, 2022, with 101.5 thousand
payments settled.
