On average per day, 4.7 thousand payments worth MDL 4.1
billion were settled through the RTGS system and 51.8 thousand
payments worth MDL 755.9 million through the DNS system. The
average value of a payment settled in the RTGS system was MDL
868.7 thousand, and of a payment settled in the DNS system –
MDL 14.6 thousand (Table A.17).
With regard to the structure of AIPS payments in 2022, it was
found that payment transactions made by participants on behalf
of clients were predominant (99.8% of the number of transactions
settled), but their total value remains relatively low (only 44.4% of
the value of transactions settled).
In terms of the structure of payments made on behalf of clients by
AIPS participants, we ﬁnd the following representation:
52In 2022 there were 254 operational days.


Chapter 5. Financial market infrastructures
97
• ordinary customer transfers – 59.1% of total payments;
• transfers related to budget payments – 37.9%;
• other types of payments – 3.0%.
In terms of value, transactions in AIPS made on behalf of
customers have the following structure:
• ordinary customer transfers – 63.1% of total payments;
• budget transfers – 29.1%;
• other types of payments – 7.8%.
During 2022, the average system availability for participants was
99.9%, an indicator that conﬁrms the ability of participants to use
AIPS, without discontinuities or incidents, during the operating
program.
5.4
The
Single
Central
Securities
Depository
The Single Central Securities Depository (CSD) system has been
operational since 31 July 2018 and is administered (managed and
operated) by Single Central Securities Depository S.A.
The CSD system is a securities settlement system, which provides
the Central Securities Depository with securities registration,
centralised administration, settlement and, where applicable,
ancillary services.
The CSD system is based on modern
straight-through-processing technology connected to AIPS and
provides an efﬁcient infrastructure and services for the settlement
of securities transactions and the registration of securities
ownership.
At the end of 2022, 20 participants were registered within the CSD
system, including:
• The National Bank of Moldova;
• 11 banks licensed by the National Bank of Moldova;
• Deposit Guarantee Fund in the Banking System;
• 7 non-bank investment companies.
Securities registration services provided by CSD
At the end of 2022, 351.6 million securities of type SS and NBC
with a nominal value of MDL 41.1 billion were registered in the
CSD system.
Therewith, according to data presented by the CSD at the end of
2022, 913.4 million corporate securities (CS) were registered in
the CSD system, of which 910.9 million ordinary shares, 2.3 million
preferential shares and 7.5 thousand municipal bonds. The total
value of CS for the reference period amounted to MDL 14.6 billion,
of which 99.5% of the total value of CS were ordinary shares.


98
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
Settlement services
During 2022, 9.2 thousand transfer orders worth MDL 318.8 billion
were settled in the CSD system.
Supervision of CSD activity
During the reporting year, a thematic audit of the activity of the
CSD was carried out, during which the internal control system and
the agreements concluded by the CSD were subject to veriﬁcation,
and some violations were found and proposals were put forward
for the necessary actions to be taken in order to remove them.
5.5
Cashless payment tools
Cashless payment tools are essential components of payment
systems and the National Bank carries out oversight of them to
ensure their efﬁcient and safe use.
Of all payment instruments, payment cards are the most widely
used by the general public (individuals). It should be noted that
the NBM undertakes relevant measures, in agreement with other
national authorities and institutions, to promote the use of cashless
payment instruments and to make the circulation of funds more
transparent.
Payment cards
The number of cards53 in circulation at the end of 2022 was 2.80
million units, an increase of 19.2% compared to the end of 2021
(Chart 5.2).
Against the backdrop of the increase in the number of cards
in circulation, the number of active54 cards increased by 16.5%
compared to the end of 2021, and the share of active cards in the
total number of cards in circulation did not change signiﬁcantly,
at 61.6%. It should be noted that this rate of card usage has
remained at the same level over the last few years.

Regarding the technical solution of payment cards, the number of

The rapid increase in the share of these types of cards in the
total number of cards in circulation has contributed to the gradual
53Standardised and, where applicable, personalised information medium by

Chapter 5. Financial market infrastructures
99
replacement of hybrid cards which, in turn, previously replaced

Thus, by the end of 2022, contactless
cards accounted for the largest share (97.6%) of all cards in
circulation in the Republic of Moldova. Hybrid cards (card with
microprocessor and magnetic stripe), whose number decreased
by 63.7% compared to the end of 2021, held a share of 2.2% of all
cards in circulation in the Republic of Moldova. At the end of 2022,
magnetic stripe cards were no longer in circulation, although at the
end of 2014 this type of card had a major share (55.1%). Virtual
cards held a modest share of 0.2%, with no signiﬁcant changes
over the last few years.

During 2022, 165.3 million transactions worth MDL 124.9 billion
were carried out using payment cards issued by payment service
providers in the Republic of Moldova, both in the country and
abroad, increasing by 27.6% in number and 26.5% in value
compared to 2021.
Of the total volume of transactions carried out with cards issued
in the country, 82.2% were cashless payments and 17.8% – cash
withdrawals.
The number of cashless payments increased by
33.8% compared to the previous year, while the number of cash
withdrawals increased by 5.3% compared to the same period.
Every day, an average of 80.7 thousand cash withdrawals and
about 372.2 thousand cashless payments are made with payment
cards issued in the Republic of Moldova. The average value of a
cash withdrawal reached MDL 2,681, up 12.8% from 2021. At the
same time, a cashless payment averaged MDL 338, increasing by
6.7% compared to 2021.
Domestic operations55 made with payment cards issued by
payment service providers account for 86.9% (143.6 million
transactions) of the total number of transactions carried out with
cards issued in the Republic of Moldova.
Of the total domestic transactions, the share of cashless payments
was 79.7% in number (75.2% for 2021) and 30.8% in value (26.9%
for 2021).

Every day, on the territory of the Republic of Moldova, an average
of 80.0 thousand cash withdrawals and about 313.4 thousand
cashless payments are made with domestic payment cards. The
average value of a domestic cash withdrawal transaction reached
MDL 2,673, up by 12.8% compared to 2021. Therewith, an average
cashless payment amounted to MDL 303, increasing by 5.6%
compared to 2021.
Of the total number of domestic operations, 62.3% were cashless
payments made with the physical presence of the card and 17.4%
without the physical card presence (CNP) (Chart 5.4).
The number of transactions with cards issued in the Republic of
Moldova and carried out abroad during 2022 (21.7 million
transactions) showed an increase of 26.2% compared to the
previous year. However, the number of transactions carried out
abroad is about 6 times lower than the number of domestic
transactions with cards issued in the Republic of Moldova. Out of

As regards the assessment of the payment card frauds, the basic
indicator applied is the share of the total amount of fraud
committed in the total amount of transactions carried out both at
home and abroad with payment cards issued by local providers. In
the Republic of Moldova, this indicator amounted to 0.0086% for
2022, which is about 4.2 times lower than the European average
(0.036% for the year 201956).
The positive trends that emerged in the card market in the Republic
of Moldova during 2022 were as follows:
- The number and value of card payments issued in the
Republic of Moldova at the physical locations of domestic
merchants increased by 37.8% and 36.6% respectively
compared to the previous year;
- The number and value of domestic card payments made
without physical use of the card (in e-commerce platforms,
automated remote service systems, etc.) increased by 27.2%
and 56.4% respectively compared to 2021;
- 55.2% of the number of non-cash transactions carried out
abroad with payment cards issued in the Republic of Moldova
are e-commerce payment transactions;

enable

through
an
IT
application,
authentication method and means of communication to have
remote access to funds on the payment account for the purpose
of obtaining information about the status of the payment account

The total number of ARSS holders amounted to 3,201.9 thousand
at the end of 2022, increasing by 23.4% compared to 2021. The
number of active holders57 also showed an upward trend of 23.6%
to 974.7 thousand,
which shows the growing interest and
conﬁdence of payment service providers’ customers to use ARSS.
Of all ARSS holders, active ARSS holders account for 30.4% (Chart

The distribution of ARSS holders by type of systems used reveals
that 54.5% of them are registered in internet-payments systems
(2.0 percentage points more than at the end of 2021); 45.5% of
their total number are mobile-payments holders (2.0 percentage
points more than at the end of 2021); 0.05% use PC-payments
(PC-payment holders are only legal entities), this share remained
at the same level as in 2021. Therewith, both in 2021 and during
2022 it was found that payment service providers stopped offering
telephone-payments systems.
In 2022, 47.3 million transactions were conducted through ARSS,
21.2% more than in 2021.
The increase in the number of
transactions via ARSS can be explained by the fact that,
customers of payment service providers are increasingly paying
for various services/goods via ARSS due to the convenience it
offers, a phenomenon that has gained momentum during the
COVID-19 pandemic.

Of the total number of credit transfer payment transactions
initiated by bank customers (21.4 million transactions), 89.1%
were in electronic format via the ARSS. Over the last 5 years,
there has been an improvement in this indicator, from 69.5% for
2018 to 89.1% for 2022, which indicates an increase in the degree
of digitisation of payment services in the Republic of Moldova.

transfers increased compared to the previous year by 4.1%, while
the number of paper transfers decreased by 4.4% compared to

Direct debiting
During 2022, 174.1 thousand transactions were carried out via
direct debit for a total amount of MDL 172.4 million, decreasing
in number by 10.2%, but increasing in value by 14.1% compared
to 2021. The total number of users of this instrument at the end
of 2022 was about 4.7 thousand, 49.0% less than in the previous
year.

In 2022, cash in circulation continued its upward trend (+8.7%,
to a value of MDL 37,788.4 million58), with a growth rate 3.6
percentage points higher than in 2021.

6.1
Evolution of currency (banknotes
and coins in circulation)
Banknotes in circulation, at the end of 2022, amounted in terms
of value to MDL 37,455.3 million, about 8.6% more than at the
end of the previous year. The evolution in value of banknotes in
circulation according to the structure by nominal value is shown in
the chart 6.1.
At the end of 2022 there were some 323.8 million banknotes in
circulation, up 1.9% compared to the end of 2021. Nominal value
development of banknotes in circulation in terms of quantity is
shown in the chart 6.2.
The MDL 200 banknote had the most signiﬁcant share, 26.7%, in
the total number of banknotes in circulation, followed by the MDL 1
banknote with a share of 25.1% (1.2 percentage points less than in
2021, due to the natural withdrawal of banknotes from circulation
as they wear out) and the MDL 100 banknote with a share of 15.7%.
The smallest share in the total number of banknotes in circulation
went to banknotes with a face value of MDL 500 (4.1%) and MDL
1,000 (1.8%).
According to the nominal value structure, at the end of the
reporting period, the quantity of banknotes in circulation was
approximately: for MDL 1 – 81.3 million banknotes, MDL 5 – 14.3
million banknotes, MDL 10 – 16.7 million banknotes, MDL 20 –
14.6 million banknotes, MDL 50 – 40.5 million banknotes, MDL 100
– 50.9 million banknotes, MDL 200 – 86.5 million banknotes, MDL
500 – 13.2 million banknotes and MDL 1,000 – 5.8 million
banknotes.
The face value structure of banknotes in circulation at the end of
2022 is shown in terms of quantity and value in the chart 6.3.
At the end of 2022, there were 937.3 million divisional coins in
circulation, equivalent to MDL 137.0 million, with a 2.7% increase
in quantity and a 2.9% increase in value compared to the previous
year (Charts 6.4 and 6.5).
58Including cash balance in the head ofﬁce.


104
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
The increase in the quantity of coins in circulation results from the
predominant increase in the number of 5 bani, 10 bani and 25
bani nominal value coins. According to the face value structure
in terms of quantity, at the end of the reporting year, the 1 ban
coin had a share of 7.6%, 5 bani – 25.7%, 10 bani – 30.9%, 25 bani
– 31.0% and 50 bani – 4.8%, and in terms of value, 0.5%, 8.8%,
21.2%, 53.0% and 16.5% respectively (Chart 6.6).
At the end of 2022, there were MDL 85.7 million coins in circulation,
their total value being MDL 179.4 million, with both the quantity
and value coefﬁcients increasing by approximately 1.5 times (+
48.4% and + 54.2% respectively) compared to 2021 (Charts 6.7
and 6.8).
The quantitative increase of MDL coins in circulation results from
the predominant increase in the number of MDL 1, MDL 2 and MDL
5 nominal value coins, as a result of the replacement of MDL 1 and
MDL 5 nominal value banknotes withdrawn from circulation as they
wear out. According to the nominal value structure, at the end of
the reporting period, the MDL 1 coin had a dominant quantitative
share of 53.9% of all MDL coins in circulation, the MDL 2 coin –
31.6%, MDL 5 – 10.5% and MDL 10, including commemorative
coins – 4.0%, and in terms of value – 25.7%, 30.2%, 25.2% and
18.9% respectively (Chart 6.9).

During 2022, the National Bank of Moldova put into circulation a
total amount of MDL 10,536.7 million, about 92.7% more than in
2021.
Banknotes issued in 2022 amounted to MDL 10,469.8 million in
value (of which MDL 8,333.1 million are modernized banknotes)
and 78.6 million in quantity, which represents an increase of about
93.5% and 80.2% respectively compared to the previous year.

The number of banknotes in circulation continued to decrease for

circulation was as follows: MDL 10 – 1.9 million banknotes, MDL 20
– 6.3 million banknotes, MDL 50 – 21.2 million banknotes, MDL 100
– 21.6 million banknotes, MDL 200 – 24.4 million banknotes, MDL
500 – 2.0 million banknotes and MDL 1,000 – 1.2 million banknotes.
The nominal value structure of banknotes put into circulation is
shown in terms of quantity and value in chart 6.10 and chart 6.11

The value of the metal divisional coins issued in 2022 was MDL 3.9

The nominal value structure of the issued divisional coins has
changed quantitatively compared to 2021 and indicates: for 5
bani – 4.6 million coins, 10 bani – 8.8 million coins, 25 bani – 11.1
million coins.
The share by nominal value of divisional coins issued is shown,
in terms of quantity and value, in the chart 6.12 and chart 6.13
respectively.
The value of MDL coins, including commemorative coins, issued
in 2022 amounted to MDL 63.0 million, about 20.7% more than
in 2021, and the quantity – 27.9 million coins, about 40.3% more
than in 2021.
The quantitative structure by nominal values of MDL coins issued
in 2022 indicates: for MDL 1 – 14.3 million coins, MDL 2 – 9.1
million coins, MDL 5 – 2.9 million coins, MDL 10 – 1.5 million coins
and for commemorative coins – 0.1 million coins.
The share of each nominal value of MDL coins issued is shown
in terms of quantity and value in the chart 6.14 and chart 6.17
respectively.
During 2022, through deposits made by licensed banks, the
National Bank of Moldova withdrew from circulation banknotes in

2021), which amounted to 72.7 million banknotes (about 27.4%

more than in 2021).

Maintaining the quality of banknotes in circulation in accordance
with the established criteria is one of the main objectives of
the National Bank of Moldova in the ﬁeld of cash operations.
Respectively, in 2022, 76.0 million banknotes were processed
at the central bank (14.2 million more than in 2021) by checking
both authenticity and quality. Following processing, some 29.4
million banknotes were sorted as unﬁt for circulation.

National currency counterfeiting
During 2022, counterfeit MDL banknotes registered in the banking
system amounted to 0.54 thousand pieces, an increase of about
19.4% than in 2021. Compared to the total number of genuine
banknotes in circulation at the end of the reference period, which
amounted to 323.8 million banknotes, the number of counterfeits
remained at a very low level, accounting for about 1.7 pieces per
1 million genuine banknotes in circulation.
The most counterfeits were recorded for the MDL 100 banknote,
with 309 counterfeits, followed by the MDL 20 banknote, with 186
counterfeits, the MDL 200 banknote, with 29 counterfeits, the MDL
50 banknote, with 13 counterfeits and the MDL 1,000 banknote,
with 6 counterfeits.
As a basis for comparison, in 2021 the number of counterfeits
of national currency totalled 0.46 thousand banknotes, and the
highest number of counterfeits was also recorded for the MDL
100 banknote, totalling 251 pieces and MDL 20 – 150 pieces,
followed by the MDL 50 banknote – 22 counterfeits, MDL 200 – 18
counterfeits and MDL 1,000 – 11 counterfeits59.
The share of counterfeit banknotes by nominal value identiﬁed
during 2022 and 2021 is shown in chart 6.16 and chart 6.19.
In order to combat the counterfeiting of monetary signs, the
National Bank of Moldova continues to recommend the public to
study the security features of banknotes described on the ofﬁcial
website
of
the
NBM
(access
the
link
here
https://www.bnm.md/ro/content/elementele-de-siguranta-pe-leul-
moldovenesc) and when making daily cash payments to be
vigilant against counterfeiting by checking the authenticity of
banknotes and coins and, in case of suspicion, to inform the
Ministry of Internal Affairs.
Therewith, the NBM regularly cooperates with the Ministry of
Internal
Affairs
in
withdrawing
counterfeit
banknotes
from
circulation, discouraging, and keeping counterfeiting as low as
possible, and ensures that the banking environment is informed in
an operational manner about the methods of detecting and
removing counterfeits from circulation.
59According to adjusted data reported by banks.

Numismatics issuance
At the end of 2022, there were 46.85 thousand commemorative

Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
Chapter 7
Legal
and
regulatory
framework
development activity
7.1
Prudential regulation, regulation of
accounting evidence in banks
During 2022, in order to implement the Law No. 202/2017 on
Banks’ Activity and to bring some normative acts in line with
the European framework, the National Bank of Moldova made
amendments:
• to Regulation No. 109/2018 on banks’ own funds and capital
requirements. Thus, the Regulation has been supplemented
with requirements for the inclusion in the calculation of own
funds at consolidated level of core Tier 1 own funds
instruments relating to a subsidiary owned by persons other
than undertakings included in the consolidation (minority
interests), additional Tier 1 and Tier 2 own funds instruments
issued
by
bank
subsidiaries,
as
well
as
own
funds
requirements for credit adjustment risk and counterparty
risk.
Concomitantly, in the context of prior approval for the
distribution of proﬁts to shareholders, the inclusion of the
option to issue an approval for a lower amount than that
requested by the bank, if the requested amount may lead to
non-compliance with own funds requirements or other
prudential indicators, or to jeopardise the bank’s stability;
• to Regulation No. 109/2019 on large exposures. The purpose
of the amendments was to specify some particularities
related to the calculation of the exposure value, which relate
to the treatment of counterparty credit risk for banks in the
context of large exposures of the bank. At the same time,
requirements have been established for reporting, on a
consolidated basis, information on the ten largest exposures
to banks and the ten largest exposures to ﬁnancial sector
entities other than banks;
• to Regulation No. 111/2018 on the treatment of credit risk
for banks. The amendments provide for updating the list
of external credit assessment ﬁrms, the list of countries
with equivalent regulatory regime, as well as clarifying some
issues related to the revaluation requirement for real estate
by excluding a redundant provision;
• to Instruction No. 117/2018 on the submission by banks of
COREP reports for supervisory purposes. The amendments
have been made in order to specify how to complete the


Chapter 7. Legal and regulatory framework development activity
111
report on credit risk, counterparty credit risk and incomplete
transactions: standardised approach to capital requirements.
The Instruction has also been supplemented with provisions
on the timing and reporting of own funds and large exposures
on a consolidated basis.
The obligation for banks to comply with banks’ own funds
requirements was also determined, as well as the obligations
related to the treatment of credit, operational and market
risk not only on an individual basis but also on a consolidated
basis;
• to Regulation No. 292/2018 on requirements for members of
the management body of the bank, ﬁnancial holding company
or mixed ﬁnancial holding company, managers of the branch
of a bank in another state, persons holding key positions
and the liquidator of the bank in liquidation. The Regulation
was amended in order to make it compulsory for the NBM
to conduct an interview with members of the management
body and to supplement the provisions on the assessment of
persons appointed to certain management and key positions;
• to Regulation No.
322/2018 on the framework for the
management of banks’ activity. The amendments relate to
the creation of the secondary regulatory framework related
to the bank’s recovery plan, which is an integral part of the
bank’s governance framework. Thus, requirements for the
preparation of the recovery plan have been established, as
well as the criteria for the NBM’s assessment of the
completeness of banks’ recovery plans. The main elements
of the recovery plan include summary of key elements,
governance information, strategic analysis, a communication
and information plan, an analysis of preparatory measures, a
description of recovery indicators, information on scenarios
used. These amendments made it mandatory to notify the
NBM of breaches of the threshold indicators and the decision
of the management body on the recovery actions applied.
In addition, provisions have been included regarding the
aspects
to
be
examined
in
the
process
of
assessing
independent thinking, namely the behavioural skills of the
member of the management body, as well as conﬂicts of
interest that would hinder the person’s ability to perform
his/her duties independently and objectively. The purpose of
the provisions is to increase clarity on the aspects of the
independent thinking assessment that the members of the
management body must have.
Amendments have been included to create the secondary
regulatory framework for managing the risk associated with
excessive leverage, which is an integral part of the framework
for managing the business of banks.
In addition, in order to avoid excessive risk-taking in lending,
adjustments to the requirements for banks’ credit risk
management policies have been introduced. In addition, in
order to avoid cash ﬂow mismatch from the perspective of
scheduling repayments only when loans mature, which may
negatively affect bank liquidity, these policies have been
supplemented with requirements on how banks should
schedule repayments;
• to Regulation No. 158/2020 on disclosure requirements for
banks. The amendments concern the updating of the list


112
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
of indicators related to the bank’s economic and ﬁnancial
situation, namely the indicators related to the bank’s liquidity.
Also, the requirements for the disclosure of the leverage ratio
have been extended;
• to Regulation No.
118/2018 on the audit of ﬁnancial
statements and audit for other purposes of banks, excluding
the provision regarding the limitation of audit ﬁrms that
perform the audit of the bank’s annual ﬁnancial statements
to enter into other contracts with the bank, namely with
regard to audit for other purposes;
• to Regulation No. 240/2013 on the bank’s transactions with
its afﬁliated persons. The list of transactions with related
persons has been revised and completed in order to exclude
those that will not be considered as transactions with related
persons.
Therewith, the aggregation of the value of the
transaction of the bank’s related person (which forms the
exposure) with the value of the transactions of the related
clients has been included for the purpose of determining the
threshold in the transaction approval process. The rules on
exemptions from prior approval of related party transactions
by the bank’s governing bodies have also been completed.
Also, during 2022, the National Bank of Moldova:
• established the Criteria applicable to the audit ﬁrm in order
to qualify it as a ﬁrm of international repute (DEB of the NBM
No. 138 of 30.06.2022). By this decision, criteria have been
set against which the audit ﬁrm will assess the extent of the
damage caused and/or the price of the shares, to ensure
maximum objectivity;
• approved in new wording Regulation No. 114/2022 on banks’
investments in real estate, aimed at implementing the
provisions of Law No 202/2017, aligning the terminology with
the Basel III framework and International Financial Reporting
Standards;
• approved in new wording Regulation No. 113/2022 on banks’
qualifying holdings. The Regulation contains provisions on
banks’ qualifying holdings in the capital of undertakings,
including the limits accepted for such holdings, and speciﬁes a
list of undertakings in the capital of which the bank may have
qualifying holdings, including control over the undertaking
and the procedure for obtaining prior approval issued by the
NBM for qualifying holdings;
Aligning the banking legislation of the Republic of Moldova with
international
standards
by
improving
the
quantitative
and
qualitative mechanisms of bank management will contribute to
promoting
a
safe
and
stable
banking
sector,
increasing
transparency, conﬁdence, and attractiveness of the domestic
banking sector for potential investors and creditors of banks, as
well as for depositors and customers, the development of new
ﬁnancial products and services.


Chapter 7. Legal and regulatory framework development activity
113
7.2
Foreign exchange regulation
In 2022, the National Bank of Moldova (NBM) introduced a number
of important amendments related to foreign exchange regulation,
aimed at improving the legal framework for foreign exchange.
• Amendments to the Law on Foreign Exchange Regulation
(Law No. 363/2022 on the amendment of some normative
acts) were made in order to implement the measures of the
National
Action
Plan
for
the
implementation
of
the
Association Agreement between the Republic of Moldova and
the European Union for the period 2017-2019, to adjust the
provisions of the law with the provisions of the amended
legal framework, as well as to complete other rules.
The basic amendments made to the Law on Foreign Exchange
Regulation relate to the following issues:
- implementation
by
the
NBM
of
the
solution
for
streamlining the licensing, authorization and notiﬁcation
process, which will allow the use of digital technologies
and the automation of the licensing process of the
foreign exchange activity of foreign exchange ofﬁces
and hotels;
- strengthening the process of licensing and supervision of
foreign exchange entities;
- establishing the right of licensed banks to grant loans in
foreign
currency
to
residents
for
the
purpose
of
repayment of loans obtained in foreign currency from
licensed banks;
- speciﬁcation and completion of some rules related to
the authorization of foreign exchange operations by the
NBM;
- adjustment
to
the
provisions
of
the
Law
on
the
Prevention and Combating of Money Laundering and
Terrorist Financing in order to prevent the establishment
of control or holding of leading positions by criminals
and organised criminal groups, their accomplices within
the foreign exchange ofﬁce/ hotel;
- adjustment to the provisions of the Administrative Code,
the Tax Code and the Civil Code;
- the establishment of the requirement for the provision
by the foreign exchange entities of surveillance and
video recording systems of their activity, as well as of a
new way of control – making control purchases, ensuring
that the rights of the customers of the foreign exchange
entities are not infringed, in particular in the part
concerning
the
revocation
of
foreign
exchange
operations;
- lowering the notiﬁcation threshold for certain loans/
credits received by residents from non-residents (from
the equivalent of EUR 50,000 to the equivalent of EUR
10,000), as well as excluding the application of the
notiﬁcation regime for the receipt of guarantees by
residents from non-residents.


114
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
• The amendments made to the Instruction on the notiﬁcation
of foreign liabilities are aimed at excluding the ground for
refusal of the NBM to record the foreign liability (i.e.
if
the competent authorities have provided information on the
suspicious nature of the transaction subject to notiﬁcation
and/or the activity of the resident), given that the notiﬁcation
is a statistical procedure, which aims to inform the NBM on
foreign liabilities received by residents from non-residents.
Also, given the statistical nature of the notiﬁcation procedure
for external liabilities, the purpose of recording them by the
NBM is speciﬁed, as well as the purpose of keeping a record
of external liabilities and the transactions carried out under
them.
• The amendments made to the Regulation on licensing,
control and sanctioning of foreign exchange entities and to
the Instruction on how to submit reports in electronic form
to the National Bank of Moldova are aimed at modifying the
manner of submission to the NBM by the licensed banks of
the information related to the opening of foreign exchange
ofﬁces and the installation of foreign exchange machines, in
connection with the termination of the use of the transport
component of AIPS for the submission of reports/ information,
as well as in view of the implementation of the solution for
streamlining the licensing, authorization and notiﬁcation
process of the NBM.


115
Chapter 8
International relations
8.1
International collaboration
International Monetary Fund (IMF)60
The International Monetary Fund plays a signiﬁcant role for the
economy of the Republic of Moldova, addressing the challenges
facing our country and supporting the creation of a strong ﬁnancial
sector and sustainable economic development.
During 2022, cooperation between the Republic of Moldova and the
IMF took place within the framework of visits to monitor and assess
progress in implementing reforms under the IMF programme.
In May 2022, following an IMF staff mission from 4-11 April 2022,
the IMF Executive Board61 completed the ad hoc assessment62 of
the 40-month programme with the Republic of Moldova, supported
by the Extended Credit Facility (ECF)63 and the Extended Fund
Facility (EFF)64, approved in December 2021. Therewith, following
the request of our country’s authorities for increased funding to
respond to the impact of the war in Ukraine and the energy crisis,
the IMF has approved an increase in the total disbursement amount
of about USD 260.11 million (USD 194.26 million Special Drawing
60http://bnm.md/en/content/relatia-republicii-moldova-cu-fondul-monetar-
international-fmi
61The IMF’s Board of Executive Directors is one of the bodies of the IMF, consisting
of 24 Executive Directors (elected by member countries) and the Managing Director
of the IMF, who chairs meetings of the Board. The Executive Board is responsible for
the day-to-day running of the IMF and usually meets several times each week and
conducts its work largely on the basis of documents prepared by IMF management
and staff.
https://www.imf.org/external/np/sec/memdir/eds.aspx
62Ad hoc assessments outside the agreed schedule may be requested by
the authorities of a country implementing an IMF-supported programme when
it faces urgent balance of payments problems that need to be addressed as
soon as possible, ahead of the scheduled assessment.
In the case of ad hoc
assessments, the IMF’s Executive Board is examining whether the programme is
being implemented according to agreed plans and whether the authorities will be
able to achieve their objectives.
63The Extended Credit Facility (ECF) is an IMF ﬁnancing instrument through
which eligible (low-income) countries can receive concessional ﬁnancial assistance
in a more ﬂexible form and better tailored to their various needs, including during
crises (e.g. in the case of protracted balance of payments problems).
https://www.imf.org/en/About/Factsheets/Sheets/2016/08/02/21/04/Extended-
Credit-Facility
64The Extended Fund Facility (EFF) is an IMF ﬁnancing instrument for countries
with large balance of payments imbalances caused by structural impediments
or slow economic growth and certain inherently precarious balance of payments
positions.
https://www.imf.org/en/About/Factsheets/Sheets/2016/08/01/20/56/Extended-
Fund-Facility


116
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
Rights (SDR65), under that programme, which reached a volume
of approximately USD 795.72 million (SDR 594.26 million).
An ofﬁcial IMF delegation led by Mr Kenji Okamura, Deputy
Managing Director of the IMF, was on an ofﬁcial visit to the
Republic of Moldova between 12-15 June 2022. During this visit
there was also a meeting between representatives of the NBM and
the IMF delegation where they discussed the progress in the
implementation of the NBM’s commitments under the reform
programme ﬁnancially supported by the IMF. At the end of his visit
to the Republic of Moldova, Mr. Kenji Okamura noted that it was
very productive and appreciated the achievements of the NBM in
the area of monetary policy and the rapid response to rising
inﬂation.
Following an IMF expert mission to our country from 20 July to 10
August 2022, the IMF Executive Board concluded in September
2022 the ﬁrst review of the above-mentioned programme. Thus,
the Republic of Moldova obtained access to SDR 20.65 million
(about USD 27 million) earmarked for budget support.
Both
assessments
assessed
the
implementation
of
the
IMF-supported programme by the authorities of our country
through the fulﬁlment of structural performance criteria on ﬁscal
governance, ﬁnancial sector surveillance and strengthening of
anti-corruption legislation.
The importance of adopting the
necessary legislation to strengthen the independence of the NBM
as well as supervision and regulation in the ﬁnancial-banking
sector was also emphasized.
Given that the Republic of Moldova is represented on the IMF
Board of Governors by the members of the Executive Board of the
NBM, who hold the positions of Governor and Alternate Governor
respectively, NBM representatives participated in the Spring and
Annual Meetings of the IMF and the World Bank Group, which
were held in Washington, D.C., USA, on April 19-24, 2022 and
October 10-16, 2022, respectively. These meetings covered topics
on global crises, the war in Ukraine, energy security, inﬂation,
global debt, current changes in the foreign exchange market, the
role of globalisation and climate change, prospects for ﬁnancial
inclusion, and other topics of global interest.
The Governor of the NBM also participated in the meeting of the
Constituency of the IMF66, which took place on 14-17 September
2022 in Sarajevo, Bosnia and Herzegovina, where policies on
inﬂation, energy security and cooperation etc. were discussed.
In 2022, total disbursements related to the servicing of IMF loans
to the NBM amounted to SDR 18.8 million (equivalent to USD
25.3 million), of which disbursements for the principal amount of
65Special Drawing Rights (SDR) is an international reserve asset created by the
IMF. The value of the SDR is determined on the basis of a basket of 5 currencies:
the US dollar, the euro, the pound sterling, the Chinese yuan renminbi and the
Japanese yen.
https://www.imf.org/en/About/Factsheets/Sheets/2016/08/01/14/51/Special-

66Within the IMF, the Republic of Moldova is part of the group of countries

Bulgaria, Cyprus, Croatia, Georgia, Israel, Luxembourg, Republic of North
Macedonia, Montenegro, Kingdom of the Netherlands, Romania, Republic of
Moldova and Ukraine.
The IMF Executive Director representing the countries
in this group since November 2020 is Paul Hilbers, elected for a 4-year term.

European Union (EU)67
For the implementation of the common objectives set by the
Republic of Moldova and the EU in the framework of the RM-EU
Association Agreement, on 4 March 2022, the Republic of Moldova
formally applied for EU membership. The EU Council invited the
European Commission to present its opinion on the Republic of
Moldova’s application as a ﬁrst step in the accession process.
In order to be able to draft such an opinion, on 11 and 19 April 2022,
the European Commission sent two parts of the Questionnaire on
Moldova’s application for EU membership to the authorities in
Chis, in˘au.
It is important to note that the NBM participated in the
comprehensive
exercise
to
complete
the
above-mentioned
questionnaire,
submitting
comprehensive
information
for
a
number of chapters related to the EU accession process, including:
chapter on economic criteria for EU accession, Chapter 4 (Free
movement of capital), Chapter 9 (Financial services), Chapter 17
(Economic and monetary policy), Chapter 18 (Statistics), Chapter
24 (Justice, freedom and security), Chapter 32 (Financial control).
The information presented by the NBM covers various areas in its
area of competence, for example: macroeconomic developments,
monetary and foreign exchange policy, central bank activity and
independence, the banking sector, ﬁnancial stability, ﬁnancial
market infrastructures and payment services, preventing and
combating money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing,
cash
operations, statistics, etc.
The European Commission’s Opinion of 17 June 202268 on
Moldova’s application for EU membership, issued following the
assessment of the replies to the above-mentioned questionnaire,
noted
Moldova’s
progress
in
several
areas,
including
the
signiﬁcant strengthening of ﬁnancial sector stability and the
consolidation
of
macroeconomic
policies.
The
European
Commission also noted that before the manifestation of global
inﬂationary pressures fuelled by the war in Ukraine, the NBM was
generally successful in keeping inﬂation within the target range of
5.0% per year with a possible deviation of ±1.5 percentage points
and supported a stable national currency.
67Cooperation relations between the Republic of Moldova and the EU are
developing on the basis of the Association Agreement between the Republic of
Moldova, on the one hand, and the European Union and the European Atomic
Energy Community and their Member States, on the other hand, signed in Brussels
on 27 June 2014, in force since 1 July 2016 (provisionally applied since 1 September
2014).
68https://neighbourhood-enlargement.ec.europa.eu/opinion-moldovas-
application-membership-european-union_ro


118
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
Among the progress listed by the European Commission were:
a well-capitalised banking sector, with all banks complying with
capital ratio requirements; a substantial decline in the share of non-
performing loans; and improved access to ﬁnance, especially for
small and medium-sized enterprises, thanks to successful reforms
in the banking sector.
Thereby, on 17 June 2022, the European Commission issued its
opinion on the membership application, recommending to the EU
Council to grant the Republic of Moldova the status of candidate
country for EU membership, with the understanding that the
Republic of Moldova will undertake certain measures in a number
of areas.
Subsequently, on 23 June 2022, the European Council recognised
Moldova’s European perspective and decided to grant it the status
of candidate country for EU membership.
In the context of preparations for the accession of the Republic
of Moldova to the EU, the NBM participates in the Coordination
Mechanism for the European Integration Process of the Republic
of Moldova, approved by Government Decision No. 868/202269.
The purpose of this national mechanism is to organise the inter-
institutional coordination processes according to the EU accession
criteria and to gradually prepare the Republic of Moldova for the
opening of EU accession negotiations. The Government Decision
also establishes the institutional composition of the working groups
involved in ensuring the implementation of the mechanism. These
groups are set up for each of the 33 negotiating chapters related to
EU accession and their general task is to contribute in an organised
manner to the preparation of the Republic of Moldova to advance
in the EU accession process.
In this context, NBM representatives were delegated to 12
working groups established at national level under Government
Decision No. 868/2022, as follows: Working Group responsible for
monitoring the economic criterion; Working Group for Cluster No.
2
"Internal
Market";
Working
Group
for
Cluster
No.
3
"Competitiveness and Inclusive Growth"; Working Group No. 3
"Right of Establishment and Freedom to Provide Services";
Working Group No. 4 "Free Movement of Capital"; Working Group
No.
5 "Public Procurement"; Working Group No.
9 "Financial
Services"; Working Group No. 17 "Economic and Monetary Policy";
Working Group No. 18 "Statistics"; Working Group No. 24 "Justice,
Freedom and Security"; Working Group No. 32 "Financial Control";
Working Group No. 33 "Financial and Budgetary Provisions".
It should be noted that the NBM has an important role to play in the
European integration process of the Republic of Moldova, given its
tasks and their link with the EU accession criteria. Therefore, the
NBM, as the central bank of the Republic of Moldova, continuously
promotes the alignment of its regulatory framework and activities
with EU legislation and best practices, and actively participates in

European
Bank
for
Reconstruction
and
Development
(EBRD)70
In the Republic of Moldova EBRD is among the top institutional
investors.
It should be noted that the presence of the EBRD as a shareholder
in the banking sector of the Republic of Moldova contributes to
improving corporate governance, which complements the NBM’s
efforts to admit into the sector only quality shareholders who fully
meet the criteria of respectability and professionalism.
In 2022, the NBM cooperated with the EBRD to implement the
EBRD Country Strategy for the Republic of Moldova for 2017-
2022, approved by the EBRD Board of Directors in 2017. Among
the priorities of this strategy were improving governance and
strengthening resilience by driving banking sector restructuring.
The NBM also continued its cooperation with this institution in the
context of the ﬁnalization of the Strategy for the Republic of
Moldova for the period 2023-2028, adopted by the EBRD. Thus,
the NBM speciﬁed about the systemic and deep reforms carried
out during the last years in the banking sector, which contributed
to achieve a high level of resilience and stability of the banking
sector and thanks to which the licensed banks of the Republic of
Moldova not only managed to overcome the pandemic crisis
without signiﬁcant difﬁculties, but also became part of the
mechanisms of support of the national economy during the crisis
period. The NBM also mentioned the importance of continuing the
policy dialogue with the IMF and the EU to strengthen the
resilience of both the banking and non-banking sectors.

71The European Automated Clearing House Association (EACHA) is a non-proﬁt

the Automated Interbank Payment System (AIPS), became a member of EACHA in
2014.

72The International Operational Risk Working Group – IORWG is a working
group for central banks and monetary and supervisory authorities, which aims
to exchange experience, innovate the methodological framework and share best
practices among central banks in the ﬁeld of operational risk management. The
NBM became a member of the IORWG in 2015.
73The Centre of Excellence in Finance (CEF) is an international organisation with
a mission to support the implementation of public ﬁnance and ﬁnancial-banking
reforms in South East Europe through training activities. The CEF was established
in 2001 within the framework of the Stability Pact for South Eastern Europe by the
Government of Slovakia, at the initiative of the Slovenian Ministry of Finance and in
close cooperation with other Ministries of Finance of the former Yugoslav countries
and Albania. In 2015, CEF became an international organisation after 14 successful
years as a regional institution.
The Republic of Moldova became a member of the CEF by approving Law No.
115/2015 on the ratiﬁcation of the Agreement on the establishment of the Centre
for Excellence in Finance. In the Governing Board of the CEF, the Republic of
Moldova is represented by the NBM and the Ministry of Finance, each with one vote
in the decision-making process.

of
Understanding
aimed
at
cooperation
and

and

of
entities
falling
within
the

Within the framework of the performance of the tasks of licensing
the banks’ activity, the NBM cooperated with the following

In 2022, NBM representatives participated in a number of events
and seminars on various areas of central bank activity organized

France, the National Bank of Georgia, the Central Bank of Germany,
the Central Bank of Italy, the Central Bank of Poland, the Polish

External technical cooperation with international bodies
and central banks of other countries
Assistance from external development partners continued to play
an important role in the institutional strengthening of the NBM and
contributed signiﬁcantly to the process of reform and consolidation
of the banking sector of the Republic of Moldova. In this regard,
in order to ensure the successful implementation of the NBM’s
medium-term strategic objectives, as well as to strengthen its
institutional capacities in various areas of activity speciﬁc to a
75JVI is a regional training centre primarily for public sector ofﬁcials from
countries in Central, Eastern and South-Eastern Europe, the Caucasus and Central
Asia.
It was established in 1992 by the Austrian authorities (represented by
the Federal Ministry of Finance and the Austrian Central Bank), the International
Monetary Fund and several other international organisations.
Seminars and
workshops offered by the JVI focus on macroeconomic policy formulation and
management, monetary policy and the exchange rate system, ﬁnancial sector
stability, macroeconomic and ﬁnancial statistics and many other specialised topics.


124
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
central bank, the institution promotes effective cooperation in
various bilateral technical cooperation projects with development
partners and counterpart institutions in other countries.
The institution’s cooperation with international development
partners takes place through the organisation of different types of
external technical assistance (ETA) such as: consultancy, missions
of foreign experts, video conferences, study visits, professional
certiﬁcations and the mixed type which can include different
combinations of activities listed above.
During the year under review, the Bank worked with the donor
community to implement a number of external assistance
projects, which helped strengthen the institution’s capacity to
achieve its objectives. Thus, during 2022 the NBM beneﬁted from
ETA to strengthen its capacities in various areas, such as: reﬁning
the bank recovery and resolution framework; payment systems;
preventing
and
combating
money
laundering
and
terrorist
ﬁnancing;
ﬁnancial
stability;
statistics;
monetary
policy
instruments; bank licensing; strengthening bank supervision
capacities; suptech and ﬁntech; increasing the effectiveness of
the NBM’s institutional capacity (e.g.
internal audit, ﬁnancial
education and international cooperation).
Among the ETA donors to the NBM during the reporting period
were the European Union, the International Monetary Fund, the
World Bank Group, the United States Agency for International
Development,
the
European
Bank
for
Reconstruction
and
Development,
the United States Department of State,
the
National Bank of Romania, the Central Bank of Lithuania, the
Central Bank of Germany and the Ministry of Finance of the
Kingdom of the Netherlands.
The European Union (EU) has traditionally been one of the
institution’s key development partners.
In the context of the
Republic of Moldova being granted the status of a candidate
country to become a member of the EU, the NBM aims to further
develop its institutional capacities based on EU standards and
best practices in order to fulﬁl its commitments on the European
path and to ensure a high level of ﬁnancial sector stability for the
beneﬁt of its citizens.
A special place in achieving this goal is given to the Twinning
project "Strengthening supervision, corporate governance and
risk management in the ﬁnancial sector"76. Thus, from October
2021, the NBM and the National Commission for Financial Markets
will beneﬁt from the valuable expertise of EU partner institutions
(the consortium formed by the National Bank of Romania, as
senior partner, and the Central Bank of the Netherlands and the
Central Bank of Lithuania, as junior partners, in collaboration with
the Romanian Financial Supervisory Authority and the Romanian
National Authority for Consumer Protection).
76The project budget of EUR 1.72 million is allocated to ﬁve core components:
Component 1: Macro-prudential framework strengthened by improving regulatory
capacities to reduce the build-up of systemic risks; Component 2: The new legal
framework on insurance supervision and third party liability for motor insurance
in line with the EU acquis is developed and implemented; Component 3: New
legal framework strengthening the supervision of non-bank credit organisations
is developed and implemented; Component 4:
NBM internal regulations on
supervisory activities revised, developed and improved; Component 5: Moldova’s
legislation and regulatory framework are in line with the requirements of the SEPA
schemes and the application procedure for non-EEA membership is ready.


Chapter 8. International relations
125
During the reporting period, project activities focused largely on
strengthening the macro-prudential framework by improving the
capacities of regulators to reduce the build-up of systemic risks,
harmonising the legal framework in the insurance sector, and the
supervision of entities in the non-bank ﬁnancial sector. Technical
assistance was also provided for the development and approval of
a new domestic regulation on the supervision and monitoring of
ﬁnancial market infrastructures and payment services, aligned
with international best practices and the EU acquis. In addition,
the NBM, with the support of the National Bank of Romania (NBR),
has
taken
further
steps
to
assess
the
current
regulatory
framework in the Republic of Moldova in accordance with the
requirements of the Single Euro Payments Area (SEPA) schemes
and to prepare the application ﬁle, as a non-member state of the
European Economic Area, for SEPA accession77. In this context,
we
note
the
organization
in
Chisinau
of
the
International
Conference on 17 November 2022, entitled "Accession of the
Republic of Moldova to the Single Euro Payments Area –
achievements and prospects".
Also through the Twinning cooperation instrument, thanks to the
ﬁnancial support provided by the EU, the project "Efﬁciency of the
system to prevent and combat money laundering and terrorist
ﬁnancing in the Republic of Moldova" was implemented, with
15 institutions in the Republic of Moldova with responsibilities
in the ﬁeld of combating money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing
(CML/TF), including the NBM. Thus, during April 2022, within the
framework of the project, NBM experts participated in a study visit
to Poland, Warsaw, to study the Polish practice of inter-institutional
cooperation in this ﬁeld. NBM CML/TF experts also participated
in a study visit to Madrid, where the participants familiarised
themselves with aspects related to the institutional and regulatory
framework of this area in Spain.
An ETA project for the NBM aimed at contributing to the
advancement of the European path of the Republic of Moldova is
the "Mission of the High Advisers of the European Union to the
Republic of Moldova" completed in December 2022, where the
National Bank of Moldova continued to beneﬁt from the assistance
of the EU High Adviser for the ﬁnancial sector.
The Bank beneﬁted from the assistance of an EU medium-term
expert from the NBR from June to December 2022, which focused
on consolidation of the banking sector and implementation of
risk-based supervision in line with Basel III principles.
The
recommendations provided by the NBR expert will contribute to
improving the internal regulation of banking supervision, as well
as the management practices governing the activity carried out
by banks and aligning them with the relevant provisions contained
in the European Banking Authority guidelines.
Also noteworthy is the initiation of technical assistance from the
European
Bank
for
Reconstruction
and
Development
(EBRD) on money market diagnostics using the survey method.
To this end, the Cooperation Agreement was signed on 26 August
2022 by the parties involved. The results of the exercise will be
reﬂected in an executive report, which will contain the gaps
identiﬁed and some conclusions/recommendations following the
questionnaire carried out. These recommendations will contribute
77SEPA – Single Euro Payments Area


126
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
to the improvement of the transmission mechanism of monetary
policy decisions and the effective management of excess liquidity.
International Monetary Fund (IMF) continues to be one of the
main external partners providing technical assistance on various
areas speciﬁc to central bank activity.
Following the ﬁnancial stability assessment exercise conducted in
2021 by IMF experts, in early 2022 the report "Financial Sector
Stability Review (FSSR) of the Republic of Moldova" was published.
As a reminder, the IMF, in the ﬁrst phase, carried out the
assessment of the ﬁnancial sector, i.e.
prudential framework,
systemic risk assessment and stress testing, ﬁnancial crisis
management, ﬁnancial inclusion and capital market development.
The second phase of the FSSR focused on assessing ﬁnancial
sector development needs and providing recommendations and
appropriate
assistance
for
their
implementation,
with
30
recommendations identiﬁed for NBM input.
In the context of the implementation of a recommendation of
the FSSR Report, IMF experts provided advice to the NBM in the
reﬁnement of the legal framework on the compulsory liquidation of
banks, with the objective of assessing the sufﬁciency and adequacy
of the relevant legal framework for the compulsory liquidation
of banks, identifying an optimal model for the regulation and
management of the process of compulsory liquidation of banks
and developing a draft law reﬂecting the optimal model identiﬁed.
We mention the IMF assistance related to the regime to prevent
and combat money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing in order to
enhance ﬁscal discipline to the NBM, the Money Laundering
Prevention and Combating Service and the State Tax Service.
Existing CML/TF tools, such as those used to identify beneﬁcial
owners of entities and accounts, can be applied to enhance
corporate transparency, facilitate the detection and prosecution of
tax offences and cases of improper transfer of proﬁts (including to
non-transparent offshore jurisdictions) and ﬂag situations where
tax audits are needed. In doing so, the Government aimed to
improve the exchange of information between authorities and the
quality of information on actual beneﬁciaries, as well as to
strengthen cooperation between institutions.
From 5 to 8
September 2022, a mission of experts from the IMF’s Legal
Department took place to provide support for development of the
framework and tools for analysing money laundering risks arising
from cross-border payments. As a result of the meetings, at the
end of 2022, the experts submitted a report reﬂecting the ﬁndings
of this mission and recommendations for further action to be
taken, as well as a plan of measures for their implementation.
We appreciate the collaboration with the IMF in the area of statistics
in the framework of the long-standing project on compilation of
ﬁnancial accounts and sectoral balance sheets statistics (FASBS).
A technical assistance mission took place from April to May 2022,
following which all sectoral balance sheet datasets were revised
and new sources for the compilation of annual and quarterly
sectoral balance sheets were added. The methodology for the
compilation of the sectoral balance sheets was also adjusted to
include the chapter on publication and data revision policies.
Another signiﬁcant external partner of the NBM is the World Bank
Group.

increased ﬁnancial stability and economic growth.

131
Chapter 9
Institutional
and
organizational
framework
9.1
Corporate
Governance
of
the
National Bank
Law No. 548/1995 on the National Bank of Moldova provides the
legal authority and governance framework of the National Bank.
The structure of corporate governance includes governing bodies,
committees that support governing bodies, and external and
internal control levels. This structure is complemented by the
compliance and ethics framework, which includes the Code of
Conduct for NBM Employees, the Anti-Fraud Policy of the National
Bank of Moldova, the Information Security Policy, as well as the
public access to normative acts, statistics, and publications of the
NBM.
Governing bodies of the National Bank
Supervisory Board
The Supervisory Board consists of 7 members appointed by the
Parliament for a 7-year term: 4 non-salaried members of the NBM
and 3 members of the executive structure of the National Bank –
the Governor, the First Deputy Governor, and a Deputy Governor.
The Chairman of the Supervisory Board is the Governor of the NBM.
During 2022, the Supervisory Board operated in full composition.
In exercising its powers attributed by law, the Supervisory Board
(i) approves the annual report and the annual ﬁnancial statements;
(ii) adopts the internal control system standards, continuously
checks and assesses the operation of the internal control system;
(iii) appoints the controller general upon the proposal of the Audit
Committee and establishes the terms of his/her remuneration,
(iv) adopts the rules of professional ethics; (v) determines the
remuneration fund of the National Bank, the level of remuneration
of the members of the Executive Board and the amount of monthly
indemnities of the members of the Supervisory Board who are not
members of the Executive Board; (vi) submits proposals to increase
the National Bank’s capital; (vii) approves the National Bank’s
estimates of expenses and investment allowances and monitors
their execution; (viii) selects, on a tender basis, the external audit
organization; (ix) establishes the method in which the National
Bank Committees are created and operated; (x) determines the
nominal value, the design of banknotes and metallic coins, the way
of putting into circulation and the conditions for their withdrawal


132
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
from circulation; (xi) approves the internal regulations relating to
the exercise of its powers. The Supervisory Board submits to the
Parliament, at least annually, reports on the developed supervision
activity.
In order to perform its duties during 2022, 10 meetings of the
Supervisory Board were convened,
and 39 decisions were
approved, with the following issues:
• Approval of the Report on internal audit activity in 2021;
• Approval of the Reports on the level of implementation in
the second half of 2021 and in the ﬁrst half of 2022 of the
projects of the National Bank of Moldova;
• Approval of the Report on the management of operational
risk in 2021 in the National Bank of Moldova;
• Approval of the Report of the Audit Committee of the National
Bank of Moldova on the monitoring activity in 2021;
• Approval of the Report of the National Bank of Moldova for
2021;
• Approval of the Report of the Supervisory Board of the
National Bank of Moldova on supervisory activity in 2021;
• Approval of the graphic designs, nominal values and putting
into circulation of anniversary and commemorative coins
issued in 2022;
• Examination of the direct or indirect ﬁnancial interests of
members of governing bodies and members of their families;
• Amendments to the internal regulations of the National Bank
of Moldova;
• Approval of the Financial Statements of the National Bank of
Moldova for the year ended 31 December 2021;
• Approval of the National Bank of Moldova’s Investment
Estimate and Allocations for 2023;
• Approval of the Internal Audit Plan for 2023;
• Approval
of
the
Supervisory
Board’s
indicative
work
programme for 2023.
During 2022, 2 decisions of the Supervisory Board were submitted
for publication in the Monitorul Oﬁcial of the Republic of Moldova
(according to Table A.19).
In its work, the Supervisory Board has continuously cooperated
with representatives of Moldova’s development partners and other
ﬁnancial and banking institutions on issues of common interest.
The Supervisory Board promoted transparency in decision-making
and monitored the activities of the NBM with reference to its
statutory tasks.


Chapter 9. Institutional and organizational framework
133
Executive Board
The Executive Board is composed of 5 members appointed by
Parliament: the Governor, the First Deputy Governor, and three
Deputy Governors. The Chairman of the Executive Board is the
Governor of the National Bank.
EB member, Deputy Governor Arcadie Albul, took ofﬁce on 1 March
2022.
According to Law No. 548/1995, the main duties of the Executive
Board are:
(i) to establish the monetary policy in the state,
including the level of interest rates on monetary policy tools, the
conditions for granting loans, the type and level of reserves that
banks are obliged to maintain with the National Bank; (ii) to
establish the foreign exchange policy in the state and the
exchange rate regime of the national currency; (iii) to decide on
the procedure of issuing of licenses, authorizations, permits,
approvals to be issued by the National Bank; (iv) to examine the
results of controls performed on the entities supervised by the
National Bank and adopt the related decisions; (v) to decide on
the issuance of the National Bank’s claims, their volume and
conditions of issuance; (vi) to adopt the normative acts of the
National Bank; (vii) to ensure the implementation of the decisions
of the Supervisory Board; (viii) to plan and organize the current
activity of the National Bank.
Its Chair shall convene meetings of the Executive Board at least
once a month.
In order to perform its duties during 2022, the Executive Board
convened 106 meetings, during which 282 resolutions were
examined and approved.
These resolutions concerned the following areas:
• Monetary and foreign exchange policy of the National Bank
of Moldova;
• Financial stability;
• Payment systems oversight policy in the Republic of Moldova;
• Banking regulation and supervision;
• Regulation and supervision in the ﬁeld of combating money
laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing;
• Bank resolution;
• Financial markets;
• Reporting and statistics;
• Cash operations;
• Internal acts of the National Bank of Moldova.
During 2022, the Executive Board issued for publications 33
decisions in the Monitorul Oﬁcial of the Republic of Moldova
(according to tables A.19 and A.20).


134
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
The
Executive
Board
has
continuously
promoted
the
implementation of monetary policy tools and measures to ensure
and maintain price stability; ensure the economy with the national
currency and promote the credibility of the national currency;
regulate and supervise banks and maintain ﬁnancial stability;
regulate
and

the
activity
of
ﬁnancial
market
infrastructures, as well as the efﬁciency, safety, accessibility, and
