Governor
The Governor of the National Bank is appointed by the Parliament
on the proposal of the President of the Parliament.
According to the Law No.
548/1995 on the National Bank of
Moldova, the Governor is responsible for formulating monetary and
foreign exchange policy initiatives to be submitted to the Executive
Board and for their execution. The Governor also organizes and
manages the Bank’s activity and represents it in relations with
any legal or natural person both in the Republic of Moldova and
abroad, issues binding orders and provisions for the National
Bank’s employees, controls their execution.
Audit Committee
The
Audit
Committee
consists
of
three
members
of
the
Supervisory Board who are not employees of the National Bank.
The Audit Committee assists the Supervisory Board by advising
on: (i) monitoring the ﬁnancial reporting process of the NBM; (ii)
monitoring the effectiveness of the internal control and risk
management system; (iii) monitoring and directing the internal


Chapter 9. Institutional and organizational framework
135
audit function; (iv) monitoring the independence and work of the
external audit.
In order to ensure the work and fulﬁlment of its duties, in 2022,
the Audit Committee undertook a series of practical measures and
actions, the most important of which are:
• Reviewing and coordinating the Additional Agreement to the
contract with the audit organisation, relating to the schedule
of audit services for the ﬁnancial statements for the year
ended 31 December 2021 and changes in the composition of
the ﬁnancial audit team;
• Reviewing with members of the external audit team the
Annual Financial Statements and their compliance with
International Financial Reporting Standards;
• Examination of the Activity Reports and coordination of the
Activity Plans of the Internal Audit Department of the NBM;
• Assessment of the Controller General’s performance for 2022;
• Review procedures for monitoring and controlling risk
exposures and compliance issues that could have a material
impact on the NBM Financial Statements;
• Formulation of proposals for improvement.
During the reporting period, the Audit Committee developed and
presented to the SB of the NBM recommendations for streamlining
the work of the Internal Audit Department,
improving and
developing the NBM’s business processes, including on the NBM’s
ﬁnancial reporting process; the process of consolidating the new
regulatory and methodological framework for managing the
NBM’s operational risks and creating a centralised system for
managing all risks; implementation of the recommendations put
forward by the external evaluator in the External Quality
Assessment Report of the Internal Audit Function related to the
development
of
professional
development
plans
for
IAD
employees, development of the strategic approach in audit
missions, development of an internal audit strategy.
Operational committees
In order to ensure the efﬁciency of the decision-making process,
the Audit Committee,
the Investment Committee,
the Risk
Committee, and the Business Continuity Committee operate
within the NBM. The Standing Committees have their own rules of
operation detailing their purpose,
composition,
duties,
and
speciﬁc responsibilities.
Investment Committee
The Investment Committee (IC) is established to assist the
Executive Board of the NBM in reviewing investment policies and
strategies related to the management of the foreign exchange
reserves of the state. The Investment Committee is composed of
10 members, employees of the NBM, including 2 members of the
Executive Board, one of whom is the Chairman of the Committee.
IC meetings are convened, as a rule, on a monthly basis, and
extraordinary meetings are held in the case of signiﬁcant issues.


136
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
The Investment Committee held 11 ordinary meetings in 2022.
Each meeting discussed the performance of the investment
portfolio, the evolution and forecasts related to the domestic
foreign
exchange
and
international
ﬁnancial
markets,
the
evolution of the ratings of authorised counterparties, and the
dynamics and factor analysis of risk indicators. At the same time,
they
regularly
discussed
issues
regarding
the
short-
and
medium-term
investment
strategy
of
the
Strategic
Asset
Allocation (SAA).
Also, in December 2022, an extraordinary meeting of the IC has
been convened to review the SAA framework. In addition, tactical
investment decisions were examined with a view to exploiting
investment alternatives based on the international ﬁnancial market
environment and emerging trends, provided that investment safety
and liquidity were ensured.
Risk Committee
The Risk Committee, as part of the second line of defence, is
established to assist the Executive Board in providing objective
direct oversight of the management of the operational risk
management system in the National Bank. The Risk Committee
consists of 8 voting members, including one member of the
Executive Board – the Risk Committee Chairperson.
The Risk Committee meets quarterly in regular meetings.
If
necessary or at the request of the Governor or any other member
of the Executive Board, it may be convened ad hoc.
In 2022, the Risk Committee met in 4 regular meetings and 2 ad
hoc meetings.
The basic decisions taken concerned:
1. endorsement of the report on operational risk management
in 2021 in the National Bank of Moldova, of dashboard and
risk proﬁle for 2021;
2. acceptance of the assessed maturity level of the operational
risk management system in the NBM by 31 December 2021,
and its development objectives for 2022;
3. taking note of the quarterly reports on the results of the self-
assessment of operational risks on NBM business processes;
4. quarterly taking note of the NBM’s operational risk proﬁle for
the processes assessed in 2022;
5. approve the proposed treatment measures to respond to the
risks in the yellow area of the risk proﬁle;
6. taking note of the information related to incidents with major
impact on business processes and validating their level of
impact;
7. taking note of the measures in place to manage the business
continuity risk arising from external factors – the outbreak
caused by the COVID-19 virus and the war in Ukraine;
8. taking note of the information on the membership of the
International Operational Risk Working Group – IORWG;


Chapter 9. Institutional and organizational framework
137
9. strengthening control measures to ensure the continuity of
processes
in
critical
periods,
as
well
as
submitting
recommendations for the management of operational risks
related
to
business
continuity,
in
the
context
of
the
declaration of a state of emergency and the situation related
to regional security and the threat to national security and
supplying the NBM with energy resources in the context of
the energy crisis.
Business Continuity Committee
The Business Continuity Committee (BCC) is established to assist
the Executive Board in managing the continuity of efﬁcient
business in order to achieve the continuity objectives of the
National Bank of Moldova. The Business Continuity Committee is
composed of 12 members, 10 of whom have voting rights,
including one member of the Executive Board – the Chairperson of
the BCC, who is the Governor of the National Bank.
The Business Continuity Committee meets annually in ordinary
meetings and ad hoc meetings are organised in case of events
that may affect the continuity of the NBM’s activity.
In 2022, the Business Continuity Committee met in 3 ad hoc
meetings where continuity risk management issues were discussed
in the context of the regional security situation and the threat to
national security. At each meeting decisions were taken to ensure
a high level of resilience of the National Bank.
Levels of control
External control
External audit
The external audit of the National Bank is performed annually for
the ﬁnancial statements, accounts, and registers of the NBM, in
accordance with the international auditing standards, by an
independent auditing company of recognized reputation and
experience in the ﬁeld of auditing central banks and international
ﬁnancial institutions, selected by the Supervisory Board on the
basis of an auction. The report of the external auditor shall be
published together with the annual ﬁnancial statements of the
National Bank. The same external audit organisation may not be
appointed consecutively for a period exceeding 5 years.
IMF assessment of the National Bank security
Periodically, the credibility and security of central banks is
assessed by IMF experts in order to receive assurance in the
implementation and operation by the NBM of control, accounting,
reporting, and audit systems related to the management of
resources that are adequate for the integrity of its operations. The
assessment covers ﬁve key areas relevant to central bank control
and governance under the acronym ELRIC:
• E – external audit mechanism;


138
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
• L – legal framework and central bank independence;
• R – ﬁnancial reporting;
• I – internal audit mechanism;
• C – system of internal control.
Audit of the Court of Accounts of the Republic of Moldova
The external public audit regarding the legality and regularity of
expenditure estimates and investment allocations is performed
by the Court of Accounts, being limited, according to the Law No.
548/1995, to examine the operational efﬁciency of decisions taken
by the management of the National Bank, with the exception of
those related to the implementation of the monetary and foreign
exchange policy of the National Bank and the management of the
State’s foreign exchange reserves.
Risk management and internal control
The National Bank aligns its risk management governance and
internal control system with international standards COSO81, ISO
3100082, best practices (including the International Operational
Risk Working Group in Central Banks (IORWG), of which the
National Bank is a member).
In the context of operational risk management, the Supervisory
Board has established the risk appetite and risk tolerance in the
National Bank with the approval of the Framework on operational
risk management in the National Bank of Moldova.
Risk management and internal control within the NBM are
established according to the three lines of defence.
The ﬁrst line of defence manages risks and is represented by
NBM subdivisions, which are also the "owners" of the risks. The
heads of the subdivisions (line managers) are responsible for
identifying and managing the risks inherent in the business
processes and the IT systems they own, properly establishing the
control activities in the risk management process. In 2022, the
activities of self-assessment of operational risks on the activity
processes continued with the establishment of control measures
to maintain the risks in the tolerance zone, with the reporting of
the results to the operational risk management function.
The
subdivisions apply a set of control procedures on a daily basis to
the business processes and systems they manage, ensuring their
effectiveness and efﬁciency.
The second line of defence consists in organising the process,
ensuring
the
methodological
framework,
coordinating
and
independently
supervising
the
internal
control
and
risk
management process. The functions of the second line of defence
include
speciﬁc
monitoring
mechanisms
and
processes
to
implement
the
appropriate
ﬁnancial
and
operational
risk
management system and exercise internal control. The second
81COSO – The Committee of Sponsoring Organizations of the Treadway
Commission.
82ISO 31000 – Risk Management – Guidelines, International Organization for
Standardization.


Chapter 9. Institutional and organizational framework
139
line of defence is represented by the structures with functions
dedicated
to
ﬁnancial
and
operational
risk
management,
Investment Committee, Risk Committee and Business Continuity
Committee. This line develops the methodological framework for
an efﬁcient risk management process in the NBM: the internal
framework on the conduct and control of foreign exchange
operations, operational risk management by business processes,
business continuity and information security, personal data
protection,
standards,
policies and plans for responding to
incidents that disrupt or threaten the operational functions of the
National Bank. An incident management system is in place at the
National Bank, where incidents are recorded, including the actions
taken and who is responsible for carrying them out.
Operational risk management function ensured in 2022 the
organisation and conduct of operational risk self-assessment
meetings on business processes. Based on the operational risk
self-assessment, the operational risk proﬁle was developed and
reported quarterly and annually to the Risk Committee. At the
same time, internal trainings were carried out for subdivision
representatives (heads of subdivisions, operational risk ofﬁcers
and participants in the assessed processes).
In order to manage the business continuity risk, generated by
the external factor – the COVID-19 epidemic, in the ﬁrst half of
2022 a number of measures were taken to protect employees and
ensure the continuity of the NBM’s activity, which contributed to
the continuous conduct of the NBM’s business processes, with
the fulﬁlment of all basic tasks. These measures were reported
quarterly to the Risk Committee meetings.
Therewith, the operational risk management function represents
the NBM at IORWG as a member.
At the end of 2022, the second line of defence was strengthened
by establishing the compliance function. The compliance function,
established as a separate subdivision, aims to create, develop,
and
ensure
compliance
within
the
National
Bank
with
a
compliance framework, as well as to provide the necessary
support in developing and implementing an effective internal
control system to manage compliance risk related to established
regulations.
The third line of defence provides independent and objective
assurance and is represented by internal audit.
Internal audit
provides governing bodies and line managers with reasonable
independent assurance on the adequacy and effectiveness of
governance, risk management and internal control, including how
the ﬁrst and second line of defence achieve risk management
objectives.
It should be noted that the internal regulatory
framework for the operation of internal audit, which is approved at
the level of the Board of Supervisors, has been revised by aligning
it with the mandatory elements of the International Professional
Practices Framework (IPPF)83 developed by the Institute of
Internal Auditors (IIA). According to the external assessment
report dated 14.03.2021, the internal audit function has been
rated "Overall Compliance" with the IIA Standards and Code of
Ethics, which represents the highest degree of compliance.
83International Professional Practices Framework.


140
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
Internal control within the NBM also operates through a set of
regulations approved by the governing bodies and ensures that
resources are managed effectively to achieve the NBM’s mission
and strategic objectives.
Framework of Compliance and Ethics
Code of Conduct for National Bank Employees
The NBM Code of Conduct for Employees operates in the National
Bank, which sets out requirements for all employees to adhere
to the highest standards of professional ethics. To this end, the
Code of Conduct addresses the ethics and professional conduct of
National Bank employees, including conﬂict-of-interest behaviour,
conﬁdentiality, security and information management.
Anti-fraud policy
The anti-Fraud Policy of the National Bank promotes fraud risk
awareness and management.
It aims to identify areas at risk of fraud and to implement
appropriate control measures at all levels of the National Bank
that will contribute to the prevention and detection of fraud, the
monitoring
of
fraud
risk
and
the
promotion
of
consistent
organisational conduct. The anti-fraud policy also sets out the
conditions for conducting investigations into the prevention and
examination of suspected fraud.
The implementation of the anti-fraud policy, training of employees
on fraud risk and its management, reporting of suspected frauds –
all establish and develop an anti-fraud culture that deters potential
perpetrators and strengthens the commitment of National Bank
employees to combat fraud. The responsibility of all National Bank
employees has a major contribution to creating an environment
conducive to raising awareness of the risk and consequences of
fraud.
The
governing
bodies
of
the
National
Bank
promote
a
zero-tolerance policy on fraud and corruption.
Measures
to
combat
money
laundering
and
terrorist
ﬁnancing
Considering the strategic objective of ensuring a safe, stable, and
resilient ﬁnancial system, the National Bank of Moldova is
committed to implementing international standards in the ﬁeld of
preventing
and
combating
money
laundering
and
terrorist
ﬁnancing, including in its own ﬁeld of activity. In this regard, the
authority has developed and approved internal rules, aligned with
international standards, aimed at avoiding the involvement of the
central bank in operations or activities of money laundering,
terrorist ﬁnancing or circumvention of international restrictive
measures (hereinafter "ML/TF and CIM"). Taking as a key element
the principle of a risk-based approach, the National Bank’s
internal regulatory framework for preventing and combating
money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing establishes both rules
related to the governance of the authority in the ﬁeld of reference,


Chapter 9. Institutional and organizational framework
141
as well as internal control measures and management of ML/TF
and CIM risks associated with the operations carried out.
Thus, during 2022, constant efforts were made to strengthen the
central bank’s compliance with anti-money laundering and
prevention requirements,
which included activities such as:
identiﬁcation of ML/TF and CIM high-risk operations, including
taking into account the results of the national risk assessment in
the reference area;
continuous application of the real-time
payment
screening
service,
which
aims
to
ensure
the
implementation
of
international
restrictive
measures
with
reference to customer payments; establishment of control and
management measures for identiﬁed risks; continuous exchange
of information between the structural sub-units of the National
Bank on identiﬁed risks, changes in the level of risk of certain
operations, measures necessary for the effective management of
these risks, etc. Similarly, improvements have been made to the
internal reporting process on ML/TF and CIM risks, both to the
Executive Board member coordinating the activity and to the
reporting process to the Executive Board.
Also, in the context of efforts to strengthen the National Bank’s
compliance capacity in the area of preventing and combating
money
laundering,
staff
responsible
for
implementing
the
requirements
of
the
relevant
regulatory
framework
have
participated in several training programmes, including technical
assistance missions.
Information security policy
The information security policy of the National Bank establishes
the general organizational context to ensure the achievement of
the objectives regarding the provision of information security and
cyber security within the NBM. It also sets out requirements for
ensuring the secure management of information within the NBM
and minimizing the impact that may arise as a result of security
incidents.
The members of the Supervisory Board and the members of the
Executive Board, as well as any employee of the National Bank, are
obliged to ensure the security of the information in their possession
and to which they have access, whatever its form. At the same
time, the responsibilities in the process of ensuring information
security are clearly established and divided, in the part related to
information management, monitoring the use and distribution of
information, reporting information security incidents, as well as
how to investigate them.
In the same context, in order to ensure information security within
the NBM, in accordance with the Information Security Policy, the
Information Security Ofﬁcer of the NBM is appointed.
Management of personal data
The National Bank processes personal data in accordance with
the Law No. 133/2011 on personal data protection. The National
Bank has effective policies, procedures, and information systems
to ensure the protection of personal data. Within the National
Bank there is a designated Personal Data Protection Ofﬁcer who
is responsible for informing and advising the employees of the
National Bank, EB members in the processing of personal data,


142
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
monitoring compliance with the regulations in force, carrying out
awareness-raising and training actions for employees involved in
personal data processing operations, cooperating with the National
Centre for Personal Data Protection.
Public
access
to
normative
acts,
statistics
and
publications of the NBM
The public access to the National Bank’s normative acts, statistics
and publications ensures a high degree of transparency while
respecting the independence and conﬁdentiality of certain aspects
related to the performance of the National Bank’s tasks. Publishing
various data and analyses related to the tasks of the National Bank,
as well as promoting an open relationship with target groups are
issues of major importance in the external communication of the
bank.
The National Bank pays particular attention to the reverse link
in the communication scheme. Thus, through the regular and
frequent transmission of messages, the National Bank provides
rigorous explanations for its activity and the functioning of the
banking sector. The functioning of the interactive database on the
website of the National Bank provides public access to information,
internal data and analysis and aims to provide accurate, current,
clear, and transparent information for creating a public opinion on
the decisions of the National Bank, but also for conducting various
studies and research.


Chapter 9. Institutional and organizational framework
143
9.2
Development perspectives
The National Bank’s development prospects are correlated with
the implementation of the strategic objectives and are reﬂected in
the new Strategic Plan of the National Bank "NBM 2025". Following
an extensive analysis of the internal and external environment
from different perspectives, in cooperation with all the Bank’s
subdivisions, new strategic, operational, complementary, and
cross-cutting objectives to be implemented in the next 3 (three)
years were approved at the beginning of 2023.
During 2023, the National Bank will focus its efforts on continuing
activities related to ongoing projects and reforms, while initiating
activities related to new projects resulting from the objectives
outlined in the new strategy. Developments in the NBM’s major
ongoing projects are reﬂected in the table A.21.
The strategic objectives resulting from the core business areas, as
well as from the governance and support areas, which underpin
the projects of the NBM strategy refer to:
1. Strengthening
the
monetary
policy
framework
and
optimising the implementation of monetary policy
2. Strengthening
the
ﬁnancial
stability
function
and
macro-prudential supervision
3. Strengthening the supervisory function at ﬁnancial-banking
and non-banking sector level
4. Continued development of ﬁnancial market infrastructures
and contributing to increasing cashless payments
5. Enhancing
the
quality
of
the
national
currency
and
strengthening the national cash insurance function
6. Strengthening institutional resilience and agility
7. Developing and exploiting human capital
The NBM will also continue, and, at the same time, initiate activities
related to the ﬁve (5) cross-cutting objectives set out in the new
Strategic Plan. These relate to:
1. Alignment with EU standards and international cooperation
2. Research, innovation and digitisation
3. Financial education and inclusion
4. Social and environmental responsibility
5. Diversity, equity and organisational inclusion
In 2023, one of the National Bank’s main priorities will be to take
over the supervision of the non-bank ﬁnancial sector on a
sustainable basis. Therefore, reforming and consolidating internal
business processes in order to integrate the new tasks of
licensing, authorization/ issuance of state registration, regulation,
and supervision of the activity of entities in the non-banking
ﬁnancial sector will be an important development objective of the
National Bank of Moldova, considering the entry into force of the
respective legal provisions from 1 July 2023.


144
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
9.3
The strategic plan of the National
Bank of Moldova
During 2022,
the National Bank of Moldova continued the
implementation of the ﬁve strategic objectives, including 25
(twenty-ﬁve) operational/ complementary/ cross-cutting objectives
from the previous strategic planning cycle.
The fundamental elements of the Strategic Plan relate to mission,
vision, values, and strategic objectives.
Mission of the National Bank of Moldova
The fundamental objective of the National Bank is to ensure and
maintain price stability.
Without prejudice to its fundamental
objective, the National Bank promotes and maintains a ﬁnancial
system based on market principles and supports the economic
development of the country.
Vision of the National Bank of Moldova
The National Bank of Moldova is an initiative-taking, efﬁcient, and
credible institution guided by good governance practices.
Values of the National Bank of Moldova
Credibility – policies, reforms, communication and attitudes that
inspire conﬁdence and security in the public and markets.
Responsibility – ongoing concern about the economic and social
impact of the work done and decisions made.
Reliability
–
human
resources,
internal
procedures
and
information technology that maintain the safety and diligence of
the functioning of the institution.
Professionalism
–
competence,
integrity,
diligence,
decision-making, continuous improvement.
Cooperation – teamwork to achieve the common goals of the
bank.
The strategic objectives derive from the core business areas and
the last objective relates to the support and governance:
1. To improve the monetary policy framework and optimize the
implementation of monetary policy.
2. To
strengthen
the
function
of
ﬁnancial
stability
and
macroprudential management.
3. To strengthen the banking sector and implement risk-based
supervision.
4. To enhance the quality of the national currency and the level
of use of cashless payments.
5. To strengthen institutional capacity and efﬁciency.
During 2022, progress was made in implementing reforms in ﬁve
key areas:
monetary policy and ﬁnancial markets, ﬁnancial
stability,
banking supervision,
payment systems and cash
operations, governance, and support. The achievements relate to
the 25 (twenty-ﬁve) operational/ complementary/ cross-cutting
objectives continued towards implementation, the most important
of which were included in the table below. Also, the table A.21
"Evolution of NBM major projects during 2022" contains the most
important achievements related to NBM projects, which derive
from the strategic objectives.

Interbank Payment System (AIPS) – RTGS (Real-time Gross Settlement) and DNS
(Designated-time Net Settlement) components, accompanied by the update of
the relevant regulatory framework;
✓Launching of the project related to the implementation of instant payments in
the Republic of Moldova. Conducting the "Analysis and design" phase of the
instant payments IT solution;
✓Launching of the project on ensuring the necessary prerequisites for the banking
sector to join the Single Euro Payments Area (SEPA). Approve the project concept
and determine the best option for its implementation;
✓Organising of the conference "Accession of the Republic of Moldova to SEPA –
achievements and prospects".
Strengthening
institutional
capacity and
effectiveness
✓Implementing of the Twinning support project "Strengthening supervision,
corporate governance and risk management in the ﬁnancial sector", funded
by the European Union, with the support of partner authorities from Romania,
the Kingdom of the Netherlands and Lithuania. Carrying out technical assistance
missions on the 5 components of the project;
✓Strengthening the organisational structure in the area of ﬁnancial stability and
create organisational structures related to the compliance function and the
Fintech area;
✓Strengthening the managerial and leadership skills of 2nd level managers by
conducting, with internal resources, trainings based on the deliverables obtained
as a result of the project "Management and Leadership skills program for NBM
Middle Management", carried out during 2021, in collaboration with the Good
Governance Fund and the UK Government;
✓Strengthening internal training capacities and conducting some 38 training
sessions during the year in the Bank’s core business, support and governance
areas, including 10 workshops related to operational risk management;
✓Submitting for coordination to the Executive Board of the NBM of the draft of the
new Code of Ethics and Professional Conduct of the National Bank of Moldova;
✓Continuing the implementation of the operational risk management framework
by assessing 79% of the bank’s total business processes;
✓Strengthening internal capacity following technical assistance from external
development partners in the areas of banking supervision, regulation and
licensing, bank resolution, ﬁnancial stability, anti-money laundering and counter-
terrorist ﬁnancing, human resources management, internal audit, reporting
and statistics, cash operations, payment systems, external relations, ﬁnancial
education, etc.;
✓Continuing actions under the project on digitisation of the NBM archive, including
strengthening the related regulatory framework;
✓Reviewing the concept and architecture of the NBM Remote Reserve Centre;
✓Strengthening the institutional culture, including the civic spirit of NBM employees
by organizing a series of events dedicated to recognising the results of
professional activity, implementing social responsibility initiatives, strengthening
team spirit and collaboration.

9.4
Management and organisation
According to the Strategic Plan of the National Bank of Moldova,
out of the total of 5 strategic objectives, one is related to the
human resources ﬁeld, namely:
Strengthening institutional capacity and efﬁciency, which implies
the streamlining of NBM’s operational activity through institutional
modernisation and the strengthening of internal capacities in
order to achieve the core competencies. One of the operational
objectives deriving from the strategic one is the implementation
of a new integrated human resource management system.
The
key
priorities
in
human
resources
management
and
development in 2022 have been focused speciﬁcally on these
areas, and the measures undertaken are detailed below.
Organisational evolutions
In 2022, there were a number of changes in the organizational
structure of the National Bank of Moldova, meant to contribute
to the achievement of the Bank’s strategic objectives and its
core competencies, but also to increase its efﬁciency by reducing
hierarchical levels.
At the same time, these changes aim to
increase the capacity of the NBM to adapt to changes in the
economic environment by redeﬁning responsibilities, restructuring
activities, and strengthening institutional capacity overall.
Thereby, in 2022, including for the purpose of ensuring an optimal
process for the NBM to take over part of the powers of the NCFM
under Law No. 178/2020, functions related to the core tasks of the
NBM have been developed, as well as competence development
functions in preparation for the implementation of the licensing/
authorisation tasks from 1 July 2023, as well as on the regulation
and supervision of the activity of some entities of the non-banking
ﬁnancial market by optimizing specialized subdivisions in the
areas of banking supervision,
regulation and authorization,
banking
resolution,
ﬁnancial
stability,
payment
systems,
anti-money laundering and combating the ﬁnancing of terrorism,
as well as optimizing some subdivisions related to the area of
support and governance in areas such as legal, external relations
and European integration, strategy and organization, human
resources, information security and supervision in information
technologies.
At the same time, a number of management
functions have been adjusted to reﬂect the current needs of the
subdivisions, with no impact on the total number of employees.
In order to optimize internal governance processes on compliance
issues and to ensure an optimal approach to FinTech developments,
2 new directorates have been created in the National Bank of
Moldova:
Fintech Directorate – the basic purpose being to identify,
analyse the opportunity and promote new ﬁnancial technologies


Chapter 9. Institutional and organizational framework
149
in the ﬁeld of ﬁnancial and payment services, within the scope of
the NBM’s core tasks, in a controlled manner, with a view to
increasing the level of accessibility of these services for end
consumers and participants in the ﬁnancial and payments market,
while also identifying potential risks and measures to manage
them. Without prejudice to the mandate of the National Bank, the
ﬁntech directorate will contribute to the promotion of ﬁnancial
technologies in the ﬁeld of ﬁnancial services other than those
mentioned, with a view to maximising the results of achieving the
overall goal.
Compliance Directorate – the basic purpose being to provide
advice on the compliance of processes/ activities carried out within
the NBM with the provisions of the code of ethics and professional
conduct, the anti-fraud policy, other regulatory acts on institutional
integrity, with the provisions of the internal and external regulatory
framework related to the NBM activity, as well as to support the
heads of subdivisions in developing and implementing an effective
internal control system to manage the compliance risk related to
those regulations.
The changes made during 2022 did not substantially inﬂuence the
control area indicator, which remained at the level of 4.9 executors
per manager, the same value being recorded in 2021. Following
the changes made, the ratio of the number of units related to the
core tasks of the NBM to the number of units related to the support
area was also maintained. It should be noted that in 2022 changes
were made in the distribution of posts in the NBM post families, so
that all technical posts were assigned to the post families related
to the support area. Contrasting the change given in both 2022
and 2021 there is a ratio of 51% units related to the core tasks of
the NBM to 49% units related to the support and governance area.

Although well below the general level recorded in the labour
market, in 2022, the voluntary staff turnover was 8.14%, increasing
compared to 2021 when it recorded a level of 7.13%, an upward
trend to which the NBM responded by adjusting the National
Bank’s human resources policy to the changing requirements of the
reference market. In 2022, in order to ensure the competitiveness
of the NBM as an employer, in collaboration with a consultant with
international experience in the ﬁeld of human resources in central
banks, the salary system was adjusted to the reference market, a
necessity imposed by the major impact on the labour market of the
COVID-19 pandemic, the energy crisis generated by the security
situation in the region, labour migration, etc. Similarly, in 2022,
amendments to the National Bank’s internal acts were approved,
which require the implementation of remote work as a work regime
applied on a continuous basis, at the request of employees, for 20%
of weekly working time. Also, during 2022, several actions have
been undertaken related to the development of organisational
culture and improvement of the working environment.
Analysing the voluntary turnover by hierarchical levels, for
managerial functions in 2022 it decreased compared to 2021 and

Professional growth
Professional development
During 2022, in order to ensure the development of skills and the
growth of the NBM’s human capital, about 71.3% of the effective
employees beneﬁted from various forms of training. Considering
the restrictions imposed by the post-pandemic situation in the
ﬁrst part of the year, as well as the changing approach of training
providers, the training of employees was conducted mainly online,
with training events being selected according to individual
professional development needs, drawing on training programs
provided by development partners, as well as ensuring internal
knowledge transfer.
In 2022, the NBM continued to develop management skills as a
result of the Project implemented with the support of the UK
Government and the Good Governance Fund. Using the project
deliverables,
NBM trainers delivered the programme to 35
managers over a 12-week period in sessions totalling 60 hours.
Training areas included:
management and leadership, team
management, emotional intelligence, change management and
strategic thinking.

Work environment and organisational culture
In the context of the COVID-19 pandemic, the National Bank of
Moldova applied remote working for the ﬁrst time,
which
contributed to the continuity of the functioning of the National
Bank of Moldova, avoiding loss of activity in the context of the
need to ensure the occupational health security of employees.
Following the positive experience gained during the pandemic
period, based on the changes in the legal framework, in 2022 the
National Bank of Moldova implemented internal regulations on the
application of remote work for the NBM employees, whose duties
can be performed without physical presence at the NBM premises
by using information and communication technology means. This
tool is intended to be used both to ensure the continuity of the
NBM’s activity in a situation of increased risk and as a beneﬁt
offered by the employer for a more ﬂexible, modern, and dynamic
working environment, adapted to new developments in the labour
market.
In addition, activities and actions were organised during the year
to help strengthen the organisational culture and maintain a
sense of belonging to the institution.
Thus, the NBM through
its activities promoted such values as social responsibility, care for
the environment and a healthy way of life, all made possible by
the development of a strong team spirit. In this context, with the
participation of several NBM employees, we organized initiatives to
provide material aid and transport to refugees and visit two of their
destinations. Additionally, during 2022, two Charity Fairs were also
organized, where participants showed creativity and generosity
with very active involvement. The proceeds raised were directed
towards colleagues suffering from incurable diseases. At the same
time, NBM employees and their family members participated in
the tree planting campaign, contributing to the greening of the
land.
Another nice initiative was the participation in the National Blood
Donation Campaign. NBM employees felt part of the mission to
save lives.
Among the initiatives aimed at promoting healthy lifestyle and
social
responsibility
were
participation
in
the
Chis, in˘au
International Marathon for charity and the Charity Football
Championship, where participants showed a lot of interest in
getting involved and belonging to the community.
On another note, a real professional development achievement
was the event to strengthen organisational values and culture. All
NBM employees jointly participated in trainings by practicing in
an interactive and diverse way the NBM values and how to apply
them in their daily activities.

candidates for participation in competitions for vacancies in the
NBM. Considering the speciﬁc nature of the Bank’s vacancies,
which are characterised by higher educational requirements,
complexity and expertise, as well as the shortage of qualiﬁed

Thus, several

young people concerned with their professional development, got

organisational structure of the subdivision. It should be noted
that the National Bank of Moldova ensures the integration and
professional development of new employees through induction
and orientation programs, internal and external trainings, on-the-
job trainings with the support of colleagues.

Chapter 9. Institutional and organizational framework
153
9.6
Internal audit
The overall purpose of IAD’s (Internal Audit Department) activity
is to provide assurance, independent, and objective advisory
services, designed to add value and contribute to the improvement
of the NBM’s activities. The mission of the internal audit is to
enhance and protect the values of the NBM by providing objective
assurance based on risk, advice, and perspective approach. The
internal audit assists the NBM in achieving its objectives through a
systematic and disciplined approach to assessing and improving
the effectiveness of governance, risk management and control
processes.
The IAD is guided in its work by the mandatory elements of the
International
Professional
Practices
Framework
(IPPF)83,
developed by the Institute of Internal Auditors (IIA), including the
Core Principles of the Professional Practice of Internal Auditing,
the Code of Ethics of the Institute of Internal Auditors (hereafter
Code of Ethics), the International Standards for the Professional
Practice of Internal Auditing and the Deﬁnition of Internal Auditing
(hereafter IIA Standards). The IAD operates on the basis of the
regulatory framework84, aligned with the Supplemental Guidance
"Model Internal Audit Activity Charter" Institute of Internal
Auditors (IIA)85. Under the Qualiﬁcation Standards, the quality
assurance and improvement programme are designed to provide
an assessment of the compliance of internal audit work with the
IIA Standards, as well as an assessment of the application of the
Code of Ethics. Compliance with those Standards and the Code of
Ethics has been conﬁrmed by 17 March 2021, the Report on the
External Quality Assessment of Internal Audit.
Based on best
practices in the ﬁeld, the independence of internal audit is
achieved through functional subordination to the NBM Supervisory
Board/ Audit Committee and administrative subordination to the
Governor of the NBM.
During 2022, the internal audit function self-assessment process
was carried out as part of the quality assurance and improvement
programme.
The period under review was 2021-2022.
The
completion of the self-assessment also enables the preparation of
the information support for the external quality assessment of the
internal audit function in accordance with the International
Standards for the Professional Practice of Internal Auditing, which
is
planned
to
be
carried
out
in
2025.
Based
on
the
self-assessment performed, the degree of compliance of the
internal audit activity with the IPPF83 and the Code of Ethics is
"Overall Compliance", which means that the relevant structures,
policies and procedures of the activity, and the processes by
which they are applied, meet the requirements of the individual
standard or elements of the Code of Ethics in all material aspects.
84Regulation on the functioning of the Internal Audit Department of the National
Bank of Moldova, approved by the Decision of the Supervisory Board of the NBM
No. 32 of 12.12.2019.
85Supplemental Guidance "Model Internal Audit Activity Charter" Institute of
Internal Auditors, March 2017.


154
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
During 2022, actions continued on the implementation of the
recommendations put forward by the external assessor86 set out
in the Plan87 respectively.
In 2022, the audit work was conducted in accordance with the
approved audit plan, which was developed using the risk-based
approach, and the priorities set.
In its work, the IAD did not
encounter any limitations in providing its independent opinion.
The Director of the IAD regularly reported the results of the audit
work to the NBM’s governing bodies: Audit Committee, Executive
Board and Supervisory Board.
The
annual
internal
audit
plan
for
2022
has
been
80%
completed88. IAD conducted assurance missions, endorsements
of ﬁnancial statements and execution of the NBM budget, carried
out veriﬁcation and monitoring activities in high-risk areas.
Several types of audits were conducted – ﬁnancial/ compliance/
operational (59%), information technology (25%), integrated (8%)
and monitoring (8%).
Audit activities covered 24%89 of the
processes assessed by the IAD as medium residual risk and 6% as
low risk. In 2022, IAD ofﬁcials participated in a monitoring role in
eight major NBM projects.
9.7
Information technology
In the ﬁeld of information and communication technology (ICT),
2022 was a year of signiﬁcant achievements for the National
Bank of Moldova (NBM), with important actions being taken to
implement major projects and strengthen ICT infrastructure and
capabilities.
During the reporting period, the Automated Interbank Payment
System was successfully upgraded and put into operation.
Signiﬁcant progress was made on key projects to streamline the
process of monitoring shareholder transparency and remote
analysis in the area of anti-money laundering and combating the
ﬁnancing of terrorism, and to implement a new instant payment
system. The description of these projects is presented in table
A.21 "Evolution of major projects of the NBM during 2022".
On the technological dimension, several projects and initiatives
related to infrastructure upgrades and capacity expansions have
been carried out. This has ensured, from an ICT perspective, the
alignment of capacities with the operational and strategic
development needs of the NBM. In this respect, it is worth
mentioning the completion of the modernisation, extensive
testing, and commissioning of a new technological infrastructure

ﬁeld).

as
part
of

improvements in terms of performance, resilience and security.
Signiﬁcant efforts have also been made to improve cyber

against attacks in cyberspace by identifying and blocking
unauthorised web trafﬁc and other types of sophisticated attacks.
Of note are the achievements related to the expansion of
processing capabilities related to the workplace virtualisation
infrastructure and data storage infrastructure.
In addition, in order to be an efﬁcient institution, the NBM has
initiated activities to reorganize the ICT function, by expanding
the tasks and responsibilities on how to identify, analyse and
implement technological innovations in the ﬁnancial sector. These
initiatives will enable the NBM to understand new trends in
emerging technologies and adopt innovative solutions to meet
new challenges.
In conclusion,
the implementation of modern technological
solutions is essential to better align ICT capabilities with the
strategic and operational needs of the NBM, so as to ensure
security, efﬁciency and transparency of the activities conducted.
The NBM will continue to invest in innovative solutions and
constantly improve its technological infrastructure to successfully
fulﬁl its role as a credible ﬁnancial authority.


156
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
Chapter 10
Research activity
A central bank’s research serves as the conceptual and empirical
basis for formulating proposals for central bank policies and
decisions. The role of research is to ensure that the central bank
has at its ﬁngertips the information needed to effectively manage
the challenges arising in its areas of activity.
To this end the
Department of Economic and Applied Research produces original
research, analysis and other analytical material on topics related
to the tasks and functions of the National Bank and carries out the
coordination of research activity within the National Bank.
The research activity in the National Bank is coordinated between
two working groups: economic research and applied research,
each focusing on different topical areas. In 2022, research activity
was
focused
on
theoretical
and
practical
support
to
the
subdivisions of the National Bank in areas relevant to its tasks,
functions and policies, which included work on ﬁnancial market
infrastructures, development of ﬁnancial technologies and their
supervision, "green" economy, economic consequences of the
geopolitical situation in the region. Efforts were also invested in
improving the analytical process in the area of monetary policy.
The economic structural changes at global, regional and national
level caused by the effects of the health and energy crisis on
the economy and the ﬁnancial sector require a review of central
bank policies and instruments. In this context, the role of analysis,
studies and research is increasing in order to create theoretical
and practical foundations adapted to the new conditions.
In order to achieve the expected level of efﬁciency in terms of
academic excellence and practical usefulness, but also to bring
the National Bank’s scientiﬁc research to a level of international
recognition by ensuring quality at the level of high standards
of research excellence, it is necessary to continuously invest in
staff development in order to enhance its expertise, knowledge of
current situations and developments in ﬁnancial markets. Thus,
during 2022 the Department of Economic and Applied Research
has continuously monitored the evolution of the national and
international economic environment in order to have the capacity
to deliver an informed opinion and to be ready to participate in the
continuous development of the analytical and research tools that
underpin policy decisions.
The Department of Economic and Applied Research actively
participates in thematic working groups organised at the initiative
of other subdivisions or third-party organisations, and supports
research cooperation within and outside the Bank, as information
exchange, discussion and debate are vital to the research process.


157
Chapter 11
External communication
The National Bank of Moldova focuses its external communication
on the criteria set out in its communication strategy, fulﬁlling its
mission of promoting and maintaining a ﬁnancial system based on
market
principles
and
supporting
the
country’s
economic
development. Thus, the central bank reiterates its openness to
the public,
opting for an explicit,
optimal and transparent
character of its communication, addressed to various target
groups.
Therewith, the effects of the pandemic felt in society at the
beginning of the year, and subsequently the outbreak of war
in the neighbouring country, led to changes in the institution’s
communication process. It has been adapted to the new conditions
through a mixed approach – combining physical presence and
online participation, while keeping the principles of accessibility,
clarity, timeliness, predictability and transparency at its core. The
communication continued to focus on achieving the main goal:
anchoring inﬂation expectations close to the National Bank’s target
and increasing the credibility of the ﬁnancial-banking sector in the
Republic of Moldova.
International sanctions and the migrant crisis – shocks triggered
by the outbreak of war in Ukraine – have forced the central bank to
adopt active communication aimed at ﬁnancial and banking sector
stability.
For a wider information coverage, the NBM used several channels
to inform the general public. Traditionally, the ofﬁcial website of
the NBM has been used as the ﬁrst source of information. During
2022, on the https://www.bnm.md/ page were published and
updated in Romanian, English and Russian: 1,715 information
materials
(press
releases,
media
interviews,
publications,
analytical materials, normative acts, drafts proposed for public
consultation, announcements, explanatory articles) and 2,769
statistical reports.
The relationship with the media was manifested through extensive
proactive and reactive collaboration.
Thereby, the NBM representatives participated in a number of
public media events of general interest including: the conference
"Digital Transformation and Financial Inclusion", the economic
forum Moldova Business Week which was held under the theme
"Moldova – business relocation destination and solution for the
regional supply chain".
In 2022, the NBM organized the ﬁrst international conference
dedicated to the prospect of Moldova’s accession to the Single Euro
Payments Area, which was widely reported in the press through


158
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
press releases, interviews, participation in broadcasts. Similarly,
the National Bank organised a public event entitled: "Discover
the ﬁeld of banking supervision in the NBM" and "Twinning project
launch event".
In order to promote the principle of fairness in relations with the
media, the National Bank has implemented, since 2018, a new
communication format – the cumulative interview – with questions
from representatives of various media institutions. In this context,
in 2022, the NBM maintained the format – the cumulative
interview with the Governor of the NBM – which includes answers
to questions taken from media outlets in the Republic of Moldova.
In the same vein, the National Bank has shown openness in working
with the media, responding to over 474 requests for information.
Communication on social media
Analysing the usefulness of the website and the messages received
via social networks, we note the increase in the number of users
and visits to the NBM website and their interest in topics such as:
monetary policy, banking supervision, regulations developed by
the NBM, events dedicated to ﬁnancial education (Table A.22).
The post-pandemic period has been characterised by extensive
online communication. In this respect, increased attention was
paid to improving the quality of posts on social media proﬁles
(Table A.23).
In this way, the National Bank achieves clear and uncompromising
external communication, structured on target groups and based
on modern institutional communication principles. Through its
communication, the National Bank shows its openness to the
public in order to inform them transparently and effectively in the
conduct of its central bank activity.


159
Chapter 12
Financial education
The National Bank continues to support the citizens in the
implementation
of
its
ﬁnancial
education
approach
as
a
component of ﬁnancial inclusion and a response to society’s need
to deepen ﬁnancial literacy.
This objective undertaken by the
central bank, together with other authorities and civil society, is
becoming increasingly important in the Republic of Moldova,
against the background of the growing interest in the subject
globally.
Both young and older people, regardless of their education and
activity, are keen to broaden their knowledge of saving, credit,
non-cash payments, investing and other ﬁnancial concepts. It
is a prerequisite for the modern consumer who in everyday life
makes ﬁnancial decisions and seeks to adapt to the ever-changing
ﬁnancial environment.
Thereby, in order to support citizens, the National Bank of
Moldova, as the national coordinator of ﬁnancial education,
considers important the conﬂuence of efforts at the level of
authorities and non-governmental organizations, by areas of
competence.
In this context, together with other authorities,
members
of
the
Inter-Institutional
Working
Group
for
the
elaboration of the concept and draft of the National Financial
Education Strategy, the central bank has identiﬁed ﬁnancial
education issues and problems related to consumer protection
and ﬁnancial inclusion in the working group meetings held.
In the same line, in order to determine the level of ﬁnancial
education in society, the National Bank conducted a sociological
survey among the general public, in order to create a study base
necessary for the development of the National Financial Education
Strategy. The results of the survey are also a benchmark in the
approach to the updated ﬁnancial education process currently
underway.
Therewith, a Partnership Agreement was concluded between the
National Bank and the Ministry of Finance in order to guide and
strengthen cooperation in the ﬁeld of ﬁnancial education. The
agreement
was
signed
during
the
opening
event
of
the
International Financial Education Week 2022 (the National Bank of
Moldova is among the ﬁrst participants who joined this global
project in 2012, and so far more than 170 countries have joined
the project).
Throughout the year, the National Bank continued its campaign to
promote cashless payments and the advisable behaviour of card
users to avoid falling prey to fraud. Central bank experts addressed
this topic at ﬁnancial education events for the public of different
ages. Bank representatives also visited several localities (Chisinau,


160
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
Edinet, Rezina, Balti, Causeni, Cahul) to explain to citizens’ various
aspects of cashless payments, their advantages and safe ways of
use.
As part of the same effort, the National Bank organised a training
workshop for representatives of the Ministry of Internal Affairs on
"Safe Use of Payment Cards, Application of All Necessary Measures
to Minimise Fraud and Deter Fraud Attempts with Payment Cards".
In this respect, the central bank has published and issued 10,000
leaﬂets, which will be distributed by the Ministry of Interior in all
districts through territorial police inspectorates.
The institutional experience of addressing the public online during
the pandemic period has highlighted the possibility of broad public
access to ﬁnancial education events organised by the central bank.
Thus, this mode of external communication was also constructive
in 2022, providing a beneﬁcial complement to physically attended
events.
Therewith, the Doors Open Day marked on the date of the founding
of the National Bank of Moldova – June 4 – is an activity that, by
deﬁnition, expresses the opportunity for the public to visit the
central bank. During this and other educational events, more than
700 visitors crossed the threshold of the National Bank during
2022.
In conducting ﬁnancial education activities, the National Bank
focuses on target groups, tailoring its messages according to
informational and thematic needs.
Thus, it is worth mentioning the events held for children, young
people and other target groups, including projects such as "In the
World of B˘anut,", "Financial Education Camp", "The Money Road".
Therewith, "the Generation I" project is aimed at all citizens,
regardless of age or profession, and traditionally offers the
audience the opportunity to learn about approaches and practices
presented by international experts. In 2022, the format chosen
was the round table "We Choose Financial Independence", which
is also the central message of the project.
In order to achieve the proposed objective more effectively, the
institution uses tools and channels to promote ﬁnancial education.
The ﬁrst of these include visits to the central bank, organised on
the occasion of certain historical dates or other dates of general
interest, conferences, round tables, workshops, press releases,
radio and television broadcasts, live broadcasts, etc.
A new practice that the National Bank tends to capitalise on is
the development of multimedia products on the subject of ﬁnance.
The public around the world, including in the Republic of Moldova,
is more receptive to this kind of information. Thus, even more
complex stories are more easily understood by the audience when
they are reﬂected through multimedia products.
Also in 2022, the National Bank organised for the ﬁrst time a
ﬁnancial education event at one of the high schools in the capital,
in the form of debates among high school students on the topic
"Banknotes and Coins versus E-currency". The event took place
on the occasion of the 29th anniversary of the introduction of the
Moldovan leu and was accessible to the public via live broadcast.
Beforehand, experts from the National Bank visited the high school


Chapter 12. Financial education
161
to explain the most important aspects of this topic to the students
and help them to conduct thorough debates.
In terms of channels to promote ﬁnancial education, the central
bank opts to connect/ interact with the public through its
institutional website, social media and media proﬁles.
Through its ﬁnancial education projects, the National Bank aims to
provide citizens with the educational support needed to make
sound and informed ﬁnancial decisions.
They start from the
simplest daily needs such as managing own income wisely,
prioritising expenses, avoiding ﬁnancial risks, as well as choosing
to save, take out a loan or invest.
The National Bank is optimising its educational resources to
support the deepening of ﬁnancial literacy in society as an
essential part of achieving ﬁnancial inclusion.

The fundamental objective of the NBM is to ensure and
maintain
price
stability.
Making
proﬁts
is
not
an
objective, but guaranteeing price stability, given that the
market is inﬂuenced by internal and external factors, also
implies taking on possible losses, precisely in order to
bring balance to the market, which beneﬁts both the
business sector and the population.
13.1
Key indicators
Consolidated assets
The Bank’s consolidated assets continued their upward trend, with
the most pronounced pace in the last 5 years, and increased by
MDL 16,419 million (+18.56%). Monetary policy decisions to
increase the base rate and the reserve requirement ratio, ﬁnancial
assistance from Moldova’s development partners, which reached a
new historical high, and the depreciation of the national currency
against the USD during 2022 are some of the main factors behind
the increase in asset levels.

gold,

at
the

and

securities
in
foreign
currency.
Foreign exchange assets represent 86% of total consolidated
