13.2
General
considerations
in
ﬁnancial analysis
The basic tasks of the National Bank are laid down in the
Law on the National Bank of Moldova. In accordance with
the legal provisions, the operations of the National Bank
of Moldova are carried out for the purpose of promoting
and
supporting
the
monetary
and
foreign
exchange
policy in the state, ensuring the stability of the banking
system,
issuing
the
national
currency,
managing
the
ofﬁcial reserve assets of the state, providing payment
services and issuing electronic money, etc.
Given that the activities and operations of the National
Bank
of
Moldova
are
carried
out
in
support
of
the
implementation of its policies and the execution of its
core tasks, the ﬁnancial position and the ﬁnancial result
achieved should be analysed together with the actions
taken in the implementation of its policies and the IFRS
accounting treatment applied for the recognition and
measurement of these activities and policies.
Monetary policy implementation
The standing facilities granted to banks by the NBM are aimed
at sterilising and, respectively, providing very short-term liquidity
(with overnight maturity) and reducing ﬂuctuations in short-term
interest rates on the interbank money market, through the
corridor established by the NBM on interest rates related to the
two facilities.
Licensed banks can access on their own initiative the two
permanent facilities offered by the NBM:
Overnight
deposits
(with
an
overnight
maturity)
with
a
predetermined interest rate (the interest rate on the deposit
facility is usually the overnight interest rate threshold on the
interbank money market);


Chapter 13. Analysis of the situation and ﬁnancial position of the NBM at individual and consolidated level for
2022
165
Overnight deposits are bonds of the Bank, in the balance sheet
they are reﬂected under Banks’ liquid assets and are valued at
amortised cost.
The NBM grants overnight loans (with overnight maturity) to
cover outstanding overdrafts at the end of the operational day
 
 
and to supplement liquidity. These loans are secured by pledged
securities held by the NBM and have a predetermined interest rate
(which generally serves as a ceiling for the overnight interest rate
on the interbank money market).
 
 
Overnight loans are assets of the Bank and are valued at
amortised cost less allowances for expected credit losses. They
are reﬂected in the Bank’s balance sheet under Loans granted
to banks and other persons.
Money market operations (open market operations) are the
most important monetary policy instrument of the NBM. They
are carried out at the initiative of the NBM, with the function of
managing liquidity conditions on the money market and inﬂuencing
short-term interest rates on the interbank money market. Money
market operations may be conducted through pre-announced
tenders or direct negotiations. According to the regulations in
force, the main categories of money market operations available
to the NBM are:
Reverse repo/ repo operations – reversible liquidity-providing/
liquidity-absorbing
operations
where
the
NBM
buys/
sells
government securities from banks/ banks with a commitment by
the banks/ NBMs to repurchase the respective SS at a later date
and at a price set at the time of the transaction.
Securities purchased (repo) with a simultaneous commitment
to sell on a given date are recognized on the settlement date, but
considering the need for mandatory resale are not recognized in
the Bank’s portfolio of government securities but are recognized
as Loans to banks and other persons.
 
 
 
The difference between the sale price and the repurchase price
represents interest expenditure and the difference between the
purchase price and the resale price represents interest income
calculated using the effective interest rate method.
Issuance of National Bank of Moldova certiﬁcates (NBC)
– liquidity-absorbing operations where the NBM sells negotiable
debt instruments of the NBM to banks. Given the current structural
excess of liquidity, the NBM conducts weekly auctions of NBCs
with a 14-day maturity and the announcement of the maximum
interest rate equivalent to the NBM base rate.
 
 
Certiﬁcates issued by the National Bank are monetary
obligations
of
the
Bank
issued
in
the
form
of
discounted
securities
and
are
reﬂected
in
the
balance
sheet
on
the
settlement date under Certiﬁcates issued by the NBM. The
certiﬁcates are valued at amortised cost using the effective
interest rate method, with the amortisation of the discount
reﬂected under Interest expenditure on securities and repo
operations.
Deposit attraction – liquidity-absorbing operations in which the
NBM attracts deposits from banks with a predetermined maturity.


166
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
Sales/
purchases
of
SS
–
operations
aimed
at
absorbing/injecting liquidity, whereby the NBM sells/ purchases SS,
the transfer of ownership of which from the seller to the buyer is
carried out through the "delivery-versus-payment mechanism".
Required reserves are banks’ liquid assets, in MDL and in
foreign currency, held in accounts with the NBM. Required
reserves (RRs) are an auxiliary monetary policy instrument aimed
at restricting excess liquidity and, respectively, mitigating its
impact on interbank interest rates.
The main role of foreign
currency required reserves is to prevent excessive foreign
currency credit growth. RRs in MDL are maintained in the "Loro"
accounts at the NBM, being constituted as the average balance of
banks’ liquid assets during the application period.
RRs in
foreign currency are maintained only in US dollars and euros on
NBM "Nostro" accounts opened in foreign banks. According to the
Regulation on the required reserve regime, approved by the DEB
No. 302 of 28 November 2019, in force since 16 January 2020,
during the period of application, part of the established level of
required reserves in USD and EUR is variable – maintained by
banks on average,
and another part is maintained in an
unchanged volume.
The NBM pays interest to banks on their share of required reserves
in excess of 5% of the liabilities on which they are based. RRs in
MDL and in foreign currency are remunerated at separate rates,
applying for reserves in MDL the overnight deposit rate of the NBM,
and for required reserves in foreign currency – a remuneration rate
of 0.01%, set at this level by DEB No. 139 of 7 May 2019.
Required reserves are monetary obligations of the Bank and
are reﬂected in the balance sheet under Banks’ liquid assets.
Required reserves in MDL are presented in the category "Loro"
 
 
accounts.
Required reserves in foreign currency are shown in
separate accounts from Required reserves.
Remuneration of
required reserves (interest calculated and paid) is an expense of
the Bank and is reﬂected under Interest expenditure on
liquid assets.
Foreign exchange interventions
The National Bank uses foreign exchange interventions to achieve
monetary and foreign exchange policy objectives. They can be
carried out both through direct operations (spot purchases or
sales of foreign currency against MDL), which inﬂuence the money
supply,
and
through
adjustment
operations
(foreign
exchange swaps), which are reversible instruments and do not
inﬂuence the money supply in the long term.
The NBM intervenes in the foreign exchange market (managed
ﬂoating exchange rate regime) in order to mitigate imbalances
in the domestic foreign exchange market or to adjust the level of
foreign exchange reserves.
Direct selling interventions in foreign exchange decrease the
level of foreign exchange reserves and generate realised foreign
exchange
differences,
reﬂected
in
the
statement
of
comprehensive
income
under
Revenues/
(losses)
on
transactions
and
foreign
exchange
differences.
The
corresponding impact of the selling intervention is to decrease
the money supply (banks’ holdings of bank deposits) and sterilize


Chapter 13. Analysis of the situation and ﬁnancial position of the NBM at individual and consolidated level for
2022
167
structural liquidity in the system, the effect of sterilization on the
Bank’s performance is consequently to decrease interest costs
on banks’ holdings of bank deposits and securities operations.
Direct
purchasing
interventions
in
foreign
exchange
contribute to increasing the level of foreign exchange reserves.
Purchasing interventions do not generate Realised exchange
rate gains/ (losses), they only generate interest from the
investment.
The corresponding impact of intervention is to
 
 
increase the money supply.
Lending to licensed banks and other entities
The National Bank may grant loans to banks under conditions
established from time to time by the National Bank.
In order
to maintain the stability of the ﬁnancial system, the National
Bank may, at its full discretion and under the conditions set by it,
provide emergency liquidity assistance, secured by eligible assets,
to solvent and viable banks facing temporary liquidity problems.
Liquidity assistance is provided in local currency at an interest
rate determined on the basis of the overnight lending facility rate
plus 2 percentage points for a term of up to 3 months, with the
possibility of extension up to 12 months. Liquidity assistance is
provided on condition that the licensed bank has exhausted all
other sources of liquidity.
Loans in the form of liquidity assistance shall be granted subject
to collateralisation with eligible assets comprising government
securities issued by the Government of the Republic of Moldova;
certiﬁcates of the National Bank; deposits and other accounts with
the National Bank or deposits and other accounts with a bank
accepted by the National Bank, pecuniary claims, excluding claims
on persons afﬁliated to the bank; corporate securities admitted
to trading on the regulated market and/or a multilateral trading
facility (MTF), and other eligible assets. The adjusted value of
assets pledged as collateral must cover 100% of the assistance
provided and the related interest.
The NBM may also grant loans to the Deposit Guarantee Fund
in the banking sector, under the conditions and within the terms
established by the Executive Board of the NBM.
It is forbidden to provide credit or any form of ﬁnancial assistance
to the Government of the Republic of Moldova.
Loans to banks are assets of the Bank and are reﬂected in
the balance sheet under the item Loans to banks and other
persons. Loans are measured at amortised cost less allowances
for expected credit losses estimated in accordance with IFRS 9.
Interest income on loans is shown in Interest income on loans
 
 
and repos and recognised using the effective interest method.
Setting the exchange rate of the Moldovan Leu
The National Bank of Moldova quotes the Moldovan Leu against
foreign currencies. The ofﬁcial exchange rate of the Moldovan Leu
against the US dollar is determined for each working day by the
National Bank on the basis of the information on the volumes of
spot buying/ selling transactions of US dollars against MDL and the
exchange rates applied by licensed banks and the National Bank.


168
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
The ofﬁcial exchange rate of the Moldovan Leu against foreign
currencies other than the US dollar is determined on the basis of
the ofﬁcial exchange rate of the Moldovan Leu against the US dollar
multiplied by the cross rate of the respective currency against the
US dollar established on the international market.
Foreign currency transactions carried out by the National
Bank are recorded in the ﬁnancial statements at the exchange
rate on the day of the transaction and are reassessed daily using
the ofﬁcial Moldovan Leu exchange rate. On the reporting date,
 
 
monetary assets and liabilities denominated in foreign currency
are converted at the ofﬁcial exchange rate of the Moldovan Leu
on the reporting date established and published by the National
Bank. As the NBM has a long currency position, the appreciation
of MDL generates unrealised losses and the depreciation of the
MDL – unrealised gains from exchange rate differences.
International reserves management
The Bank holds and manages the State’s foreign exchange
reserves (also referred to as "international reserves"), conducts
foreign exchange operations using foreign exchange reserve
assets and maintains them at an appropriate level for the conduct
of monetary and foreign exchange policy.
Foreign exchange
reserves are managed for the purpose of direct ﬁnancing of the
balance of payments imbalance, for indirect adjustment of the
balance of payments imbalance through foreign exchange market
interventions in pursuance of the monetary and foreign exchange
policy of the National Bank of Moldova, and for other purposes.
In managing foreign exchange reserves, the Bank ensures a high
degree of security and the necessary level of investment liquidity.
The Bank’s investment policy is prudent, aiming at optimising
proﬁtability as a secondary objective, with safety and liquidity of
reserves being paramount.
As part of the management of foreign exchange reserves, the
Bank invests in safe instruments, also used by other central banks:
placements on correspondent accounts (with other central banks
or international ﬁnancial organisations), term investments and
securities issued by governments and government agencies of the
United States of America, the European Union and other states and
those issued by supranational institutions with implicit government
guarantees.
The management of foreign exchange reserves is carried out
through strategic asset allocation (SAA). Strategic asset allocation
involves a long-term view of foreign exchange asset management,
geared towards achieving the optimal level of return and risk.
Main sources of formation of foreign exchange reserves in
the balance sheet of the National Bank of Moldova:
• Registration of loans and grants in favour of the Ministry of
Finance;
• Procurement interventions of the National Bank of Moldova
on the interbank foreign exchange market;
• Reserve requirements in foreign currency of licensed banks;


Chapter 13. Analysis of the situation and ﬁnancial position of the NBM at individual and consolidated level for
2022
169
• Income from the management of foreign exchange reserves;
• Gains on reassessment of securities held in the investment
portfolio.
Main sources of decrease in foreign exchange reserves in
the Bank’s balance sheet:
• Foreign public debt service payments and Ministry of Finance
payments;
• Selling interventions of the National Bank of Moldova on the
interbank foreign exchange market;
• Outﬂows related to foreign currency required reserves of
licensed banks;
• Expenditure on management of foreign exchange reserves
(negative interest on reserves in EUR);
• Losses on reassessment of securities held in the investment
portfolio.
Foreign
exchange
reserves are shown in the accounting
balance sheet under "Cash and short-term placements",
"Investment
securities",
"Liquid
assets
in
SDRs",
"Monetary
gold",
"Contribution
of
the
Republic
of
Moldova to the IMF quota" and "Other assets". They are
 
 
measured at amortised cost (those held in appropriate business
models) or at fair value through other comprehensive income
(foreign currency investment securities).
Sale/ purchase transactions of foreign exchange stocks are
recognised on the trade date and reﬂected in off-balance sheet
accounts until the settlement date.
Net interest income is calculated using the effective interest
method, including coupon calculations, and amortised premiums
and discounts are included in the statement of comprehensive
income under Interest income on short-term placements
and Interest income on securities.
Negative interest expenditure on foreign exchange reserves in
EUR is shown under Negative interest expenditure on short-
term placements.
Expected credit losses on foreign exchange reserves under the
three-step method (described in the accounting policies in the
Financial Statements) and reversal proceeds are reﬂected in the
statement
of
comprehensive
income
under
Income/
(Expenditure) related to impairment of ﬁnancial assets.
Reassessment of foreign exchange reserves
Foreign exchange reserves are reassessed daily at the National
Bank’s ofﬁcial exchange rate. Subsequent to the formation of the
net result:


170
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
• Unrealised
losses
on
exchange
rate
differences
and
reassessment of precious metals are excluded from proﬁt
available for distribution/ (total loss) and are offset against
the
Reserve
for
unrealised
gains
on
exchange
rate
differences on reassessment of foreign currency stocks and
on reassessment of precious metals. If the balance of these
reserves is insufﬁcient, the loss is covered from the General
Reserve Fund account.
• Unrealised
gains
on
exchange
rate
differences
and
reassessment of precious metals are not included in proﬁt
available for distribution/ (total loss) and are allocated to the
increase in the Reserve for unrealised gains on exchange
rate differences on reassessment of foreign currency stocks
and on reassessment of precious metals.
Differences
in
the
daily
reassessment
represent
unrealised
differences,
which
are
reﬂected
in
the
statement
of
comprehensive income under Income/ (loss) on transactions,
 
 
exchange rate differences and reassessment of precious
metals.
Exchange
rate
differences
from
foreign
exchange
transactions
Realised gains and losses are calculated from foreign currency
transactions by applying the weighted average cost method, on
each business day,
for each currency,
for both sales and
purchases
of
foreign
currency.
When
foreign
exchange
transactions (daily level) do not affect the foreign exchange stock,
realised gains and/or losses are calculated as the difference
between the average cost of sales transactions and the average
cost of purchase transactions.
The weighted average cost of foreign exchange reserves for each
currency at the end of each year is equivalent to the ofﬁcial
exchange rate set by the NBM on 1 January.
Realised
gains
and
losses
are
shown
in
the
statement
of
comprehensive income under Income/ (loss) on transactions,
exchange rate differences and reassessment of precious
metals.
Realised gains and losses are distributable and are
included in proﬁt available for distribution/ (total loss).
Issuance of the national currency
The National Bank of Moldova has the exclusive right to issue
banknotes and coins on the territory of the Republic of Moldova
as means of payment. The NBM puts banknotes and coins into
circulation through the banking system. The objective of the NBM
in the ﬁeld of stability of the national currency circulation is to
promote, following the principles of continuity and credibility, the
policy, which ensures the economy with national currency, in strict
accordance with the real needs of the monetary circulation.
The withdrawal of unsuitable banknotes and coins from circulation
shall be carried out only by the National Bank of Moldova, through
the banking system, for the purpose of exchanging them for
banknotes and coins suitable for circulation or replacing them.


Chapter 13. Analysis of the situation and ﬁnancial position of the NBM at individual and consolidated level for
2022
171
Fees charged by the Bank for the issue of national currency to
licensed banks represent income of the National Bank and shall be
recognized in chapter "Other income" when issues are made.
National currency representing monetary obligations of the
Bank, is recognised at face value when put into circulation and is
shown
in
the
balance
sheet
under
National
currency
in
circulation.
National currency in circulation is valued at
amortised cost.
For balance sheet presentation purposes,
national currency in circulation shall be reduced by the national
currency at the Bank’s premises.
Until put into circulation,
 
 
national currency (banknotes and coins) is recorded off-balance
sheet.
National currency production costs are reﬂected in the
statement of comprehensive income under National currency
production costs as incurred (provision of national currency
production services) and include the costs of banknotes, coins,
divisional coins and commemorative coins.
On
the
occasion
of
the
commemoration
of
events
and
distinguished personalities of national history and literature,
promotion of literary works, national traditions, representation of
architectural monuments of historical value of the Republic of
Moldova, ﬂora and fauna, the National Bank of Moldova has the
right to issue commemorative and jubilee banknotes and coins as
a means of payment and for numismatic purposes (CJBC). The
National Bank of Moldova puts these banknotes and coins into
circulation through licensed banks.
Commemorative
and
jubilee
banknotes
and
coins are
 
 
recognised
as
liabilities
under
National
currency
in
circulation on the date of their issuance with a reﬂection in the
books at face value. Jubilee and commemorative banknotes and
coins are traded at the selling price and the difference between
the selling price and the face value is reﬂected under Other
income.


172
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
Banker and agent of the state
The National Bank acts as banker and agent of the state and its
organs. As a depository, the National Bank receives and disburses
money, keeps accounts, opens deposits and provides any other
ﬁnancial services.
In accordance with the Law on the National Bank and the Law No.
181/2014 on Public Finance and Budgetary-Fiscal Accountability,
the National Bank of Moldova opens and serves the Single Treasury
Account of the Ministry of Finance. The Single Treasury Account is
serviced through the participation of the Ministry of Finance in the
Automated Interbank Payment System.
The
National
Bank
opens
and
services
(makes
payments,
collections, currency conversions) the foreign exchange accounts
of the Ministry of Finance, including those opened for the
implementation of donor-funded projects.
All settlements on the accounts of the Ministry of Finance shall be
made within the limit of the balance of the respective accounts
(Single Treasury Account and foreign exchange accounts). The
National Bank accepts deposits from the Ministry of Finance at
market
conditions,
in
accordance
with
the
National
Bank
regulations.
The sight deposits of the Ministry of Finance bear variable interest,
calculated monthly, based on the average interest rate in the
banking system, available for the last three months, on the interest-
bearing MDL deposits drawn on sight from legal entities.
For
term deposits of the Ministry of Finance, the Bank pays interest
according to the term of the deposit by applying the weighted
average interest rate on government securities placed at the last
auction for that term.
The National Bank of Moldova, as agent of the Ministry of Finance,
administers the primary market for state securities (SS) issued
in book-entry form on the domestic market and is authorized to
organize and conduct auctions for the sale of SS on the basis of
the State Agent Agreement concluded with the Ministry of Finance.
 
 
The accounts of the Ministry of Finance opened with the National
Bank are presented in the section Accounts of the Government
of the Republic of Moldova. These include the state budget
funds held in foreign currency, the Ministry of Finance’s sight
and term deposits, which are reﬂected in the balance sheet at
amortized cost.
 
 
Interest expenditure paid on Government’s liquid assets are
reﬂected in the statement of comprehensive income under
Interest expenditure on liquid assets, and fees received by
the Bank from servicing Ministry of Finance accounts (AIPS fees,
foreign exchange transfers, conversions) are reﬂected under
Other income.
The National Bank shall not grant loans and guarantees in any form
to the State or its bodies, including by purchasing government
securities on the primary market or by granting overnight loans.
The National Bank may purchase on the secondary market
government securities that have been publicly issued only


Chapter 13. Analysis of the situation and ﬁnancial position of the NBM at individual and consolidated level for
2022
173
through open market operations and in connection with the
granting of loans to banks.
The National Bank holds in its portfolio state securities derived from
state guarantees issued in accordance with Law No. 235/2016
on the issuance of state bonds for the execution by the Ministry
of Finance of payment obligations derived from state guarantees
No. 807 of 17 November 2014 and No. 101 of 1 April 2015 and
submitted to the National Bank on 4 October 2016 in the form of
state bonds and state securities issued as a result of the conversion
into state securities of loans contracted by the Ministry of Finance
from the Bank in previous years.
Trading by the Bank on the
secondary market of government bonds derived from government
guarantees is limited to 40% of the volume of state securities
submitted to the Bank by the Ministry of Finance.
State securities as a result of the conversion into government
securities of loans contracted by the Ministry of Finance from the
Bank during the previous years were redeemed by the Government
at maturity and reissued in the same volume as new securities to
be transmitted to the Bank.
A subsidiary of the National Bank, CSD holds in its portfolio
securities issued by the Government of the Republic of Moldova,
purchased for investment purposes and held for the collection of
cash ﬂows from principal and interest, thus at consolidated level
they are included in the consolidated assets of the Bank.
State securities, available in the Bank’s portfolio, may be used in
money market operations for monetary policy purposes.
Securities issued in connection with auctions organised by the
Bank are not reﬂected in the Bank’s accounting records.
The commission received by the Bank from the exercise of its
duties as agent of the State is recorded at the time of the auctions
under Other income.
 
 
All portfolios of state securities held by the Bank and subsidiary
are valued at amortised cost, with the Bank’s intention to collect
contractual cash ﬂows.
Government bonds derived from government guarantees are
issued for terms of up to 25 years with ﬁxed interest rates
(nominal rate of 1.4% and 5.3%), the effective interest rate of
the bond portfolio being 5%, which was the fair value at the date
of initial recognition.
State securities in the portfolio of the National Bank of Moldova
and its subsidiary represent the consolidated assets of the Bank
and are reﬂected under Securities issued by the Government
of the Republic of Moldova.
Interest income on state securities is valued using the effective
interest method (taking into account issuance costs) and is
reﬂected in the statement of comprehensive income under
Interest income on securities.


174
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
Transactions with the IMF
The National Bank acts as the state agent in conducting ﬁnancial
transactions with the IMF and as depository for maintaining IMF
accounts. Membership in the IMF is distributed on a quota basis.
The National Bank, as agent of the State, makes payments on
behalf of the State for the repayment of IMF loans, including those
of the Ministry of Finance.
The National Bank of Moldova as the IMF depository opens and
maintains the IMF accounts in MDL with the National Bank.
According to the Parliament Decision (PD) No. 1107/1997 on the
accession of the Republic of Moldova to the IMF and the IMF Articles
of Agreement, the Bank was empowered to be the agent of the
Republic of Moldova to the IMF and the ﬁscal agent and depositary
of the IMF in the Republic of Moldova. Parliament, pursuant to PD
No. 1107/1997 and Law No. 548/1995 on the NBM, has empowered
the Bank to carry out, on behalf of the Republic of Moldova, all
operations and transactions authorised under the provisions of the
IMF Articles of Agreement and to receive any amount that may be
paid or transferred to the Republic of Moldova in operations and
transactions authorised under the various provisions of the IMF
Articles of Agreement.
Subscriptions to the IMF quota are made by the National Bank
of Moldova under Law No.
548/1995 and special laws, which
mandates it to assume all obligations for making subscriptions to
the IMF as required by the Fund.
The Republic of Moldova’s quota to the IMF is a strategic
investment and is recognized as an asset of the Bank and is
reﬂected in the balance sheet under Republic of Moldova’s
contribution to the IMF quota.
 
 
 
The quota is valued at fair value, with monthly reassessment
on the last working day of the management month at the XDR
exchange rate against the Moldovan Leu calculated by the IMF.
Considering that the Bank is empowered by law to represent
the Republic of Moldova at the IMF, to undertake and carry out
operations with the IMF, to carry out quota subscriptions to the
IMF, to collect funds on behalf of the Republic of Moldova from the
IMF, to designate its representatives to the IMF’s governing body,
and to act as ﬁscal agent and depositary of the IMF, in relation to
the operations of the Republic of Moldova with the IMF. With the
exception of those directly attributable to the Government of the
Republic of Moldova, the Bank represents the principal and records
the assets and liabilities of these operations on its balance sheet.
The National Bank as an agent of the Republic of Moldova may
assume obligations and execute transactions related to the
participation
of
the
Republic
of
Moldova
in
international
organizations, including the IMF.
IMF accounts 1 and 2 held with the Bank represent the Bank’s
bonds, which are recorded under Obligations to the IMF, and
are related to the Republic of Moldova’s quota contribution to the
IMF.


Chapter 13. Analysis of the situation and ﬁnancial position of the NBM at individual and consolidated level for
2022
175
The credits received by the Republic of Moldova under the
agreements with the IMF are reﬂected in the balance sheet
under Obligations to the IMF, only in the part of the volume
received by the National Bank (to supplement foreign exchange
reserves), the part received by the Ministry of Finance to cover
the budget deﬁcit being recorded by the Ministry of Finance.
 
 
Automated Interbank Payments System
The National Bank supervises the payment system in the Republic
of Moldova and promotes the stable and efﬁcient functioning of
the automated interbank payment system.
Automated Interbank Payment System (AIPS) is a payment
system owned and operated by the National Bank of Moldova,
which allows for the automatic and ﬁnal settlement of MDL
payments by AIPS participants on the territory of the Republic of
Moldova in the National Bank’s money.
AIPS consists of the real-time gross settlement system for
processing
urgent
and
large-value
payments
and
the
net
settlement clearing system for processing small-value payments
in MDL.
Payments through AIPS are made by AIPS participants through
payment accounts opened with the National Bank of Moldova.
The AIPS participants are: the NBM, banks, the State Treasury
of the Ministry of Finance, the CSD, the Deposit Guarantee Fund
in the banking system, the Tiraspol Cash and Settlement Centre,
non-bank payment service providers.
The balances of the accounts opened with the NBM of the
AIPS participants are reﬂected in the ﬁnancial statements
under the accounting balance sheet headings: "Banks’ liquid
assets", "Government of the Republic of Moldova’s cash
holdings" and "Other clients’ cash holdings". Fees charged
 
 
 
by the NBM when conducting operations within the AIPS are
recognised under Other income.
Single Central Securities Depository
The National Bank of Moldova holds 480 shares with a nominal
value of MDL 50 thousand, which represents 98.36% of the capital
of the joint-stock company "Single Central Securities Depository"
(hereinafter CSD or subsidiary), an entity founded and registered
on 4 April 2018. The activity of the CSD is regulated by Law No.
234/2016 on the Single Central Securities Depository.
CSD operates as of 31 July 2018 the Single Central Securities
Depository System, which is a securities settlement system that
ensures the provision by the Central Securities Depository of
securities registration, centralized administration, settlement, as
appropriate, ancillary, corporate services.
The CSD system is based on modern straight-through-processing
technology
connected
to
AIPS
and
provides
an
efﬁcient
infrastructure and services for securities settlement and securities
registration.


176
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
The CSD registers ﬁnancial instruments issued in the Republic
of Moldova and allocates ISIN codes.
The operation is carried
out by registering the securities in the CSD System and opening
accounts for the issuer and the initial owners. CSD carries out
settlements with the securities registered by CSD. CSD keeps
records of securities, rights and restrictions on the rights granted
by the securities.
 
 
 
 
 
 
Individually, the Bank’s investment in its subsidiary – CSD, is
shown under Investments in subsidiaries and is valued at the
historical
cost
of
the
investment
(capital
contribution)
less
impairment losses.
 
 
At the consolidated level, the investment in the CSD subsidiary is
consolidated, showing the position, performance and cash ﬂows
of the Bank and CSD, with the elimination of related transactions
and balances from the balances and transactions between NBM
and CSD. Consolidated transactions and balances that include
Central Depository related positions on consolidation include
Cash and investments, Government securities issued by the
Government of Moldova,
Other assets,
Fixed assets,
Other
liabilities, Non-controlling interests;
Other income, Operating
expenditure, etc.
Banking,
payment
systems
and
foreign
exchange
supervision
In order to protect the interests of depositors and to ensure the
stability and viability of the banking system, the National Bank of
Moldova conducts prudential supervision of banks in the Republic
of Moldova, monitoring compliance with legal requirements for the
prevention and limitation of risks speciﬁc to banking activity.
According to Law No. 114/2012 on payment services and electronic
money, the NBM licenses, regulates and supervises the activity of
payment service providers and electronic money issuers.
According to Law No. 62/2008 on foreign exchange regulation,
the NBM licenses, regulates and supervises the activity of foreign
exchange entities.
Licensing fees and ﬁnes imposed in the process of supervision of
entities are transferred to the state budget. The National Bank
does not apply regulatory supervisory payments similar to other
European or Moldovan supervisory authorities.
Payments collected from licensing and ﬁnes imposed in the
supervision process are not reﬂected in the ﬁnancial statements
of the NBM, they are revenues of and transferred directly to the
state budget of the Republic of Moldova by the payers.


Chapter 13. Analysis of the situation and ﬁnancial position of the NBM at individual and consolidated level for
2022
177
13.3
Analysis
of
the
ﬁnancial
result
worldwide
The ﬁnancial result of the National Bank of Moldova depends
directly on the necessity to carry out monetary and foreign
exchange operations in order to achieve the fundamental
objective and fulﬁl the basic tasks of the Bank. Considering the
complex situation in 2022, the development of the ﬁnancial result,
during 2016-2022, is reﬂected in chart 13.1.
According to the ﬁnancial statement, as of 31 December 2022, the
Bank incurred a total loss in the amount of MDL 500.53 million,
compared to the situation as of 31 December 2021 when a proﬁt
available for distribution in the amount of MDL 294.27 million was
recorded (Table A.24).
Chart 13.1: Development of the ﬁnancial result during
2016-2022 (MDL million)
Source: NBM
Mainly, the negative ﬁnancial result for 2022 is derived from the
remuneration of monetary policy instruments, as a result of the
gradual increase in the base rate applied to the main monetary
policy operations and the required reserves rules, which led during
2022 to the incurrence of expenditure with the remuneration of
required reserves and sterilization of liquidity, offset by income
from the remuneration of repo operations, in the amount of MDL
2,291.73 million.
The impact of the factors that led to a lower result in the
comparative period was mitigated by the recording of net income
from the management of foreign exchange reserves higher by
MDL 1,252.24 million, due to the increase in the return on foreign
exchange reserves from 0.13% in 2021 to 1.18% for 2022 and the
recording of net income from transactions in foreign currency
higher by MDL 484.17 million.
Therewith, the result of the national currency management activity
in 2022 is positive, being MDL 99.53 million higher than in 2021,
determined both by the decrease in expenditure related to the
production, transportation and storage of national currency by MDL
87.05 million and by the recording of higher commission income
by MDL 14.99 million, due to the increase in the volume of cash
issued to banks.
Impact of monetary policy implementation and activities
regarding
the
relations
with
the
Government
of
the
Republic of Moldova on the ﬁnancial situation of the
National Bank of Moldova.
Given the unprecedented circumstances in 2022, characterized by
the post-pandemic recovery, a global energy crisis, inﬂationary
pressures arising from increased international and regional food
and energy prices, ampliﬁed by the escalation of the armed
conﬂict between Ukraine and the Russian Federation, and the
resulting global and national socio-economic consequences, the
NBM
implemented
a
series
of
restrictive
monetary
policy
measures.
Therefore, during 2022, the NBM gradually increased the base rate
from 6.50% at the beginning of the year to 21.50% since August
2022, and decreased it to 20.0% in December 2022.


178
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
The required reserve ratio of MDL attracted funds gradually
increased from 26.0 % at the beginning of the year to 40.0% as of
September 2022. Strating with December 2022 it decreased to
37.0%.
The reserve requirement ratio for freely convertible
currency (FCC) gradually increased from 30.0% to 45.0% since
September 2022 and maintained at the same level until the end
of the year. Therewith, the average annual remuneration rate of
RRs in MDL increased to 14.63% (2021: 1.30%), which resulted in
higher expenditure related to the remuneration of required
reserves by MDL 2,003.44 million, while the remuneration rate for
required reserves in FCC remained unchanged at 0.01% annually.
At the same time, sterilization expenditure through NBC increased
by MDL 263.40 million,
which was largely driven by the
development of the average interest rate, during the reporting
period constituting 16.04%, compared to the same period of 2021,
when the average rate was 3.29%. The average daily balance of
NBC during 2022 amounted to MDL 2,576.40 million (2021: MDL
4,525.55 million).
The increase in interest expenditure on overnight deposits
attracted on term from resident banks by MDL 61.31 million was
due both to the development of the average interest rate, during
the reporting period constituting 15.06% (2021: 1.57%), and to
the recording of an average balance of deposits by MDL 210.78
million higher than in 2021.
Net expenditures with the accounts and deposits of the Ministry
of Finance in 2022 were MDL 83.88 million higher than in 2021,
mostly due to the recording of interest expenditure on demand
deposits of MDL 77.28 million, the average interest rate on the
balance of the single treasury account being 2.92% (2021: 1.32%).
The average daily balance of the single treasury account during
2022 was MDL 5,381.92 million (2021: MDL 6,072.15 million).
It should be noted that the effect of the increase in these
expenditure was partially offset by the recording of interest
income on repo operations of MDL 258.73 million higher than in
the comparative period.
The average daily balance of repo
transactions during 2022 recorded MDL 1,588.30 million (2021:
MDL 53.20 million) and the weighted average interest rate was
16.39% (2021: 3.72%).
The set of monetary policy instruments applied by the National
Bank of Moldova during 2022 and activities related to relations
with the Government generated a net loss in the amount of MDL
1,704.33 million, compared to a net proﬁt of MDL 325.97 million in
2021 (Table A.25).
Impact of the management of ofﬁcial state reserve assets
on the ﬁnancial situation of the National Bank of Moldova.
The ofﬁcial reserve assets of the State, held by the National Bank
of Moldova, increased as of 31 December 2022 compared to
31 December 2021 by 14.67% or by about USD 572.30 million
(from USD 3,901.88 million to USD 4,474.17 million), being mainly
invested in US dollar, euro and pound sterling instruments.
At the same time, during 2022, the average annual volume of
international reserves increased by about USD 36.22 million (from
USD 3,826.74 million to USD 3,862.96 million).


Chapter 13. Analysis of the situation and ﬁnancial position of the NBM at individual and consolidated level for
2022
179
Net income from the management of ofﬁcial reserve assets
amounted to MDL 1,499.03 million, an increase of MDL 1,252.24
million compared to the previous year, when it amounted to MDL
246.79 million. This result was largely due to the increase in the
return on ofﬁcial reserve assets from 0.13% in 2021 to 1.18% in
2022.
The increase in yields is explained by the increase in interest
rates on ﬁnancial assets related to foreign exchange reserves as a
result of the measures implemented by the monetary authorities
in the context of inﬂationary pressures, combined with the NBM’s
tactical decision to diversify the currency structure of the foreign
exchange investment portfolio to a limited extent, but also from
the NBM’s interventions on the domestic foreign exchange market,
in order to avoid excessive ﬂuctuations in MDL exchange rate in
the context of negative gaps between the demand and supply of
foreign exchange recorded on the interbank market, generated,
in particular, by the escalation of the geopolitical situation in
the region and the maintenance of high import prices for energy
resources.
Thus, the US Federal Reserve’s benchmark rate moved from 0% to
0.25% at the beginning of the year, positioning it in the 4.25-4.50%
range at the end of December 2022, the Bank of England’s rate
moved from 0.25% to 3.50%, and the European Central Bank’s
rate was raised successively over the reporting period, moving it
back into the positive corridor, and at the end of 2022 it was at
2.5%.
Therewith, the increase in the result from the management of
ofﬁcial reserve assets was partly inﬂuenced by the recording
of net proceeds from foreign exchange sales higher than the
comparative period (MDL +484.17 million). The income recorded
was generated by a positive difference between the selling rate
and the cost of the currency stock. During the period under review,
the NBM recorded foreign exchange sales in the amount of USD
835.79 million, of which sales interventions of USD 816.56 million,
which cumulatively resulted in the recording of realized revenues
of MDL 656.43 million, compared to the same period in 2021
when revenues amounted to MDL 171.15 million (in 2021, foreign
exchange sales in the amount of USD 371.8 million were made, of
which sales interventions of USD 360.78 million).
Chart 13.2: Development of the proﬁtability of ofﬁcial
reserve assets in 2022 compared to 2021 (%)
Source: NBM
Additional information on the management of ofﬁcial reserve
assets is provided in Chapter 3 Monetary policy. Ofﬁcial reserve
assets.
The impact of ofﬁcial reserve asset management operations on
the ﬁnancial situation of the National Bank of Moldova is presented
in Table A.26.
In line with international best practices and the provisions of the
Law on the National Bank of Moldova, the main criteria for selecting
reserve assets are the safety of the underlying amounts and
liquidity. Therefore, proﬁtability considerations for the instruments
in which the state’s ofﬁcial reserve assets are invested are only
taken into account after ensuring these two criteria.
The development of the ofﬁcial exchange rate of the Moldovan
Leu against the currencies in which the ofﬁcial reserve assets
are held had a signiﬁcant impact on the ﬁnancial situation of the


180
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
National Bank of Moldova (i.e., in 2022, the USD/MDL exchange
rate depreciated by 8.0%, compared to 3.1% in 2021).
Therewith, at the end of 2022, as a result of the depreciation of
the Moldovan Leu against some component currencies of foreign
exchange stocks, net unrealized income from exchange rate
differences on the reassessment of foreign exchange stocks was
recorded in the amount of MDL 2,686.55 million. At the end of
2021, unrealised income from exchange rate differences on
reassessment of foreign exchange stocks amounted to MDL
778.39 million (Table A.27).

As od 31 December 2022, the consolidated ﬁnancial position of the
National Bank of Moldova increased by MDL 7.13 million due to the
consolidation of the ﬁnancial result of its subsidiary. This increase
was the result of the investment of reserve assets accumulated
by CSD in state securities and partly in cash held at the National
Bank of Moldova.

Bank of Moldova following the consolidation of the subsidiary are

13.6
Capital and reserves
According to Article 19 of Law on the National Bank of Moldova,
the bank’s capital shall include the statutory capital, the reserve
accounts created in accordance with the provisions of Article 66
and the reserve accounts of the unrealized income. The statutory
Chart 13.4: Capital and reserves structure in 2016-
2022 (MDL million)
Source: NBM
capital shall include the authorised capital and the general
reserve fund, shall be dynamic and shall be formed from the proﬁt
of the year available for distribution, from the income earned in
accordance with Article 64 paragraph (3) and/or from the
contributions of the Government of the Republic of Moldova, until
its size reaches 10% of the total monetary liabilities of the
National Bank of Moldova. The General Reserve Fund may be used
exclusively to cover losses made under the outturn for the year at
the end of the ﬁnancial year in the event of a negative result.
According to the proﬁt allocation mechanism, if the size of the
statutory capital of the National Bank of Moldova at the end of
the ﬁnancial year constitutes from 4.00% to 10.00% of the total
monetary liabilities of the National Bank of Moldova, only 50% of
the proﬁt available for distribution shall be allocated to increase the
statutory capital in the manner provided for in Article 19 paragraph
(3).
If at the end of the ﬁnancial year the level of the statutory capital,
after distribution of the ﬁnancial result for the current year, falls
below 4.00% of the total monetary liabilities of the National Bank,
the Government, in the person of the Ministry of Finance, within 60
days after receiving the external auditor’s report on the ﬁnancial
statements of the National Bank, shall transfer to the National
Bank, in a single instalment, a capital contribution in government
securities at a market interest rate, in the amount necessary to
increase the statutory capital up to 4.00% of the total monetary
liabilities of the National Bank, in the manner provided for in Article
19 paragraph (6).
The situation of capital and reserves is shown in table A.33.
As of 31 December 2022, the general reserve fund was used to
cover the total loss recorded by the Bank, decreasing by MDL
500,531 thousand to MDL 1,748,214 thousand. Correspondingly,
the statutory capital also decreased, recording the amount of MDL
2,872,586 thousand.
Over the past few years, the NBM has contributed to continued
economic development, aiming to keep inﬂation within the target


182
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
range of 5.0%.
At the same time, systemic and far-reaching
reforms in the banking sector over the past years have contributed
to a high level of resilience and stability. Thus, banks have not only
weathered the pandemic crisis without signiﬁcant difﬁculties, but
have also been part of the mechanisms supporting the population
and the economy, ready to support economic growth through
lending.
However,
considering the unprecedented situation in 2022
associated with the post-pandemic recovery, the global energy
crisis, inﬂationary pressures generated by the effects of rising
international and regional food and energy prices, ampliﬁed by
the escalation of the armed conﬂict between Ukraine and the
Russian Federation, and the social and economic impact at global
and national level, the NBM adopted a series of restrictive
monetary policy measures.
Against the backdrop of the simultaneous ampliﬁed crises that
unfolded throughout 2022, the National Bank has been diligently
addressing the repercussions of price shocks. It has strived to
strike the balance that would simultaneously meet restrictive
monetary policy conditions and stimulus measures for economic
growth while ensuring ﬁnancial and currency stability.
These
efforts are intended to facilitate a swift economic recovery and a
return to sustainable growth once the crises are surmounted and
external shock risks are minimized.
The correctness and appropriateness of the restrictive nature of
the monetary policy measures adopted is conﬁrmed and validated
by the slowdown in annual inﬂation recorded in the fourth quarter
of 2022, when the NBM decided to resume monetary policy easing
measures, joining the ﬁrst countries in the world to adopt similar
measures. This is due to the fact that the inﬂationary process,
compared to other countries in the region, manifested itself earlier
and the restrictive monetary policy measures had the expected
impact of moderating inﬂation.
Consequently, the monetary policy measures adopted during 2022
had a negative impact on the proﬁtability of the NBM and on the
level of capital, which on 31 December 2022, after covering the
total loss, constituted 3.52% of total monetary liabilities, which is
below the minimum permissible level of 4.00%.
Thus, in 2023 the NBM will not distribute proﬁts to the state budget
revenues. During 2022, 50% of the proﬁt available for distribution
of the NBM for the ﬁnancial year ended on 31 December 2021,
amounting to MDL 147,134 thousand, was transferred to the state
budget.
Under these circumstances, considering the size of the statutory
capital below the minimum legal limit of 4.00% of the monetary
bonds, it is necessary that the Government, in the person of the
Ministry of Finance, within 60 days after receiving the external
auditor’s report on the ﬁnancial statements of the National Bank,
transfer to the National Bank a capital contribution in the amount
of MDL 396,149 thousand in the form of government securities
at a market interest rate, in the amount necessary to increase
the statutory capital to 4.00% of the total monetary bonds of the
National Bank.
In the NBM’s view, the level of capital below the 4.00% limit on
monetary bonds does not affect the Bank’s ability to operate


Chapter 13. Analysis of the situation and ﬁnancial position of the NBM at individual and consolidated level for
2022
183
effectively or to adequately implement its policies or perform its
duties. It is recognized that by virtue of its unique nature, central
banks may operate for a period of time with capital below the
appropriate level established by law. Unlike other entities, where
capital below the legal limits would affect or raise concerns about
business continuity, the level of capital below the 4.00% limit does
not affect the NBM’s ability to continue operations.
However, it is imperative that the capital level is restored to the
minimum level in time by injecting capital from the Government
within the legal deadlines.
It is important to note that the
generation of future proﬁts by the NBM at a level sufﬁcient to
cover the accelerated pace of growth of monetary bonds and the
restoration of the 4.00% government capital level is still quite
uncertain, considering the gap between the return on ofﬁcial
reserve assets available in the market the level of prudence and
liquidity adequate for the management of government reserves,
the interest on government securities in the NBM portfolio
compared to the interest paid on monetary policy instruments and
the Ministry of Finance’s holdings at the NBM. Moreover, the level
of monetary liabilities may increase at a faster pace than forecast
due to inﬂows of ﬁnancial assistance from Moldova’s development
partners, inﬂows of foreign exchange from individuals, direct
equity investments and foreign exchange interventions, which
may make it impossible to regenerate and maintain the capital
level at the 4.00% limit without capital injections from the
Government or additional sources of funding for some NBM
operations. These judgments derive from the NBM’s forecasts of
ﬁnancial performance and statutory capital levels for the years
2023-2024, which are dependent on the level of the benchmark
rates of the countries in which foreign exchange reserves are
invested for those years, the assumptions and risks associated
with the forecasts used in monetary policy decisions, and the
factors driving the increase in monetary liabilities.

200
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
Table A.14: Investment limits and constraints applied by the NBM in the investment risk management process
Risks
Decreasing methods
Credit risk
 Investment in safe, highly rated counterparties as determined by the international rating agencies Standard & Poor’s, Moody’s
and Fitch Ratings (the average rating of the three agencies’ ratings, estimated based on at least two ratings, is applied).
 Establishing a minimum permissible rating of counterparties authorised to conduct transactions.
 Establishing limits on investment instruments, counterparties/issuers, deviation from benchmark and daily monitoring of
compliance.
 Quantifying credit risk, using the default probabilities estimated by the rating agency Standard & Poor’s, for each rating type
and according to the residual maturity of the ﬁnancial instruments.
 Monthly calculation of expected credit losses according to IFRS 9.
Market risk
 The daily monitoring of the market risk is performed through the evaluation at the market price of the unrealized income/losses
from the ﬂuctuation of the exchange rates and from the modiﬁcation of the securities prices.
 The market risk related to the investment securities portfolio is assessed based on risk value methodology (VaR and CvaR),
which estimates the potential ﬁnancial losses, based on set parameters and assumptions regarding adverse developments in
market conditions.
 Monitoring interest rate risk and determining the price sensitivity of securities in investment portfolios to changes in interest
rates is performed by applying the PV01 indicator.
 Setting by the Executive Board of the NBM of the normative foreign exchange composition of the investment portfolio aiming
at hedging the foreign exchange risk and optimizing the portfolio through an adequate correlation of the foreign exchange
composition of assets and liabilities, as well as through an acceptable investment horizon to ensure the continuity of servicing
the current external obligations and implementing the monetary and foreign exchange policies of the state. The normative
foreign exchange structure is determined based on a broad analysis of: the foreign exchange composition of trade in goods
and services, external debt, the currency in which interventions are carried out, etc. The foreign exchange composition of
reserves may deviate within ±10 percentage points from the normative foreign exchange structure.
 The NBM Investment Committee establishes the foreign exchange structure of each sub-portfolio and the permissible
deviations.
 Compliance with the normative foreign exchange composition and the foreign exchange structure of each sub-portfolio within
the permissible deviation limits.
Liquidity risk
 Ensuring liquidity through a substantial share of sight deposits and investments in highly liquid, low-risk securities.
 Setting duration limits per sub-portfolio and maximum maturity of investments depending on the instrument.
 In the case of index-linked investment sub-portfolios, daily monitoring of liquidity risk is also carried out by applying
benchmarks depending on the type of instrument and currency. Benchmarks are market indices, recognised and used
worldwide to benchmark the performance and risks of investment portfolios.
 The NBM’s investment policy allows for active reserve management, with limited deviations of instruments and duration from
the benchmark.
Source: NBM

Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
Table A.21: Development of major projects of the NBM during 2022
Ord.
No.
Project name
Reference
strategic
objective
Project objectives
Started on
Achievements
Next steps
for 2023
1.
Implementing
the solution
to streamline
the licensing,
authorisation
and notiﬁcation
process
3. Strengthening
the banking sector
and implementing
risk-based
supervision
Ensuring a centralised,
efﬁcient and transparent
management of the
NBM’s relationships
with all counterparties
it interacts with in the
licensing, authorisation and
notiﬁcation processes, and
assuring NBM users with a
modern tool to facilitate the
achievement of basic tasks
by increasing the degree of
automation processes.
Quarter III,
2015
During the year, the use within the IT
system on licensing, authorization and
notiﬁcation of the functionality on the
electronic submission of notiﬁcations by
banks on the opening of foreign exchange
ofﬁces and the installation of foreign
exchange machines was initiated.
Therewith, activities were carried out
to promote the amendments to Law
No.
62/2008
on
foreign
exchange
regulation and Law No.
548/1995 on
the National Bank of Moldova, in the
context of the implementation by the
NBM of the solution for streamlining the
licensing process of foreign exchange
ofﬁces and hotels that intend to carry out
currency exchange activity in cash with
individuals on the territory of the Republic
of Moldova, as well as the improvement
of the legal framework related to the
foreign exchange ﬁeld. Subsequently, the
Moldovan Parliament approved Law No
363/2022 to amend Law No. 62/2008 and
Law No. 548/1995.
Further promotion of
amendments to normative
acts, as a result of
the approval of Law
No. 363/2022 on the
amendment of some
normative acts (Law
No 62/2008 on foreign
exchange regulation and
Law No. 548/1995 on the
National Bank of Moldova);
Forwarding in use to third
parties of the module
on licensing/withdrawal
of the licence of foreign
exchange ofﬁces and
foreign exchange bureaux
of hotels.

207
Ord.
No.
Project name
Reference
strategic

Project objectives

Achievements

for 2023
2.
Implementing
the solution
to streamline
the process
of monitoring
shareholder
transparency and
remote analysis
in the area of
preventing and
combating money
laundering and
terrorist ﬁnancing
3. Strengthening
the banking sector
and implementing
risk-based
supervision
Streamlining the monitoring
process, strengthening the
National Bank’s capacity
in the area of increasing
transparency in the
ownership structure of
licensed banks through
continuous monitoring,
as well as ensuring an
automated monitoring
process of shareholder
transparency. Also, the
prompt identiﬁcation and
assessment by the National
Bank of the risks of money
laundering and terrorist
ﬁnancing in its supervision
of banks.
Quarter III,
2014
Hardware infrastructure was procured and
installed to install the test and production
environments
for
the
IT
solution
to
streamline the process of shareholder
transparency
monitoring
and
remote
analysis in the area of preventing and
combating money laundering and terrorist
ﬁnancing.
The construction phase of the IT solution
was carried out and accepted, and the
historical data required for testing was
prepared and uploaded. Training of NBM
staff on the use of the IT solution was also
conducted.
End-user testing of the IT solution has
been initiated.
The
installation
documentation
for
the production environment has been
approved and the installation process has
started.
Acceptance of deliverables
related to the training and
testing phase.
Installation of the IT
solution on the production
environment.
Experimental exploitation
on the production
environment. Final
acceptance of the software
solution and project
respectively.
3.
Implementing
the banking,
corporate resource
management
(ERP) and cash
management
software solution
(CBS)
5. Strengthening
institutional
capacity and
efﬁciency
Transforming the NBM’s
business processes related
to banking operations,
management of ﬁnancial,
material and human
resources, as well as
modernization of the NBM’s
banking IT system, in order
to streamline the NBM’s
operational activity.
October
2013
Considering the time factor, which is
critical for the successful conduct of
the tendering procedure, related to the
issues of limiting the liability of economic
operators in contracts concluded in public
procurement procedures, during the year,
actions were planned and implemented
to address this dependency.
In this regard, the NBM has consulted
the Ministry of Finance on the provisions
of the national regulatory framework,
including proposals for the adjustment of
Law No. 131/2015 on public procurement,
with reference to this aspect. Following
the consultation, adjustments were made
to the domestic regulatory framework
on the limitation of liability of economic
operators in procurement contracts.
Approval of the draft
amendment to the internal
regulatory act, following the
inclusion of regulations on
the limitation of liability of
economic operators;
Launch a market
consultation procedure on
the relevant aspects related
to the subject matter of
the tender to be launched
(implementation of CBS,
ERP and cash management
IT solution);
Approval of changes to the
project concept;
Initiation and conduct of
tender procedure.

208
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
Ord.
No.
Project name
Reference
strategic
objective
Project objectives
Started on
Achievements
Next steps
for 2023
4.
Migration to new
SWIFT standards -
ISO 20022
Ensuring successful
transition to ISO 20022
and migration to new
platforms and services
working with MX messages,
including ensuring business
continuity during migration
to new standards.
Quarter I,
2022
In line with the new standards and SWIFT
strategy, the NBM has initiated the project
to migrate the SWIFT message format
to the ISO 20022 standard, which aims
to ensure a successful transition to the
new standards with its own resources
within the set deadlines, as the migration
process is quite complex.
Thus,
during the year,
actions were
carried
out
to
formalise
the
project,
strengthen
the
project
team,
and
subsequently
the
action
plan
for
migration to the new standards was
approved.
During the year, requirements were also
developed for the creation of functionality
for exporting, processing and tabular
presentation of SWIFT MX messages.
The Alliance Web platform was updated.
Migration of authorisations to FINPlus RMA
was completed.
SWIFT Alliance interface
settings ﬁnalisation
activities;
Updating and completing
the requirements for
the visual XML message
rendering tool, testing and
releasing it for use;
Testing the receipt and
sending of XML format
messages;
Development of the
speciﬁcations (per module)
that will form the basis for
automating the import of
incoming SWIFT messages
in XML format.
5.
Implementing an
instant payment
scheme
4. Increasing the
quality of the
national currency
and the level of
use of cashless
payments
Implementing the instant
payment solution (platform)
(24/7/365), which will
create the premises
for the development
of innovative/mobile
payments, with their
immediate ﬁnal settlement
and the possibility of
immediate reuse of funds
received by beneﬁciaries,
facilitating the circuit of
economic ﬂows.
Quarter I,
2020
In the third quarter of 2022 the contract
with the IT solution provider was signed.
The project team was strengthened and
mobilised
to
launch
the
project
and
start the planning and initiation phase.
The project initiation documentation was
prepared and approved.
Following the
initiation phase,
actions were carried
out
during
the
analysis
and
design
phase.
The technical and functional
documentation was prepared, along with
the documentation for conﬁguring and
setting up the future solution.
NBM approval of the
detailed technical and
functional speciﬁcations
document, and the solution
conﬁguration and setting
document;
Construction of the
solution according to the
requirements stipulated in
the detailed technical and
functional speciﬁcations
document;
End-user training;
Technical and functional
testing of the solution by
NBM end-users.
continued on next page

and

related
to
the
Automated
Interbank

project.

In parallel with these activities, actions
were carried out on the adjustment and
consolidation of the internal regulatory
framework for electronic archiving of
documents.

of documents in the

throughout the approval
workﬂow;

to the internal regulatory

archive.

8.
Twinning project:
Strengthening
supervision,
corporate
governance and
risk management
in the ﬁnancial
sector
Providing the necessary
support to the Republic
of Moldova in the
implementation of reforms
related to the Republic of
Moldova (RM) – European
Union (EU) Association
Agreement and the RM-
EU Association Agenda on
strengthening supervision,
corporate governance and
risk management in the
ﬁnancial sector.
Quarter IV,
2021
Within this project, the National Bank of
Moldova and the National Commission
for Financial Markets beneﬁt from the
assistance of partner institutions from the
European Union (EU), brought together
in a consortium formed by the National
Bank of Romania, project leader, the
Central Bank of the Netherlands and the
Central Bank of Lithuania, in collaboration
with the Romanian Financial Supervisory
Authority and the Romanian National
Authority for Consumer Protection.
Following the signing of the Twinning
contract by the parties, the ofﬁcial project
launch event was organised in the ﬁrst
quarter of 2022.
During 2022 activities were carried out
according
to
the
project
plan,
as
a
result of which the project stakeholders
beneﬁted from the organisation by the
external partners involved of missions
of
foreign
experts
on
all
5
project
components.
Within this project, the
conference "Accession of the Republic
of Moldova to SEPA – achievements and
prospects" was organized.
Conduct foreign expert
missions as planned;
Organisation of Project
Monitoring Committee
meetings.

over
the
duties of the National Comission for
Financial Markets (NCFM) in the ﬁeld of
regulation/supervision of the non-banking
ﬁnancial
sector,
starting
with
1
July
2023.
In this context, the NBM and
the NCFM have initiated the process of
working together to initiate the project. A
Memorandum of Understanding outlining
task transfer was drafted and signed in
the second quarter of 2022. During the
year, representatives from both parties
held working meetings to address speciﬁc
aspects of tasks transfer to the NBM.
Within this scope, maps detailing the
current operational processes on the
NCFM related to the non-banking ﬁnancial
sector (AS IS) were developed. Also, in
the second part of the year, the Project
Concept was developed by the Project
Working Group and approved by the
NBM Executive Board. Subsequently, the
project team expanded, and a Detailed
Action Plan for project implementation
was developed. During the same period,
cooperation with the Romanian Financial
Supervisory Authority (FSA) commenced,
including a working visit by NBM staff to
the FSA for insights into best practices in
insurance market supervision.
Implementing project
actions according to the
approved plan;
Completing the project,
with the NBM taking
over the regulatory/new
supervision of the non-
banking ﬁnancial sector
from 1 July 2023.
continued on next page


212
Annual Report (NBM, 2022)
Ord.
No.
Project name
Reference
strategic
objective
Project objectives
Started on
Achievements
Next steps
for 2023
10.
Ensuring the
necessary
conditions for the
banking sector
to join the Single
Euro Payments
Area (SEPA)
4. Increasing the
quality of the
national currency
and the level of
use of cashless
payments
The performance of all
necessary actions by
the NBM as the national
coordinator of the Single
Euro Payments Area
(SEPA) accession process,
in cooperation with
the relevant national
institutions and market
actors, in order to ensure
the prerequisites and the
highest level of success in
the process of accession of
Moldovan payment service
providers to the Single Euro
Payments Area (SEPA).
Quarter III,
2022
In line with the institutional strategic
objectives, in 2022, the NBM initiated
the implementation of the project on
ensuring the necessary prerequisites for
the banking sector to join the SEPA
area.
Thus,
in
the
third
quarter
of
2022,
actions
were
carried
out
on
the
formalisation
of
the
project
and the Executive Board approved the
Project Concept.
Subsequently,
the
project implementation team was set
up.
A
working
meeting
was
also
organised with all the authorities of the
Republic of Moldova that are concerned
by the process of meeting the SEPA
criteria, where the accession criteria were
discussed in detail, as well as the process
and the steps that need to be taken in
order to comply with the SEPA criteria.
The conference "Accession of the Republic
of
Moldova
to
SEPA
–
achievements
and prospects" was also organized and
addressed the relevant topics for the
implementation of the project.
Approval of the Action Plan;
Approval of the draft
Regulation on credit
transfers and direct
debits issued by the NBM,
which is subject to the
SEPA accession criteria,
following the examination
of comments from the
transparency process;
Collecting information from
authorities for the SEPA
accession ﬁle;
Drawing up the legal
opinion and its annexes
(which will contain
information demonstrating
compliance with the SEPA
Standards);
Assessment of options
for connecting to SEPA
infrastructures;
Supporting payment service
providers’ understanding of
the technical requirements
for joining SEPA;
Completion and submission
of the SEPA accession ﬁle.
Source: NBM
